The rest matched once the reader followed `Omit` and `Pick` (Link's navigation props), arrays, and quoted keys. `observability.md` is an explicit exemption (plan 15), and `Link props`' `target` and `rel` are named as external, from Solid's anchor attributes.
113.5 [x] Test: dx-options `option tables` over every dx file (bite: `swr` misspelled in cache.md's `cdn` table reports `cache.md "cdn": swrr`); tags `favicon keys` (red: the `@ts-expect-error` was unused while any key type-checked).

114 [x] B114 Bun cut an oversized POST's connection before Flare's 413
114.1 [x] CI 38591370441, e2e-mac 2/2: bun-prod-webkit docs-server `body limit` failed with `read ECONNRESET`. From 335c23d6 (B110): `createBunFetch` set Bun's `maxRequestBodySize` to the server's `bodyLimit`, and Bun answers a body over its cap by closing the socket, so a client still writing gets `EPIPE` or `ECONNRESET` and never reads a status. `Expect: 100-continue` does not help: Bun sends `100 Continue` for a `Content-Length` over its cap. Linux passed because its loopback buffers absorbed the 70 KB body. flare-77's bare Bun.serve, 64 KiB limit, Node client: 413 100/100 at 70 KiB; `EPIPE` 100/100 at 8 MiB and 40 MiB, plain and chunked.
114.2 [x] Fix (flare-77's ruling): Bun's `maxRequestBodySize` is a ceiling, never the enforcement. `createBunFetch` returns Bun's own default, 128 MiB, a constant above the 32 MiB `bodyLimit` maximum, so Flare's capped reader (`server-fn/input.ts`: the `Content-Length` precheck, then the capped read) answers every over-limit body with 413 first, as on Node. Above 128 MiB the platform cuts the connection. B110's part stays: createServer checks `bodyLimit` and carries it on the server object.
114.3 [x] Test: bun serve `bun oversized post`, under `test:bun` only, a real Bun.serve with a 64 KiB `bodyLimit` and an 8 MiB server-function POST over a raw kept-alive socket, sized and chunked: 413 both, 3 of 3; on the old adapter `EPIPE` both, 3 of 3. Bun's own `node:http` client read Bun's early 413 either way, so it could not witness the cut. `ceiling` and `server limit` replace `body limit` and `default`. The e2e `body limit` sends 8 MiB: node, bun, and workers prod and dev chromium ×5, 30 of 30; on the old adapter bun-prod-chromium failed 5 of 5 with `write EPIPE`. Node and Workers answered 413 at 8 MiB, so neither has this bug.

115 [x] B115 The release workflow ran v0's Bun and the runner's Node
115.1 [x] Found by flare-77: `.github/workflows/release.yml` pinned `BUN_VERSION` 1.3.11 after 6ef6863c moved ci.yml and `packageManager` to 1.4.3, and its verify job set no Node, so it ran the runner's own, older than the 22.18 the repo needs. The workflow last ran 2026-10-06, in the v0 era, so it had never verified the v1 tree. Under 1.3.11 the size gate measures 1137 against the 1100 budget (BUGS 35), so a v1.0.0 tag would have failed its own verify step. ci.test checked ci.yml's pin only.
115.2 [x] Fix: release.yml pins 1.4.3, and its verify and npmjs jobs set Node 24 right after checkout, the npmjs job before setup-bun. ci.test reads every workflow in `.github/workflows`.
115.3 [x] Test: ci `ci bun version` over every workflow (red: `bun@1.3.11`), with every setup-bun step reading `BUN_VERSION`; `release node` (red: setup-bun came first, and verify had no Node). flare-77 dispatches release.yml on main once this lands: a dispatch verifies only, since `release.ts --publish` refuses off a tag.

116 [x] B116 An exiting test run left a Vite cache dir behind
116.1 [x] CI 38597128404's lint job: boot.test `exit cleans` failed 1 of 2080, `existsSync(session.cacheDir)` still true right after `process.emit("exit", 0)`. boot.ts's exit path sent the dev child SIGTERM, which is asynchronous, then removed the cache dir at once, so a child still alive under the suite's load wrote into it again (the dep optimizer's metadata and `deps_temp_*`). A real runner that exits early leaves the dir in the same way. Isolated, the case passed 25 of 25 (flare-77).
116.2 [x] Fix: the exit path sends SIGKILL, which leaves the child no handler to run, removes the dirs, waits 50 ms for a write already in flight, and removes them again. Each removal retries when a racing write interrupts it (`maxRetries`). `exited()` treats a child a signal ended as exited, which has a signal code and no exit code, so a `close()` after the exit path no longer waits forever.
116.3 [x] Test: boot `exit cleans a writer`, a stub child that ignores SIGTERM, as Vite's graceful shutdown keeps working after it, and rewrites its cache dir every millisecond: the dir is gone right after the exit event and 100 ms later. Red on the old exit path (SIGTERM, one removal) with `expected true to be false` in 169 ms; flare-77 found the first stub, which died on SIGTERM, did not bite, and the red first reported was the `close()` hang. `close after a kill`: a child the exit path killed, its exit event already fired, still closes within 2 s; red on the old `exited()` with `hung`. Each fix has its own red; both pass 3 of 3.

117 [x] B117 Found writing plan 14 9.6's adapter cases: two runtime behaviors that differ from their decisions
117.1 [x] B37 says a root layout whose SSR output has no `<html>` and no `</body>` is a 500 in production. It is not: through the real `createServer` with production stubbed, such a root answers 200 with a document that has neither, and B46's write rule then skips the store silently (`ssr` stays `miss`). No code implements B37 (the `B37` comments in `features/i18n` are BUGS.md B37, an unrelated shim note).
117.2 [x] On Workers, `createWorkersHandler` collapses a path's empty segments before `server.fetch`, so `/a//static.txt` is served as 200 at that URL, where Node and Bun answer B22's 301 to `/a/static.txt` (bits/02 `no double slash`). Measured in Miniflare with the real server: `//stored` 400, `/stored/` 200, `/STORED` 301 to `/stored`, `/a//static.txt` 200. dx/adapters.md says only that the path is canonical before the binding sees it.
117.3 [x] flare-77's rulings: wire B37 as written, and the Workers handler stops canonicalizing, so B22's redirect comes from the server's ingress on every runtime.
117.4 [x] Fix (B37): `renderDocument` takes the root layout's id in production and rejects a shell that `isDocument` (`server/render/shape.ts`, also B46's write rule) refuses, before any byte is sent. `drawn` answers that rejection with the empty 500 of a throw before the shell, under the route's security set, and logs `root layout "<id>" rendered no <html> or </body> (B37)` once per root per server: a stored miss renders twice, shared then live, and a broken root fails every request. A root whose own loader failed never renders, so its bare failure is not checked. Dev passes no id (D22). The unused `shapeDocument` read `NODE_ENV` and is gone.
117.5 [x] Test: shape `prod document-less` through the real `createServer` with production stubbed (red: 200 with the `<div>`), two requests and one log line (bite: logging per render gives two lines on the first request); `dev document-less`; `document`. create `unknown failure` bites on checking a root that never rendered.
117.6 [x] Fix (B22): `createWorkersHandler` hands `server.fetch` the request as it came, and forwards a 404 GET or HEAD to the binding unchanged: it passed the server's ingress, so its path is already canonical. A non-canonical path is the server's 301 or 400 on Workers as on Node and Bun. A file is the platform's on every runtime: the e2e harness's static layer for Node and Bun and Cloudflare's assets run before Flare, so `/icons//icon-96.png` is 200 on node-prod and bun-prod and Cloudflare's 307 on workers-prod. The e2e case uses route paths.
117.7 [x] Test: docs-server `canonical ingress` on node, bun, and workers prod (red: workers-prod answered `/docs//routing` 200; node-prod and bun-prod passed); handler `assets escape` through the real `createServer` (bite: the old handler gives `/a//b` 200 from the binding).
117.8 [x] Dev warns (flare-77, 2026-10-15, the defer.md finding): dev still serves a document-less shell as written (D22), and each server now warns once per root, `[flare:render] root layout "<id>" rendered no <html> or </body>; production answers 500 (B37)`, so the 500 is no surprise at the first deploy. `renderDocument` takes the root's id in dev too, with `onDocumentless`, which serves the shell and reports the root instead of rejecting. Test: shape `dev document-less`, two requests, the `<div>` served both times and one warning (red before: none).

118 [x] B118 Workers dev loses a `//` request-target's path before Flare (upstream)
118.1 [x] Found by flare-77 on 671d1a25: docs-server `canonical ingress` failed on workers-dev-chromium and workers-dev-firefox, `//x` 200 where B22 says 400. A probe at `createWorkersHandler`'s entry showed the worker receives `http://x/` for `//x`; `/docs//routing` arrives intact. The cause is @cloudflare/vite-plugin 1.44.0's dev middleware: `createRequestHandler` calls `createRequest` (`dist/index.mjs:1537`, vendored from `@remix-run/node-fetch-server@0.8.0`), which builds `new URL(req.url, `${protocol}//${host}`)` (line 1546). A target that starts with `//` is a scheme-relative reference, so its first segment becomes the host: `//x` is `http://x/`, and `//evil.example/a` is `http://evil.example/a`. node-fetch-server 1.0.0 builds `${protocol}//${host}${req.url}` (`request-listener.ts:429`) and keeps the path, but @cloudflare/vite-plugin 1.64.0, the latest, builds its request in its own `createRequestForIncomingMessage` with the same `new URL(req.url ?? "/", `${protocol}//${host}`)`. Flare's dev glue never sees the raw target.
118.2 [x] Decision (flare-77, revising a first ruling that 3c5383ee encoded as a workers-dev expectation of 200): the plugin is upstream and is not patched, but Flare composes this dev server, so it enforces B22 at its own dev edge (118.4) and `canonical ingress` expects 400 on all 18 projects. The upstream issue draft is for the user to file.
118.3 [ ] Open until @cloudflare/vite-plugin ships a node-fetch-server that keeps the path.
118.4 [x] Fix: `vite/dev/guard.ts` `targetGuard` runs the Node listener's own request-target check (`toRequest`, `adapters/node/request.ts`) on the raw `req.url`, and answers a refused target with that listener's bare 400; every other target passes untouched. flare() registers it in its pre `configureServer`, before the Cloudflare plugin's handler, only when that plugin is composed. Test: guard `target guard` on a real `node:http` server; docs-server `canonical ingress` on all 18 projects (red without the guard: workers-dev-chromium 200), the full docs-server spec on workers-dev chromium and firefox (38 passed), and the docs and minimal specs on workers-dev in three engines (395 passed). A path check (`shared/url/canonical.ts`) is not the classifier here: it reads a parsed path, so it would refuse a query's `%2f` and miss absolute-form.
118.5 [x] Flare's behavior is fixed (118.4). 118.3 stays open only as the upstream report the user files.

119 [x] B119 The user's manual testing of the production docs (2026-10-15): flashing, shifts, scroll jumps, and a WebKitGTK crash
119.1 [x] /docs/server-functions: clicking Yes or No makes both buttons flash. Measured (Chromium, frame by frame): both buttons drop to opacity 0.5 and disabled for one frame, then return, with view transitions on or off; the same nodes throughout (no remount) and no skeleton. WebKit and Firefox finish the vote between two frames. Cause: the docs app's `Tally` gave both buttons `disabled={mutation.isPending}` and `disabled:opacity-50`, so every vote dimmed both. Not Flare and not the transition. Fix: a pending vote ignores further clicks (`aria-disabled` while pending) and changes neither button's look. Test: docs-data `vote holds still` holds the vote request and checks both buttons while it is pending, then that a second click sent nothing (red: `["0.5", "0.5"]` against `["1", "1"]` on node prod chromium, firefox, and webkit); green with `mutation sets data`, 16 of 16.
119.2 [x] Medium viewport, /docs/server-functions scrolled to the bottom, then a sidebar click to /docs/defer: the content shifts. Measured at 1024×700 and 1280×800: the DOM steps from scrollY 1047 to 0 in one frame in every engine, with transitions on or off, so no DOM shift. Chromium's frames show it: with the element-scoped transition (`scope: "auto"`, the docs app's boundary around the article), the old article's image travels from its scrolled-off box (top −917) to its new one (131), so the old page's top section, never on screen before the click, slides in over the new page; with no transition the cut is clean, and with a document transition the old bottom view crossfades in place. Fix (B119 (5)): the default scope is the document; `"auto"` is an opt-in with a documented caveat, and the docs app drops its boundary. Test: unit `direction.test.ts` `scope` and navigate `view transition` (red: `main` where the document was expected); e2e docs-navigation `view transition document` (red: `["article" ×4]` on node prod and node dev chromium); frames on the new default match the document ones.

So Flare's runtime is not required, the white is the old snapshot itself (hiding it removes every flash, painting what is behind it does not), and it is Chrome's under this compositor and GPU: headless Chrome and Chrome in a headless wlroots compositor (cage) on the same GPU never showed it, and the user reproduced it by eye on an unrelated production site built with another framework (https://prodaj.hr, Next.js view transitions). No extensions were involved: branded Chrome ignores `--load-extension` (since 137). Filed upstream as Chromium https://issues.chromium.org/issues/573693508. Recording the virtual output once crashed Hyprland (SIGBUS in readPixels from CScreenshareFrame::copyShm); no run records that output again. Fix, the user's choice (decision B125): ViewTransitionCSS adds `::view-transition-old(root){display:none}` (root only; the opt-back is the app's own `::view-transition-old(root){display:block}`) and its default duration becomes 150 ms. Green after: 4 of 4 unit, 4 of 4 e2e. Whether the flash is gone on the user's own display is theirs to see: no harness here reproduces it without recording a Hyprland output. Since the user's call of 2026-10-15 Flare's default no longer mitigates it: the document transition crossfades again, and an affected app adds `::view-transition-old(root){display:none}` itself (B125, dx/navigate.md).
119.16 [x] The user's report (2026-10-15): during a navigation's view transition, hover and the pointer cursor stop working on what does not change (the sidebar), then come back. Since B119 (5) the transition is document-scoped, and the engines route the pointer away from the live page while it runs. Measured (headless Playwright, a document transition held running, the pointer on the /docs/defer sidebar link: without the rule all three engines hit-test the document element, the link gets no hover, and a click is lost; with `::view-transition{pointer-events:none}` Firefox hits the link, hovers it, and the click navigates, while Chromium and WebKit still hit the document element and lose the click. Setting `pointer-events:none` on every transition pseudo-element too, leaving the root uncaptured and naming only the content, or (Chromium) an element-scoped start on the content, changes nothing in Chromium or WebKit: they hit-test the document element while any transition runs). Red: docs-navigation `pointer during transition` read `{ hit: "HTML", hovered: false }` on node-prod chromium, firefox, and webkit and node-dev chromium, 4 of 4, and in each engine a click on the link during the held transition was lost (Caching stood). Fix, as far as Flare can (decision B126): ViewTransitionCSS adds `::view-transition{pointer-events:none}`; Firefox then hits, hovers, and navigates (5 of 5), and goes red again without the rule (2 of 2). Chromium and WebKit keep the engine limit, asserted per engine so a browser fix shows; B125's 150 ms default keeps the dead window short. A Chromium report is drafted for the user to file (.tmp/grunt/p14/b119/upstream/chromium-hover.md). Fixed for a page with a boundary by B129 (plan 18): the finding above that naming only the content changes nothing came from a probe that ran before B126's rule existed and named a `main` the guide pages lack. The default `"content"` scope leaves the root uncaptured, and the docs guide's article is a `ViewTransitionBoundary`. docs-transition `chrome stays live` and `chrome stays live scrolled` replace docs-navigation's `pointer during transition`: red before B129 8 of 8 (the root captured), green 8 of 8 on node-prod chromium, firefox, webkit and node-dev chromium (the link hit, hovered, the click landing; header and sidebar equal to the settled frame; no moving group), red again with the boundary removed (8 of 8) and with the still-group rule removed (8 of 8). verify:safari's `pointer during transition` checks the same in Safari.

Both passed 20 of 20 rerun alone, so they count as unattributed under concurrent load, not as B126 bugs; heavy runs are serialized from now on (a shared flock on this machine). The second failure exposed a real harness bug: five e2e afterAll hooks closed a boot their beforeAll never made, adding a TypeError to the real failure. grid.test `boot close guarded` was red listing the five (minimal session and base, navigation, server-fn, static); each now closes with `?.close()`, as golden.test does.

120 [x] B120 A node in `<head>` that Flare did not render kills hydration, and every client navigation, silently
120.1 [x] Found probing 119.4 in release Firefox through a proxy that put its script first in `<head>`: the header's API link changed the URL and the title, and the content stayed Routing; no error, no failed request. Measured with the docs prod server in chromium, firefox, and webkit: a meta, a style, a script, a comment, or a whitespace text node first in `<head>`, or a meta between two of Solid's markers, breaks it; a node at the end of `<head>`, first in `<body>`, first in `#app`, or at the end of `<body>` does not. Mechanism: the compiled root layout walks `<head>` by position (`_el$3 = head.firstChild; getNextMarker(_el$3.nextSibling)`), and Solid's hydrate prelude moves only leading `data-dh` elements (the charset) out of the way. A foreign first node stops that move, the marker search starts on the charset, every pair after it balances, `getNextMarker` returns `null`, and `_el$4.nextSibling` throws inside the root layout's render. The root boundary paints its error template detached (dev: `Hydration key miss` and `84 unclaimed server-rendered node(s)`; production: nothing), so the server's markup stays on screen, inert. The title still updates: `Heads` is Matches's sibling, outside the root render.
120.2 [x] Fix (decision B120): the server records the final shell's `<head>` sequence in the state (`head-sequence.ts`, 632 to 651 bytes on the docs pages); before the hydrate, the client aligns the live `<head>` with it and detaches every other node, and puts each back after its anchor right after the hydrate call, in the same task (`head-guard.ts`, `create-client.ts`). Tests: docs-smoke `foreign head node` (red 24 of 24) and `foreign head extension` (red with the guard off 9 of 9; with the restore deferred a frame, `same task` 3 of 3); units `head-sequence.test.ts`, `head-guard.test.ts`, state `head sequence`, create-client `head guarded`.
120.3 [x] A route render that throws while the document hydrates was silent in production. It is logged: `console.error("[flare] a route failed to hydrate", error)`, in every mode (create-client `hydrate failure logged`).
120.4 [ ] Upstream: report the positional `<head>` walk to solidjs/dom-expressions (draft in `.tmp/grunt/p14/b120/upstream.md` for the user to file). The guard stays until Solid's hydrate tolerates foreign `<head>` nodes.
120.5 [x] Flare's behavior is fixed (120.2, 120.3). 120.4 stays open only as the upstream report the user files.

121 [x] B121 A navigation a newer one superseded loaded its own target as a document when its route chunk failed
121.1 [x] flare-77's grid at c64cc50e: node-dev-webkit docs-smoke `rapid links`, `Execution context was destroyed`. Navigation log: the changelog commit at 999 ms, then a document load of `/api/server/create-server` at 2907 ms, the first of four rapid clicks, which the three after it had superseded. No trace, and 90 unforced runs at c64cc50e on node-dev-webkit (×30 at 4 workers, ×60 at 8) passed, so the failing request is not on record; the stats gate is not involved (the API trip loads no changelog code).
121.2 [x] Cause: `arrive` (client/navigation/request.ts) loaded the trip's target as a document on a chunk error without checking `trip.signal`, which the next navigation aborts (navigate.ts `latest?.abort()`); `request` did the same on a build mismatch landing after the abort. Shown with the API page's route module aborted during the rapid clicks, 3 of 3: the API trip's chunk failed, `/api/server/create-server` loaded as a document though Examples had been clicked, that load cancelled Examples' imports, `/examples` loaded too, and the page never reached its last click's target.
121.3 [x] Fix (commit scope bit-06; `request.ts` is P10, so bit-10 was right, left unamended by the standing rule): neither reloads when `trip.signal` is aborted; the newer navigation decides. Tests: navigate `superseded chunk` and `superseded mismatch` (red 2 of 2); docs-resilience `superseded chunk failure` (red 15 of 15 on five projects). Audit of every step a trip takes after an await (navigate.ts `go`): the commit is guarded by `latest !== controller` after the arrival and by `settled.signal.aborted` inside the transition's update; the history write and the scroll run only after `shown`, which the store resolves false when a newer commit replaced this one (B106); heads apply with the commit; the hole fill checks `sameUrl` with the page on screen (`holeWatch`); a redirect follows only after the `latest` guard; the two reloads were the only steps before that guard.
121.4 [x] So the next trigger is named: every e2e case's navigation log (`crash.ts`, attached when a case fails) also records each failed request with its failure text and each response at 400 or above, timed.

122 [x] B122 dx/server-fn.md and the README promised a handler context field `revalidate` that never existed
122.1 [x] Found writing plan 14 9.6's server-fn.md cases: the dx handler-context table listed `revalidate` ("handler only, `({ keys?, tags? }) => void`, the work is passed to `waitUntil`"), and both the dx sample and the README's Server functions sample destructured it. `ServerFnContext` is `{ input, auth, env, request, serverContext }`, and dispatch builds exactly that, so a handler destructuring `revalidate` failed to type-check and got `undefined`. The types case only asserted the stream context lacked it.
122.2 [x] Fix (flare-77's ruling, the doc and not the code): one API revalidates, `revalidate()` from `@lovrozagar/flare/server` (store.md), which hands its promise to the request's `waitUntil` from any handler or loader. dx/server-fn.md drops the row and the destructured field and points there; the README sample is now a compiled `quick-start` block (`src/fns/save.ts`, checked by exports.test `readme quick start` against the packed package) that calls `revalidate()`; `tests/types/server-fn.test.ts` `server fn stream` asserts the handler context has no `revalidate`.

123 [x] B123 Client navigation and streamed server functions did nothing in Safari 17.6
123.1 [x] Found by flare-77 on the user's Mac with safaridriver: a sidebar click reached Flare's link handler, the `_flare=data` request completed in 11 ms, and then nothing: no commit, no history write, pending back to false, no error. The playground's stream button did nothing either. Safari 17.6 has no async iteration of `ReadableStream` (`Symbol.asyncIterator`, `values`, and `ReadableStream.from` are all undefined), and the client iterated bodies with `for await` (client/navigation/frames.ts `framesOf`, client/server-fn/stream.ts `lines`). The TypeError was swallowed by the navigation's settled arrival. The grid missed it: Playwright's Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit all have it.
123.2 [x] Fix: `chunksOf` (shared/protocol/chunks.ts) reads a body with a reader, cancels it on an early exit, and releases the lock; both sites use it, and no other client code iterates a stream. Red: docs-smoke `no stream iteration` deletes the iterator and `values` before the page runs; it was stuck on Routing in node-prod chromium, firefox, and webkit and node-dev chromium, 4 of 4, and passes after.
123.3 [x] The silent failure: a body that fails to read or land, other than by the trip's abort, is now logged with `console.error` and the target loads as a document under the reload guard; a rejected fetch stays a network failure that commits nothing. Tests: request `unreadable body`, `aborted body`; docs-resilience `unreadable response` (red with the fallback removed, 2 of 2).
123.4 [x] The support floor (decision B123): the e2e `node-prod-floor` project (tests/e2e/floor.ts) deletes every API newer than Chrome 111, Firefox 114, and Safari 16.4 the client could touch, and tests/structure/browser-floor.test.ts checks every client-reachable source's browser APIs against @mdn/browser-compat-data 8.1.5 at that floor. The scan found three more floor bugs, each fixed: `Promise.withResolvers` (Chrome 119, Firefox 121, Safari 17.4) in the router store's commit and frames.ts's deferred slots, now `resolvers()` (shared/promise/resolvers.ts); `toSorted` (Firefox 115) in route ranking and the not-found owner, now `sort` on the fresh array; and `URL.canParse` (Chrome 120, Safari 17) in prefetch, now the constructor's throw. Red: the floor project failed 70 of 78 before the fixes, every page stuck before hydrate on `toSorted`; each fix reverted alone fails 2 of 3 of its cases; the structure test was red at 8ae9ec28 with 10 findings, and on the tree before these fixes with 5. Bits/16 has the cases.
123.5 [x] Correction: 123.1 said Safari before 18 lacks stream iteration. @mdn/browser-compat-data 8.1.5 lists it first in Safari 27 (Chrome 124, Firefox 110), so the comments and docs now say Safari before 27; 17.6 is the version verified on the Mac.
123.6 [x] Closed by flare-77 at 7b667393: `bun run verify:safari` passed 5 of 5 flows in real Safari 17.6 on the Mac (sidebar from the bottom, Back to 900, /hr Back to the click position, the playground stream, the stream gate). With `chunksOf` put back to iterating the stream, the same run fails 0 of 5, exit 1. All seven local gates passed in a fresh worktree (unit 2198, grid 1242 with the floor), and CI run 38718415771 passed every job.

124 [x] `modules: "all"` does nothing in Safari
124.1 [x] Found by flare-3c (2026-10-15): `modules: "all"` does nothing in Safari: it adds `<link rel="prefetch">`, which Safari ships disabled by default through 26.x (caniuse link-rel-prefetch; WebKit 194539), so Safari users get no background module preload. `"all"` is the router's default (create-router.ts `prefetchOf`), and its `all()` (client/navigation/prefetch.ts) is the only code that inserts the tag; the hover and viewport triggers import the route chain directly, so they are unaffected. The fix is planned with the cross-app prefetch feature, not yet.
124.2 [x] Red, measured in real Safari 17.6 on the Mac through verify:safari's lock and tunnel (.tmp/grunt/p14/b124/safari-prefetch.ts `bare`): `relList.supports("prefetch")` is false, and a static `<link rel="prefetch" as="script" crossorigin>`, the same tag inserted after load as Flare's `all()` inserts it, and a plain inserted `rel="prefetch"` made no request and fired neither load nor error in 4 s. The controls on the same page, an inserted `rel="modulepreload"` and a `fetch`, were each requested within 70 ms and loaded (`modulepreload` is supported). The server logged only the page, the favicon, and the two controls.
124.3 [x] flare-3c's probe in real Safari 17.6: a `<link rel="modulepreload">` is reused across a navigation for the same origin and for a same-site subdomain, `rel="prefetch"` is ignored, and a `fetch()` warm-up is not reused (the module downloads twice). So Safari's fix for `modules: "all"` is `modulepreload`, not `fetch`; the cross-app prefetch plan covers it.
124.4 [x] The reuse matrix, measured (plan 17 phase 1, 2026-10-16), with the harness tests/e2e/prefetch-reuse.ts: a landing page warms a module of an app by `none`, `rel=prefetch` (as=script), `fetch()`, or `modulepreload`, then the app loads it; one module URL per trial, a fresh context per trial, and the server counts downloads after the warm step and in all. Same-origin app under /app, in the grid (docs `prefetch-reuse.test.ts` `same-origin reuse`, node-prod): Playwright Chromium 149 and Firefox 151 reuse all three; Playwright WebKit 26.5 ignores `prefetch`, downloads a `fetch()` again, and reuses `modulepreload`. Stock browsers, headless, with names mapped to the loopback in the browser (`reuse-probe.com`, `app.reuse-probe.com`, `other-probe.net`; the same with `*.localhost`), through .tmp/grunt/p14/plan17/stock-reuse.ts (Chrome over CDP, Firefox over WebDriver BiDi on its own `--remote-debugging-port`, a user context per trial): Chrome 154 reuses all three for the same origin and the same-site subdomain and downloads all three again cross-site; Firefox 155 reuses all three for the same origin only and downloads all three again for the subdomain and cross-site. So a same-origin app is the one topology every engine reuses, with `modulepreload` where `prefetch` is unsupported (124.3, Safari 17.6); a subdomain works in Chrome and Safari and not in Firefox; cross-site works nowhere.
124.5 [x] The modulepreload path's in-flight limit, measured in real Safari 26.6.1 (plan 17 leaf 2.2.1, .tmp/grunt/p14/plan17/measure-limit.ts): the docs app built with each limit and booted in production, /docs/routing opened 3 times per limit, a requestAnimationFrame recorder injected ahead of every page script (by the measuring proxy, which drops the CSP on that HTML only; the first attempt, injected after hydration, saw 0 frames inside the warm window and was discarded), and once each modulepreload link has its resource entry: the warm window from the first one's start to the last one's end, and the frame gaps inside it. 14 chunks every run. Limit 4: 34, 32, 32 ms, max gap 38, 41, 35 ms. Limit 8: 42, 32, 35 ms, max gap 35, 43, 45 ms. Unlimited: 28, 34, 32 ms, max gap 48, 35, 39 ms. No gap over 50 ms at any limit, so the default is unlimited (decision B127).
124.6 [x] Red and green (plan 17 leaves 2.1, 2.2, 1.3). Unit: prefetch.test `all without prefetch support` (relPrefetch false) found a rel=prefetch link, red; green with the modulepreload fallback. Real Safari: verify:safari `modules warm in Safari` (docs /docs/routing, the idle step's own links beyond Vite's hydration-time modulepreloads, then the Playground nav link) failed `the idle step warmed nothing` with rel=prefetch and passes with modulepreload, 7 of 7 in Safari 26.6.1. The flow's first form counted Vite's links as warm and saw `DIdyS3i02.js`, the Playground route chunk, fetched on the navigation; with the wait for the idle step, the route chunk is reused, and `BgTWtLmb.js` (451 B), a chunk the route chunk imports, is still fetched: neither rel=prefetch nor modulepreload warms a chunk's own imports, in any engine. That is leaf 2.5.
124.7 [x] The modulepreload fallback (73007bde) logs a console warning per warmed chunk in WebKit: 192b0e85's verify grid failed docs-smoke `preloads settle` on node-prod, bun-prod, and workers-prod webkit, 18 lines of `The resource …/assets/….js was preloaded using link preload but not used within a few seconds from the window's load event`. Playwright's WebKit has no rel=prefetch either, so it took the fallback, and WebKit's unused-preload check about 3 s after load flags every modulepreload the page has not used. Real Safari is the same engine. verify:safari passed because no flow watched the console; it now watches what the page's own scripts log (`console problems fail`), but safaridriver has no log endpoint, so the engine's own messages reach no flow: the grid's WebKit (`preloads settle`) is the guard for them. Fixed by warming by import there (decision B127, 124.9).
124.8 [x] Two commits that can never be stamped: fe712263 added tests/e2e/prefetch-reuse.test.ts, a Playwright spec, without excluding it from vitest's unit-node project, where Playwright's `test()` throws on import, so its unit gate failed; 73007bde carries the same file. The full unit suite was not run before committing. Fixed forward by 192b0e85: the exclude, and grid.test `playwright specs outside vitest`, which matches every tests/e2e spec against Playwright's testIgnore and vitest's exclude (red without the exclude, naming the spec). Pushing past the two needs the user's call: a one-time `--no-verify`, or a squash of the local commits.
124.9 [x] The import path, measured in real Safari 26.6.1 on the docs app (.tmp/grunt/p14/plan17/measure-import.ts, the 124.5 method; 3 runs): 30 chunks loaded after the load event, the route modules and their imports, in 1127, 1121, 1108 ms, max frame gap 47, 37, 39 ms, none over 50; no stylesheet added and no computed style changed on 125 elements of the header, sidebar, and article; and the Playground navigation after it fetched no script. Red, then green: prefetch.test `all without prefetch support` read a modulepreload link before the change and reads no link and the about module imported after; verify:safari `modules warm in Safari`, tightened to no script at all, failed `the navigation fetched 1 script(s): …/assets/BgTWtLmb.js` with the modulepreload path and passes with the import, 7 of 7 with the console watch.
124.10 [x] The closure for rel=prefetch engines (plan 17 leaf 2.5): the client build replaces a marker after each route's chunk URL in `chunks` with the files that chunk imports, through others too, each once, without what the app's entry loads at boot or an import outside the bundle (vite/codegen/closures.ts), and `"all"` prefetches each file once, skipping a loaded route's whole closure. Red: environments.test `chunk closures` (two routes sharing a module) found no shared chunk in either entry; prefetch.test `all closures` read each route's files as one joined href; docs-navigation `all modules` (production, Playground after the idle step) downloaded `CQjD12eu.js`, the Playground chunk's import, in node-prod chromium and firefox with the marker removed. Green on node-prod chromium, firefox, webkit and bun-prod chromium. The harness's static layer now serves `/assets/*` immutable, as dx/link.md requires of a deploy: without it every engine downloaded the prefetched files again.
124.11 [x] Workers served the warmed files to be revalidated (decision B130): abb68205's verify failed docs-navigation `all modules` on workers-prod-chromium (the Playground chunk `CL8nkQ3A2.js` and its import `CQjD12eu.js` downloaded after the warm step), since the assets binding answers hashed files `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate` and Flare set nothing. Red: docs-server `assets immutable` read that value on workers-prod-chromium (node-prod and bun-prod passed through the harness's static layer); asset-headers.test failed to import a module that did not exist. Green: the plugin appends the rule to the client output's `_headers` (merged with the app's, the app's `/assets/*` rule kept with one warning; unit 2 of 2, 100% coverage), and `assets immutable` and `all modules` pass on workers-prod chromium, firefox, webkit, node-prod and bun-prod chromium, 10 of 10. Miniflare reads `_headers`.
124.12 [x] Closed (2026-10-18, flare-e4's triage): fixed by 163283a1 (`modules "all"` warms routes and imports in Safari, served immutable, B130), pushed. Every sub-row's red and green is in the tree: prefetch.test `all without prefetch support` and `all closures`, scripts/verify-safari.ts `modules warm in Safari`, docs-smoke `preloads settle`, docs-navigation `all modules`, and docs-server `assets immutable`; verify:safari passed 7 of 7 in Safari 26.6.1 at f90b4fc6. 124.8's call is moot: main was pushed past fe712263 and 73007bde long ago, so neither commit needs a stamp.

125 [x] docs-server `body limit`'s small POST was reset once on workers-prod-chromium
125.1 [x] f0d1075c's second verify grid, run alone under the heavy lock, failed 1 of 1248: workers-prod-chromium docs-server `body limit`, `apiRequestContext.post: read ECONNRESET` on the 13-byte POST before the 8 MiB one; the call log shows the request and no response. The boot's kept server log (.tmp/grunt/p14/test-results-verify-b126b) has workerd's `kj/async-io-unix.c++:186: disconnected: ::write(fd, buffer.begin(), buffer.size()): Broken pipe`. Not reproduced since: the case alone 30 of 30, the whole docs-server spec 10 times 190 of 190, and every docs spec in grid order on one worker 3 times with `body limit` passing 3 of 3 (that run's one failure, `old snapshot shown`, was my own source edit landing while its first boot built, so that boot served the old style text; the restarted worker's rebuild passed). Playwright's API requests share one module-level `keepAlive: true` agent per worker process (playwright-core coreBundle `httpHappyEyeballsAgent`) with `maxRetries` 0, so a socket an earlier case left idle can carry this POST.
125.2 [x] Cause, measured (.tmp/grunt/p14/b125/race.ts, a raw Node 24 keep-alive agent like Playwright's, on production docs boots): workers-prod (miniflare's workerd) closes an idle keep-alive socket 5.0 s after a response (4997, 5004, 5003 ms) and sends no `Keep-Alive` hint; a POST sent on that reused socket 4993 to 5003 ms after the response was reset, `ECONNRESET`, 17 of 123 sends swept across the close. Node-prod sends `Keep-Alive: timeout=5` but closes at 6.0 s, and the agent, built without a `timeout`, ignores the hint: 3 of 82 resets. So any `request` call that lands on a socket an earlier case left idle about 5 s can fail on any runtime. A `connection: close` request header does not stop it: through Playwright two calls still share one connection, and workerd keeps the socket open (16 of 123 resets with the header).
125.3 [x] Fix (flare-3c's ruling: no retry, which would hide a genuine reset and can replay a POST): the specs call `http`, crash.ts's per-case client (tests/e2e/http.ts) on a `keepAlive: false` agent, so every call opens its own connection and none outlives its request, whatever a server's idle close. It takes the call shape the specs used (`get` and `post` with `headers`, `data`, `maxRedirects`), so the 45 calls only renamed `request` to `http`; Playwright types an overridden `request` fixture as `APIRequestContext`, so the smaller client takes its own name. Red: docs-server `request socket per call` (a counting server resets a second request on a socket that already answered) failed on Playwright's `request` with `read ECONNRESET`, 6 of 6 on node-prod and workers-prod chromium; green 6 of 6; red again, 4 of 4, with the client's agent kept alive. grid.test `http client only` was red listing the seven docs specs that took `request`. Every docs spec on node-prod, workers-prod, and node-dev chromium and bun-prod firefox: 604 passed. No public helper is affected: `@lovrozagar/flare/testing` hands out `flarePage` (a browser page) and `createTestServer` (an in-process fetch), not Playwright's `request`.

126 [x] docs-navigation `pointer during transition` read a finished transition under grid load
126.1 [x] The navigation spec on all 18 projects three times with four workers (a grid-load repro for 127) failed workers-prod-webkit `pointer during transition`: `{ running: false, hit: "/docs/defer", hovered: true }` where `{ running: true, hit: "HTML", hovered: false }` was expected. The case saw the transition ready in one round trip and paused its animations in the next; under load the 200 ms transition finished between them, so the case read the live page after it. The test's own race, not B126's behavior.
126.2 [x] Fix: the init script pauses the animations in the same task as `ready`, and the case then waits 400 ms, longer than the transition, before it reads, so a hold that did not take fails every time. Red: the old two-step pause with that 400 ms wait failed 8 of 8 on node-prod chromium, firefox, and webkit and node-dev chromium with the grid's signature; green 25 of 25 on those and workers-prod-webkit, the floor skipped.

127 [x] navigation `live stream ssr` hangs in Firefox under grid load
127.1 [x] Three failures, each a 30 s timeout in `flushEvents` after the page streamed STREAMED: ef2e3fdb's verify grid (workers-prod-firefox), the navigation spec on 18 projects three times (node-prod-firefox, 1 of 54), and a 200-run probe arm (bun-prod-firefox). The trace: `page.route` turns interception on, then the marker `evaluate` (a `console.log` and a `fetch`) never returns and the page emits neither; tearing the context down then also exceeds the timeout, while screencast frames keep arriving every 240 ms. In both traced failures three route chunks start 4 to 7 ms before interception turns on. It passes alone (80 of 80) and in spec order (660 of 660). f0d1075c's first verify failed the same case on node-dev-chromium with a 404 console error, kept only as text.
127.2 [x] Ruled out so far: prefetch links in flight while interception turns on, without Flare (30 of 30 in Firefox); interception toggled 1,200 times while fetches start (0 hangs in Firefox or Chromium); a Flare-free page with prefetch links (0 of 200). Running: the case, the case without its prefetch links, and the case with the flush route installed when the context opens, 1,000 runs each on four Firefox projects.
127.3 [x] Measured: 1,000 runs each on four Firefox projects, the case (A) 0 stalls, the case with its prefetch links dropped (B) 1 stall (node-prod-firefox, the marker not back within 10 s), and the flush route installed before the page loads (G) 0. B's stall says the prefetch links in flight are not needed. Then the navigation spec on 18 projects ×3 at 4 workers, six rounds: 10,692 cases and 0 stalls (round 5's one failure is 128). The lost `evaluate` is the same family as B34 and 107.9/107.10, Playwright's Firefox driver (Juggler) never answering a call on a live page.
127.4 [x] Host load is not confirmed as a cause. The core dumps were the one removable load (103.6), and the memory floor did not follow them; with one run per arm, load cannot be split from the grid's own and other sessions' use of this host. The case runs as it did; the next failure keeps its trace.
127.5 [x] Two Juggler mechanisms, found 2026-10-16 (flare-3c), after CI 38871975650 on 163283a1 failed docs-smoke `foreign head extension › fights back` on workers-prod-firefox (a 30 s test timeout with the page on createServer) and af8e8f8a's verify failed `rapid links` on bun-prod-firefox (an evaluate that never ran: no navigation at all). (a) Lost or misrouted replies: Playwright's Firefox driver named every page's content channel `process-<pid>`, and SimpleChannel answered a reconnecting channel from another channel's buffered responses when a content process was reused, so a call's reply went to the wrong request or nowhere (microsoft/playwright#42731, fixed in Firefox r1551, `'process-' + processID + '-' + generateId()`, shipped in 1.64). Our 1.61.1 runs Firefox 151 without it. (b) A stuck navigation on a document served under `stale-while-revalidate`: Firefox shows the cached document and revalidates it with a second navigation request, and Playwright waits forever on "navigation to finish". Measured without Flare (scratch bare-nav.mjs, a page with Flare's cdn header, back then goto): 3 of 20 on 1.61.1/Firefox 151 and on 1.64.1/Firefox 157, 0 of 60 without the header. (b) is not fixed upstream; Flare stops sending browsers the directive (BUGS 135, which also fixes the stale page Firefox users saw). The upgrade to 1.64.1 (image `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.64.1-noble@sha256:7852dc5a…`) takes (a). No red case reaches (a) on demand here: #42731's own repro stalls 0 of 200 on this Linux host. The evidence is the two failures' shape, the upstream mechanism, and the grid after the upgrade.
127.6 [x] Closed by flare-e4's review (2026-10-18): 127.5's close condition, the grid after the Playwright 1.64.1 upgrade (7e2a17cc, upstream microsoft/playwright#42731 for mechanism (a); mechanism (b) is BUGS 135), is met: no `live stream ssr` failure since; the last is chain8/af8e8f8a, before the upgrade. The evidence window: 35 green CI runs on main since the Playwright 1.64.1 upgrade (7e2a17cc, 2026-10-16T09:04Z), whose two failures are unrelated unit cases and in which no e2e case failed, and 42 local verifies (unit, Bun, e2e grid) from 2026-10-17 09:15 to 22:04 under ~/.cache/flare-gates/chain11 with none of the failure signatures. Reopen on a traced Firefox lost reply.
128 [x] A host-side test worker is SIGKILLed with no OOM, oomd, or cgroup record
128.1 [x] Two failures, each `worker process exited unexpectedly (code=null, signal=SIGKILL)` on the first case of a new worker, 0 ms: node-prod-webkit docs-navigation `view transition document` in round 5 of 127.3, and bun-dev-chromium docs-cache `ssg seed` in 103.6's core=0 grid. The journal has no oom-kill, systemd-oomd, or memory-cgroup line for either window. Playwright's own SIGKILL (`_forceKill`) fires only 5 minutes after a stop it sent, and the ulimit acts inside the browser container only. The worker's PID was not recorded.
128.2 [x] A hypothesis to test, not a cause: other agent sessions share this host, and a `pkill -f node`, `killall bun`, or the like from any of them SIGKILLs a matching process with no kernel record. Next occurrence: a `ps -eo pid,ppid,lstart,args` poll every second for the run's window, so the worker's PID, its exit time, and the processes alive around it are known, and timestamps to set against the other sessions' logs.
128.3 [x] A third instance (2026-10-15): ab718d24's verify grid, node-prod-firefox minimal `hydrate` (0 ms), `vite exited` with no output: the production server child died at boot, no record. From now on every verify runs a 1 s `ps -eo pid,ppid,lstart,args` poll into .tmp/grunt/p14/b128-ps-<label>.txt.
128.4 [x] A fourth instance, the first with the poll (2026-10-16): 79ddec23's verify, node-prod-firefox minimal `hydrate` (0 ms) again, now `vite build exited null`: the minimal app's production build, PID 652699, killed by a signal while rendering chunks, 177 modules transformed. It started just after the 01:11:09 snapshot and is in none, so it lived under a second; the journal from 01:11:00 to 01:11:20 has no oom, oomd, or kill line. Alive outside the lock at the time: another session's `timeout 590 bun …/e2e-baseline.ts` (monorepo, started 01:03:05), which the pre-run check (`verify|playwright test`) does not match. Same case and project as 128.3, at the same step. Candidates to tell apart, none shown: a kill pattern from another session (128.2); Claude Code's memory-pressure reaper, which stopped this session's background verifies earlier the same night, taking one large child instead of the whole task. Next: MemAvailable in every poll snapshot, and the verify run in the foreground, where that reaper does not act.
128.5 [x] A fifth, in the foreground (2026-10-16): 79ddec23's verify run as a foreground command, where Claude Code's idle reaper does not act, failed workers-dev-firefox minimal `basepath › base path serves /app` (0 ms): the workers dev server, `vite --host 127.0.0.1 --port 0` PID 1048950 started 01:32:39, last in the 01:32:41 snapshot, exited with only its startup warnings as output; its workerd child 1050816 started 01:32:40. MemAvailable 5.4 GB then, 7.8 GB two seconds later. The journal has no oom, oomd, or kill line. Outside the lock: another project's `timeout 900 npx vitest run src/emit/go` from 01:32:34. So the reaper is not needed for a death: it is out as the sole cause. The harness's boot error drops the child's exit code and signal (boot.ts rejects with the output text only), so a kill (SIGKILL, SIGTERM) cannot yet be told from a crash (SIGSEGV, SIGABRT); that is the next discriminator.
128.6 [x] The discriminator (2026-10-16): boot.ts names a dead child's exit code or signal in both rejections, the server's `vite exited (signal SIGKILL)` and the compile's `vite build exited (…)`, through `exitOf` (tests/e2e/child.ts, split from boot.ts at its 300-line limit); child.test `death named` (a child that prints and then SIGKILLs itself) was red with only `starting\n` and is green. The verify poll also records each process's pgid and cgroup, so a kill that takes a whole process group or cgroup (systemd-oomd kills by cgroup under pressure) reads apart from a single-PID kill.
128.7 [x] A confirmed cross-session pattern-kill of an unrelated Flare server; ruled out as 128's cause (2026-10-16). honey-d9 reported that a research subagent of theirs killed flare-3c's repro server `node .tmp/grunt/p14/b119/repro/server.mjs 4500` (PID 778484) with `ps -eo pid,args | grep "[n]ode .*server.mjs" | … kill` at 03:52:35Z. Their scan of every honey subagent transcript on this machine finds four kill commands, all between 03:52:00Z and 03:52:36Z, all `server.mjs` patterns, all plain SIGTERM: none near 128's deaths (23:11Z and 23:32Z on Oct 15, and the earlier ones), none that matches vite, workerd, or bun. honey-a8's direct commands are not scanned; that is chased only if the next death reads as a single-PID SIGKILL (128.6). A related leak, found the same morning: when a dev server dies, its workerd child outlives it (PID 1050816, from 128.5's dead `vite`, alive 4.5 h, ignoring SIGTERM, until SIGKILLed by PID), so the harness's close does not reach a dead child's descendants; that orphan held the next verify's pre-run check for 4.5 h.
128.8 [x] The leak fixed (2026-10-16): bootChild spawns its child as a process-group leader (`detached`), and `signalGroup` (tests/e2e/child.ts) reaches the whole group: SIGKILL when the child cannot start or the test process exits, and on close SIGTERM, the child awaited, then SIGKILL for whatever ignored it. boot.test `group closed` (a runtime that starts a grandchild, as the Workers dev server starts workerd): red before, the grandchild of a server that died while starting stayed alive; green, nothing of the group is alive after a failed start or a close, and the child itself has exited; red again without the kill on a failed start. The pre-run check now logs an orphan of a removed verify worktree as a leak instead of waiting on it, and after 15 minutes blocked it stops to report its blockers.
128.9 [x] A sixth instance (2026-10-16): c1e0f596's verify, unit `test`, golden.test `workers` (993 ms), `vite build exited (signal SIGKILL)`. The poll (.tmp/grunt/p14/b128-ps-router9.txt) puts golden in vitest worker 3198017 and its Workers build at PID 3206499, started 08:13:58 and gone before the 08:14:00 snapshot. The build's pgid was the verify's own group, 3192562, whose other members lived on, so this was a single-PID kill, not a group or cgroup kill. MemAvailable was 12.2 GB; the verify's scope has no memory limit and its memory.events read `oom_kill 0`; the journal from 08:13:55 to 08:14:03 has no oom or kill line. The only processes outside the verify that started in the window were another project's tsc and git commit; the heavy lock kept this session's own runs out. strace, perf, tracefs, and auditd are unavailable without root, so the next discriminator is in-tree: .tmp/grunt/p14/kill-log.cjs, loaded through NODE_OPTIONS=--require in every verify, logs each `process.kill` and `ChildProcess#kill` a Node process of the run sends, with sender, target, signal, and stack. On the next instance, a logged SIGKILL of the dead PID names its sender. A clean log means only that no Node process of the run sent it: Bun processes (`bun run`, bunx), native children, and the browser container's processes are not covered.
128.10 [x] PID or PGID reuse ruled out (2026-10-16, flare-3c's lead). A SIGKILL our own tree sends to a pid or group whose process had exited would hit a new process only if Linux had handed that number out again. It allocates PIDs cyclically up to pid_max, 4,194,304 here, and this host went from PID 3.19M at 08:13 to 3.61M at 08:57, about 9,500 a minute, so a number comes back about every 7 hours. Every SIGKILL a verify sends names a pid or pgid from the same run, minutes old: c1e0f596's clean verify (router10, kill-log-router10.jsonl) sent 205, 146 group kills from tests/e2e/child.ts `signalGroup` (139 from close() after the child exited, the grandchild sweep, 5 from onExit, 2 from a failed start), 58 from miniflare's `Runtime.dispose` of its workerd, and 1 from child.test `death named` to itself. The close() sweep after exit stays: it is what reaches a grandchild that ignored SIGTERM, and the reuse arithmetic covers it. kill-log.cjs now also records what each target is at send time (its pgid, start time, and args from /proc, each member of a group, and a ChildProcess's spawn args), so the next instance shows whether any SIGKILL named the dead PID or its group, and what that target was then.

129 [x] A client navigation that starts after Flare began a document load loads its own page over the user's latest click
129.1 [x] Red evidence, CI 38810765493 on 6e99ddba, e2e-linux 1/3, node-dev-webkit docs-smoke `rapid links` (artifacts ~/.cache/flare-gates/ci-38810765493/docs-smoke-rapid-links-node-dev-webkit): `#doc-title` never showed Routing; the page ended on /playground. Navigation log, ms: 923 Changelog commits and the case clicks API, Examples, Playground, Docs 30 ms apart; nothing fails or finishes until 4072 `/examples?_flare=data` `WebKit encountered an internal error`; 4123 `example-links.tsx` (the examples module, still loading), `/playground?_flare=data`, and a blob cancelled; 4129 `playground.page.tsx` cancelled and `document /examples`; 4151 `document /examples` cancelled and `document /playground`; 4195 /playground commits.
129.2 [x] Cause, from the code and the log. Every fallback checks `trip.signal.aborted` synchronously at the load (request.ts: the chunk error after both settle, the deploy mismatch, the body failure), so a superseded failure loads nothing: a probe holding every data and module request 1.5 s and then failing `/examples?_flare=data` (and, in a second arm, the examples module) ended on Routing with one document request, 4 of 4 on node-dev webkit and chromium (.tmp/grunt/p14/b129/zrapid.test.ts). In CI, examples' module was still loading at 4123, so `document /examples` was land()'s body fallback after the 4072 failure, with examples still the latest trip: the Playground click had not run, though it was due 30 ms after Examples, and `/playground?_flare=data` started between 4072 and 4123. The page's main thread was held until the network failure (the B103 family; inferred, not shown: WebKit keeps web storage in its network process). Then the queued clicks ran: Playground started a client navigation while the /examples document load was underway, that unload cancelled its chunk, and as the latest trip it fell back to `document /playground`, which replaced /examples and buried the Docs click after it. The defect: Flare keeps starting client navigations after it has begun a document load itself, and the unload's cancellations then read as failures.
129.3 [x] Fix, red first. Once the navigator begins a document load (a guarded reload that started, or an assign, the server functions' included), the page is leaving: a later navigation to another page loads it as a document and fetches no data, and a revalidation does nothing; `pageshow` with `persisted` ends it. Red: `leaving` (no `assign`, both fetched) and `leaving restored` (no `pageshow`), first in navigate.test, now client/navigation/leaving.test.ts beside leaving.ts, which holds the state; docs-resilience `click after reload starts` (Caching's data unreadable, its document held 1 s, Auth clicked meanwhile) ended on Caching, 6 of 6 on node-prod and node-dev chromium, firefox, webkit. Green: the client units 170 of 170, the e2e 18 of 18 (3 each). Mutations: without the leaving check `leaving`, `leaving restored`, `pageshow`, and the e2e (3 of 3) go red; without the pageshow listener `pageshow` goes red. A hover's intent prefetch may still leave while the page is leaving: a prefetch never falls back to a document load. Commit 98705db3's subject says "(b129)" for this BUGS 129, while ab718d24's "(b129)" means decision B129 (the content-only view transition); subjects cannot be amended, so later ones say "decision B129" or "BUGS 129".
129.4 [x] Closed (2026-10-18, flare-e4's triage): fixed by 98705db3 (a navigation after Flare began a document load loads its page too), pushed; client/navigation/leaving.ts with leaving.test `leaving`, `leaving restored`, and `pageshow`, and docs-resilience `click after reload starts`. 35 green CI runs since, each with node-dev-webkit docs-smoke `rapid links`, the case that found it.

130 [x] A route file whose builder id string disagrees with its path builds
130.1 [x] Found by phase 9's routes.md integration (plan 14 9.6, 2026-10-16): an app whose `_root_/about/about.page.tsx` calls `createPage("_root_/abut")` built with `vite build` exit 0 and served `/about`, where dx/routes.md, dx/builder.md, and bits/02 call the mismatch a build error that names the file. Cause: `assertId` (vite/codegen/id-string.ts) is called only by its own test; codegen never compared a route file's id string with its path, so a hand edit or a moved file kept its old id silently (the router matches the generated id, so the page served while `Link` types and `from` read the stale one). Red: routes.test (integration) `id mismatch`, `expected +0 not to be +0` (the build's exit status); codegen routes.test `id mismatch`, `expected [Function generate] to throw an error`; scan.test `builder ids`, `expected undefined to deeply equal [ '_root_/a' ]`.
130.2 [x] Fix: scanRoute records the ids a file's `createPage`, `createLayout`, `createRootLayout`, or `createPathSegment` calls name, a string literal or a module-level const bound to one (the docs app's `createPage(ID)`), and generateRoutes fails with `<file>: the id string "<found>" is not "<id>"` for any that differs from the file's page, layout, root-layout, or segment id. An argument the scan cannot resolve is not checked. Green: all three cases; codegen routes.test's shared fixture source names its id by an unresolved name, and write.test `watch`'s second home page carries the id its path gives, so that case still reaches `two pages`. The docs, minimal, basepath, and types apps' 55 route files all pass the check. Open (a product decision for the user, put to them by the reviewer): dx/routes.md and dx/builder.md say codegen writes the id, but `writeId` (vite/codegen/id-string.ts) is wired nowhere and its `assertId` is superseded by this check. Either wire `writeId` in dev, rewriting a created or moved file's string as TanStack Router does, with this build check as the backstop, or drop the claim and the file. Until then id-string.ts and the sentence stay.

130.3 [x] The open question closed (2026-10-16, the user's ruling through the reviewer: option (a), plan 19). `vite dev` now writes each route file's builder id strings to the id its path gives, as TanStack Router's generator owns its `createFileRoute` string: at dev start and on every watch pass, a hand edit included, since a file's id can only ever be its path's (the build check fails anything else). It writes what the scan resolves: a literal, or a module-level const, exported or not (the scan now reads exported consts too), whose every use then names the new id. A template or an imported name is left alone. A file saved between the read and the write is left for the next pass, and each rewrite logs `[flare:codegen] <file>: id "<old>" -> "<new>"`. A build only checks, and leaves the source as it was. id-string.ts, whose `writeId` and `assertId` nothing called, is gone. dx/routes.md's and dx/builder.md's "codegen writes the id" are now true. Red: scan.test `builder id spans` (`expected [ undefined ]`), fix-ids.test (no module), write.test `fix ids` (the build check threw) and `fix ids save race`, write.test `watch` (the page not in routes.gen.ts). Green, and routes-dev.test `start fixes`, `created page`, `moved page`, and `unresolved id` through a real `vite dev`; seven mutations each red (dev start or the watch not fixing, the quote dropped, the const not followed, no re-read, no log line).

132 [x] With a custom `assetsBase`, the server renders every imported asset's URL under `/assets/`, where no file is
132.1 [x] Found by phase 9's plugin.md integration (2026-10-16): an app built with `flare({ assetsBase: "/static" })` whose page imports `logo.svg` (over Vite's inline limit) wrote the file to `dist/client/static/logo-0arWmmLx.svg`, and its client bundle points at `/static/logo-0arWmmLx.svg`, but the built server renders `<img src="/assets/logo-0arWmmLx.svg">`: a 404 in the server's HTML, and a different URL from the one the client renders over it. The same held for an image `flare({ image: { exclude } })` hands back to Vite. With the default `/assets` the two agree, which is why no test saw it. dx/plugin.md says `assetsBase` is the URL prefix of built files and that every URL Vite writes comes from the one resolver. Cause: `buildConfig` gave the client build `assetsDir` from `assetsBase` but left the server builds (ssr, the Workers ssr, and the Workers prerender build) on Vite's default `assets`, so Vite named the asset `assets/<file>` there and `assetUrl` turned that into `/assets/<file>`. Red: plugin.test `assets base`, `expected [ '.vite/manifest.json', …(33) ] to include 'assets/logo-0arWmmLx.svg'`; environments.test `assets dir`, `expected 'assets' to be 'cdn'` for the ssr environment's `assetsDir` under `assetsBase: "/cdn"`.
132.2 [x] Fix: every server build gets the client's `assetsDir`, so an imported asset has the same name, and URL, in both. Server chunks under a custom base now sit in `dist/server/<dir>/`, which only the server's own imports name. Checked (2026-10-16): `git grep` finds no hard-coded `server/assets` path in packages/core or scripts (adapters, prerender, golden, size, boot.ts), only `assetUrl`'s own `assetsBase === "/"` branch. plugin-workers.test `workers build` covers the Workers ssr and prerender builds under `/static`, and B130's `_headers` rule follows the base. Green: both cases, and the vite suite.

133 [x] The e2e flush helper can send its own marker to the server, whose 404 fails the case
133.1 [x] Found by the reviewer's verify chain (2026-10-16): af8e8f8a's grid failed navigation `live stream ssr` on bun-prod-chromium with `error: Failed to load resource: the server responded with a status of 404`, and its navigation log holds `response 404 /__flush/<uuid>`, the flush helper's own marker. 127.1 records the same 404 on node-dev-chromium in f0d1075c's first verify. Cause: `flushEvents` (tests/e2e/flush.ts) removed its route once the marker's request and console events had arrived, but Playwright can deliver the route event that runs the handler after both; with the route already gone, the request went on to the server, which has no such path. Red: flush.test `route until handled`, a page double whose route event comes after the request and console events, `expected 'sent to the server' to be 'fulfilled'`. The double models that delivery order; the evidence that Playwright delivers it is the two grids' logged `response 404 /__flush/<uuid>`, not the unit case.
133.2 [x] Fix: the route stays until its handler has fulfilled the marker, and `flushEvents` waits for that with the two events. Green: `route until handled`. The case runs in vitest and Playwright ignores it (grid.test `playwright specs outside vitest`).
133.3 [x] The fix's own hang, found in flare-14's review of 1cc82334: a marker whose request failed before any route saw it (the page navigating or closing with the fetch in flight) never runs the handler, so `flushEvents` waited for it until the test timeout, where before it ended at the request and console events. Red: flush.test `failed marker ends`, a double that fires `requestfailed` for the marker and never calls the handler, `expected 'still waiting' to be 'ended'`. Fix: the flush ends on the handler's answer or the marker's `requestfailed`, whichever comes first; a failed marker reached neither the route nor the server. The losing wait's later timeout is caught. Green: both cases.

Now the server names the failed match in `flare-failed`, a 404 walks `notFoundRender`, the client paints at that match, and a failed root loads the URL as a document. Red: builder-client.test `page not found navigation`, `layout not found navigation`, and `root failure navigation` timed out in firefox (no not-found ever painted, no load); minimal.test `layout not found navigation` on node-prod and node-dev firefox, `area missing` never found. 135 [x] Flare's public cdn header made Firefox show the previous document after a deploy
135.1 [x] Found by the reviewer (2026-10-16, a server that numbers its responses): with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=0, s-maxage=<n>, stale-while-revalidate=<swr>`, Firefox 151 served the previous document from its own HTTP cache on a normal document navigation and revalidated it behind (the second load showed version 1 after 2 requests); Chromium 149 and WebKit 26.5 showed the fresh one, and without the directive all three did. dx/cache.md said browsers revalidate so a deploy reaches them. A Flare-free page with the header also stalls Playwright's Firefox, 3 of 20. Red: policies.test `header` (the swr on Cache-Control) and the cases that make an answer private keeping a CDN header (`private header`, `headers cannot`, `response auth`, `exit headers`); minimal.test `cdn page fresh` on node-prod-firefox, `Expected: "2"`, `Received: "1"`, with Chromium and WebKit green.
135.2 [x] Fix (decision B131): browsers get `public, max-age=0, s-maxage=<n>`; CDNs get `CDN-Cache-Control: public, max-age=<n>, stale-while-revalidate=<swr>` (RFC 9213). Every path that makes an answer private (the exit's late cookie or a middleware's `private`, the locale downgrade, an auth `.response()` chain) drops it with `Cache-Tag`; `.headers()` cannot set it, and a stored entry does not keep it. Flare's stores read the decision, not the header. Green: the unit cases and the docs e2e assertions on the two headers; `cdn page fresh` waits on the 1.64.1 browser container.

138 [x] `redirect()` defaulted to 307 on a document, against B71's 303, and its cited proof tested dead code
138.1 [x] Found by phase 9's builder.md integration (2026-10-16): B71 and dx/builder.md say `status` defaults to `303`. shared/errors/redirect.ts defaulted to 307 and flagged `defaulted`, which only the server-function envelope read (303 there); a document and a data `x` frame kept 307, and bits/05 `redirect from auth` pinned 307. builder.md cited server/redirect/target.test.ts for the default, but target.ts's `redirect` and `classify` were reached by no request: only server/redirect/index.ts re-exported them. 139 [x] Hydrate never filled the match cache or asked `shouldRefetch("initial")`
139.1 [x] Found by phase 9's builder.md integration (2026-10-16): dx/navigate.md says `trigger` is `"initial"` on hydrate, dx/builder.md that `true` from `"initial"` refetches during hydrate, and dx/cache.md that a cached match inside `staleTime` is fresh. Nothing in src produced `"initial"`, and only a navigation's landing wrote the match cache, so a revisit of the hydrated URL inside `staleTime` ran its loader again and `shouldRefetch` never saw hydrate. 139.2 [x] Fix (the reviewer's ruling, (a), (b), and (c), with the clock and the one request): the state carries `at`, the render's `Date.now()`, which a stored or CDN copy replays; hydrate (client/boot/initial.ts) seeds the document's error-free matches on `min(at, now)` and plans once with `"initial"`; a page that asks is refetched by one same-URL request carrying the plan already asked, and a document with holes sends only its fill, which runs every loader live. The cache asks each page once per plan. navigate.ts's history and scroll steps moved to steps.ts at its 300-line limit. dx/navigate.md: `"revalidation"` is a same-URL revalidate; a `loaderDeps` change on a new URL is `"navigation"`, as shipped. The harness gains a per-app store and a primed path on a faked `Date`, so `stale document` needs no real wait. Green: state.test `render time`, match-cache `seed at` and `asks once`, initial.test (4), builder-client `effects loaderDeps`, `effects shouldRefetch`, `stale document`, and `holed initial` in Chromium and Firefox; 7 mutations red (no seeding, seeding at hydration, no initial plan, a fill and a refresh both sent, shouldRefetch asked twice).

Fix (the reviewer's approval): `next.params` is the target's `defined(match.params)` and `current.params` the page on screen's, in both. The search side was the doc's: the location's `search` is the URL's string, as `EffectPlace` types it and every location in Flare is, and dx/builder.md now says so beside `ctx.search`, the raw record. Green: the case, with `search: "?tab=a"`.

140 [x] A hydrate-harness page stalled past 10 s once in the full gate
140.1 [x] Found by the full unit gate for c7e93109's cache.md work (2026-10-16, .tmp/grunt/p14/first-fail/full-cache2.txt): builder-client `effects shouldRefetch` in Chromium did not hydrate within the harness's 10 s; 37 module requests were still in flight when the context closed, and the document and its client entry had loaded. The file was green alone three times, and the next full gate was green.
140.2 [x] Measured (the reviewer's request): 128 hydrations over two full gates, median 542 ms, p95 916 ms, max 1150 ms. The failed page took nine times the worst case, so it stalled; it was not slow. The 30 s wait c7e93109 set only delays the same failure, so the wait is 10 s again. The old log has no timing, so whether its loads were progressing cannot be read back.
140.3 [x] The cause. A page that does not hydrate now reports every request's start and its end, or that it was still in flight, so the next occurrence shows which load stalled. Ruled out (the reviewer's lead, 2026-10-16): a dep optimizer or Vite cache shared with cache-build's build. Each harness gives its dev server its own `mkdtemp` cacheDir (hydrate.harness.ts), and `viteBuild` runs `vite build` in a child process with its own Vite, which runs no dev optimizer; they share no cache directory, optimizer, or process.
140.4 [x] Closed by flare-e4's review (2026-10-18): not recurred with 7896ee61's instrumentation in place, about two days of gates; no `the client did not hydrate:` in any CI run or local gate log since, the only hit being the original. The Mac's WebKit timeouts before BUGS 351 were the frozen WebKit, not this. The evidence window: 35 green CI runs on main since the Playwright 1.64.1 upgrade (7e2a17cc, 2026-10-16T09:04Z), whose two failures are unrelated unit cases and in which no e2e case failed, and 42 local verifies (unit, Bun, e2e grid) from 2026-10-17 09:15 to 22:04 under ~/.cache/flare-gates/chain11 with none of the failure signatures. Reopen on the first `the client did not hydrate:` trace, which names the stalled load.

The outlet's one `Errored` is `SafeErrored`, and tests/structure/errored.test.ts fails on any other import or namespace read of `Errored` in src. dx/builder.md's render row states the guarantee. Green: a page's string, an array at any depth, a function, a memo, a `<script>`, an element, a Loading result, an errorRender, and a layout, in dev and in a production build; the hydrated page holds the literal string with no hydration warning, in Chromium and Firefox; both mutations (the escape removed) red.

143 [x] A head script's, style's, or JSON-LD's `</script` came back lowercased, and Solid rc.15 writes JSON-LD's `<` as `\u003C`
143.1 [x] Found by the Solid rc.15 upgrade's unit gate (2026-10-16): head.test `jsonld escape` expected the JSON-LD text to hold `<\/script>` and got `\u003C\/script>`. A renderToString probe on rc.14 and rc.15 showed plain script and style elements unchanged; through Flare's render, rc.15's head writes every `<` inside an `application/ld+json` script as `\u003C`, while a head script's JS and a style keep their text. The JSON is equivalent. The same probe showed Flare's own fault: shared/head/tags.ts `keepOpen` rewrote a case-insensitive `</script` or `</style` to the lowercase tag, so a JSON-LD value `</ScRiPt>` or a JS string `'</ScRiPt>'` read back as `</script>`. Red: `jsonld escape` (the block parsed back must equal the object) and `script close` (the script keeps `<\/ScRiPt>`), both on the lowercasing.
143.2 [x] Fix: `keepOpen` writes `<\/` before the tag in its own spelling. `jsonld escape` now asserts what holds on rc.14 and rc.15 alike: no closing tag, no entity, and `JSON.parse` of the text equals the block; `custom style` keeps `<\/StYlE>`. dx/head.md says both.

144 [x] CI red on main from a case-insensitive file system collision in a codegen test
144.1 [x] Found by CI 38901585502 on 7e2a17cc (2026-10-16): e2e-mac 1/3 failed write.test `empty` with `_root_/b/b.page.tsx: the id string "_root_/B" is not "_root_/b"`. The case wrote `_root_/b/b.page.tsx` and `_root_/B/B.page.tsx` to prove UTF-16 code unit order, but macOS's case-insensitive file system keeps one file for both, so the second write replaced the first and one route held the other's id. Before BUGS 130's id check the case passed there vacuously: `indexOf` of the missing id was -1. Linux, where every local gate runs, keeps both files, so only CI's mac jobs could see it. Red on the Mac at 7e2a17cc.
144.2 [x] Fix (9744cdd8, flare-3c): the pair is `_root_/a` and `_root_/B`, which sort `B` first by code unit and after `a` in a locale sort, and the case asserts both routes are present. Green on the Mac; a locale sort in write.ts turns it red on Linux. A new codegen or file-system case that writes names differing only in case collides on macOS; CI's mac jobs are the only runner with that file system.
145 [x] A `.head()` that returned a relative canonical, or a script with both `src` and `children`, made the whole document a 500
145.1 [x] Found by head.md's integration test (2026-10-16): a page whose `.head()` returns `{ title, canonical: "/relative" }` answered 500 through the real createServer(...).fetch, and so did one returning a custom script with `src` and `children`. B82 makes a `.head()` failure that match's alone: its head omitted, the status the loader's, a dev bug. The validation ran later, in `headTags` on the merged head inside the document render, so a returned bad value escaped the per-match catch that a thrown error lands in. Red: loaders/run.test `head value bug` (the head kept, no bug reported) and tests/integration/head.test `omitted` (500).
145.2 [x] Fix: loaders/run.ts `finish` passes each match's returned head through `headTags` inside the same catch, so a value the head cannot write is that match's bug: its head omitted, its `h` frame not sent, the status the loader's. dx/head.md says so.
146 [x] A custom head script's `type` was never written, and an inline script dropped its `extra` attributes
146.1 [x] Found by head.md's integration test (2026-10-16): dx/head.md gives a custom script `src` or `children` plus optional `type`, `async`, and `extra`, but `{ src: "/ext.js", type: "module" }` came out as a classic `<script src="/ext.js">`, and `{ children, type: "module", extra: { id } }` came out with neither. `HeadScript` had no `type` field and `scriptTag` wrote `extra` only on an external script. A module script written as a classic one runs as a classic script, or not at all once it uses `import`. Red: shared/head/tags.test `script type` and tests/integration/head.test `custom`.
146.2 [x] Fix: `HeadScript` gains `type`, and shared/head/tags.ts `scriptTag` writes `type` and the allowed `extra` attributes on an external and an inline script alike; an inline script keeps the request nonce, an external one has none, and `extra.nonce` stays ignored.
147 [x] Search values reached validators, loaders, and renders still percent-encoded, and one value set spelled two ways was two matches
147.1 [x] Found by link.md's integration test (2026-10-16): a Link with search `{ text: "a b&c/é", at: Date, obj: { a: [1] } }` wrote the right URL text, but the route's `.input({ searchParams })` received `text` as `a%20b%26c%2F%C3%A9`, `at` with `%3A` so a date coercion gave Invalid Date, and `obj` as `%7B%22a%22…` so its JSON parse failed and the document was a 400. dx/link.md promises that a validator's value goes back into a Link, navigate, redirect, or buildUrl and parses back to the same value. shared/url/search.ts `readSearch` split the query without decoding ("values stay encoded"), and the match id took the query text as written, so `?q=a%20b` and `?q=a+b` were two client cache entries. The same encoded record also went back into navigate from the i18n provider's setLocale and the outlet's reset, which encoded it again (`a%20b` became `a%2520b`). Red: shared/url/search.test `read decoded` and `search key`; tests/integration/link.test `url values` (400); link-client.test `search spelling` (a data request per spelling) and `url values`.
147.2 [x] Fix, as flare-3c ruled: `readSearch` decodes each key and value as URLSearchParams does, `+` a space, a malformed escape kept as written, and a banned key banned after decoding. `searchKey` writes that record back with keys sorted and values encoded, and the server's data answer, the client cache, and the outlet make the match id from it, so every spelling of one value set is one id on both sides. A literal `%41` goes out as `%2541`. Params stay encoded (B81). Decision edit: B31 says the raw search is the decoded, unvalidated record and how the id holds it; dx/types.md and dx/builder.md say the search is decoded and the params encoded.
148 [x] A Link to the hash already current did nothing, so after a scroll away a second click did not go back to the section
148.1 [x] Back and Forward between two hashes of one page had no browser test. tests/e2e/docs-hash.test.ts `hash back and forward` (every project): Files, then Params from the docs TOC, then Back and Forward, each entry at its section, with no data request and no document load. Green before and after the fix.
148.2 [x] Hash behavior was covered only in jsdom. docs-hash.test.ts runs on the grid: `hash link again` (a hash Link, the same page, no request), `hash back and forward`, and `hash on another page` (data-loading's new see-also Link to routing#params lands on the section after one data request and no document load).
148.3 [x] The user's report (2026-10-16): clicking a `#x` Link whose hash is already current did nothing. Link's click handler returned when the link was active and its hash equal to the location's, and a plain anchor to the same URL was a data navigation, since `isHashOnly` held only for a different hash. The browser goes to the target again and replaces the entry. Red first: docs-hash.test `hash link again` in node-prod chromium, firefox, and webkit (the section not in view after the second click), and link.test `same hash`, target.test `hash`, navigate.test `same hash again`.
