Fix: `isHashMove` (renamed from `isHashOnly`) holds for the hash already current too, so the target is an in-place hash move with no request; the navigator replaces the entry when the URL is the current one; an active Link stays put only when neither it nor the location has a hash. dx/navigate.md says so.
148.4 [x] The 148.3 fix regressed fills and refreshes at a hash (plan 21 phase 1, an outside review; found before push). `isHashMove` held for the URL already current whenever it had a hash, and the navigator's fill and hydrate's refresh resolve the page on screen, hash included, through `resolveUrl`, so a stored holed page loaded at `/holed#x` never filled, a refresh at a hash never fetched, and `navigate({ revalidate: true })` to the current URL with a hash fetched nothing. Red first: tests/e2e/navigation.test `stored stream fills at a hash` (node-prod chromium, firefox, webkit), tests/integration/builder-client.test `holed initial at a hash` (each engine), navigate.test `refresh at a hash` and `revalidate at a hash`. Fix: `isHashMove` is a different hash again; target.ts `hashAgain` turns a data target for the URL already current with a hash into a hash move for `navigate` and `visit` alone, and not when the call revalidates. A fill, a refresh, a pop, and a redirect never go through it.
149 [x] A deferred value JSON cannot hold stopped a client navigation's stream, and its rejection was unhandled
149.1 [x] Plan 21 2.1, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: server/navigation/response.ts wrote each settled deferred value with `encodeFrame` inside the promise's success handler. A bigint or a cycle threw there, the handler's promise rejected, `Promise.all` over them rejected, and the voided chain was an unhandled rejection, which stops a Node or Bun server by default; the body never wrote `d` or closed, so the client's request hung. The document path already projected the failure. Red: tests/integration/defer.test `unserializable deferred` through the real createServer(...).fetch timed out (the body never ended).
149.2 [x] Fix: a value that fails to encode is that key's `e` frame, projected as a deferred rejection; the other keys still stream, the body ends with `d`, and nothing rejects unhandled (the case listens for `unhandledRejection` and sees none).
150 [x] On Node, the Host header could change the routed path
150.1 [x] Plan 21 2.2, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: adapters/node/request.ts built the URL as `${scheme}://${host}${target}` from the raw Host header, so `Host: evil.example/admin?` made `GET /where` the URL `http://evil.example/admin?/where`, routed to /admin, past any check that looked at the request line. Red: tests/integration/adapters.test `node host` on a real node:http server: six hostile Host values (a path, a query, a fragment, userinfo, a space, a port then a path) were served instead of a 400.
150.2 [x] Fix: `toRequest` answers 400 unless Host is a host and an optional port: a name (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`), an IPv4 address, or a bracketed IPv6 one. A legitimate Host behind a proxy is unchanged: `example.com`, `example.com:8080`, `127.0.0.1:3000`, `[::1]:3000`, and `svc_name.internal` are served with the URL they name. dx/adapters.md says so.
150.3 [x] Bun too (flare-3c's check): Bun builds the Request URL from the Host header as sent, and a real Bun.serve over `createBunFetch` served `Host: evil.example/admin?` with 200 instead of a 400. Red: tests/integration/adapters.test `bun host` under the Bun runtime. Fix: the rule moves to shared/url `validHost`, used by Node's `toRequest` and by `createBunFetch`, which answers 400 before the server runs (serve.test `hostile host`). On Workers the URL is the edge's: tests/workerd/server.test `worker host` shows a hostile Host header leaves the routed path alone under Miniflare, with no change.
151 [x] One chunked POST past bodyLimit could stop the Node process
151.1 [x] Plan 21 7.1, from the second outside review: adapters/node/listener.ts fed the request body into a ReadableStream from `on("data")`. A server-function POST past `bodyLimit` answers 413 after `capped()` cancels that stream, but on a kept-alive connection Node goes on reading the body, and each later chunk's `controller.enqueue` into the cancelled stream threw a TypeError inside the event listener: an uncaught exception, which stops a Node server, unauthenticated. Red: tests/integration/adapters.test `node body limit` (a real node:http server, a chunked keep-alive upload of 4.8 KB against a 1 KB limit): the 413 came back and an uncaughtException TypeError was recorded.
151.2 [x] Fix: the body stream's `cancel` closes it to later chunks and resumes the request so the rest drains; `data` and `end` after it are ignored. The 413 stands, no exception escapes, and the next request is served.
152 [x] Coalesced store misses shared one visitor's Set-Cookie
152.1 [x] Plan 21 7.4, from the second outside review: concurrent misses of one stored key share one render (server/cache/inflight.ts), and server/cache/serve.ts `missed` served every request that joined it the render's `Set-Cookie`, so each got the first visitor's cookie: a session or visit id handed to strangers. Red: tests/integration/cache-modes.test `coalesced cookies`: four concurrent misses of an `isr` page whose `.headers()` sets a per-render cookie came back with one cookie value between them.
152.2 [x] Fix: only the request that started the render takes its cookies. A request that joined a render that set cookies renders its own, live; a render that set none is still shared. Each of the four now carries its own cookie, all `private, no-store`, and nothing is stored. dx/cache.md says so.
153 [x] Node's waitUntil kept every promise forever and left rejections unhandled
153.1 [x] Plan 21 7.2, from the second outside review: adapters/node/listener.ts added each `waitUntil` promise to a Set it never pruned and never caught. One failed background job, a store timeout in a stale refresh say, was an unhandled rejection, which stops a Node server by default; and every settled promise stayed in the Set with what it resolved to, so the heap grew with every stale hit. Red: tests/integration/adapters.test `node wait until` on a real node:http server, twenty requests each handing one rejecting and one resolving (1 MB payload) promise: twenty unhandled rejections.
153.2 [x] Fix: the listener releases each promise when it settles and logs a rejection as `[flare:node] waitUntil rejected`. Green: no unhandled rejection, twenty logged, and after a collection none of the twenty payloads is still reachable (a mutation that keeps settled promises turns the case red). dx/adapters.md says so.
154 [x] ArkType input validation was bypassed, and Effect's standard schemas rejected everything
154.1 [x] Plan 21 2.3, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: shared/validation/schema.ts `runValidator` tried `typeof validator === "function"` before `~standard`. An ArkType type is callable and a Standard Schema; called, it returns its errors as a value, so an invalid input passed as valid and reached the loader. Effect's `Schema.standardSchemaV1(...)` is callable too, and calling it threw for every input. Red: tests/integration/builder.test `input libraries` with the real arktype 2.2.3 and effect 3.21.4 behind `.input({ searchParams })` through the real createServer(...).fetch: `/ark?q=a` answered 200.
154.2 [x] Fix: a validator with `~standard` validates through it first, then `{ parse }`, then a plain function. Both libraries now answer 400 with `fieldErrors.q` for `?q=a` and render a valid `?q=long`. A plain Effect `Schema` has no `~standard` and is wrapped with `Schema.standardSchemaV1`; dx/server-fn.md says so.
155 [x] href() accepted a backslash or a tab after the leading slash, which a browser follows to another origin
155.1 [x] Plan 21 7.7 (the second review) and the link.md gap review (phase 6.1): shared/url/href.ts rejected only a literal `//`. A browser drops tab, CR, and LF anywhere in a URL and reads a backslash as a slash, so `/\evil.example`, a slash, a tab, and a slash, and `\\evil.example` all load `//evil.example`. Link's dangerous-href rule and `redirect({ href })` use the same check, so a Link rendered them as given. Red: tests/integration/link.test `hidden off-origin` (`href()` accepted five spellings, and the Link wrote them as its href).
155.2 [x] Fix: the `//` check runs on the value as a browser reads it, tabs, CRs, and LFs dropped and backslashes as slashes; each spelling throws, and a Link renders `#`. A backslash later in the path (`/a\b`) stays the app's. dx/link.md says so.
156 [x] buildUrl accepted a dot segment as a param value, which the browser resolves to another route
156.1 [x] Plan 21 7.6 (the second review): `encodeParam` encoded each character, and `.` stays `.`, so `{ id: ".." }` on `users/[id]` built `/users/..`, which a browser resolves to `/`. Encoding does not help: the URL parser counts `%2e` as a dot in a dot segment. Red: tests/integration/link.test `dot segment params` (buildUrl returned a URL for `.`, `..`, and their `%2e` spellings, and a Link rendered one).
156.2 [x] Fix: a param value, or a catch-all item, that is `.` or `..` in any spelling with `%2e` throws `param`, as a slash does; `...` and `a.b` are ordinary values. A Link with one is a 500, as a missing required param is. dx/link.md's URL values table says so.
157 [x] A plain Effect Schema passed to `.input()` failed every input with a quiet 400
157.1 [x] flare-3c's follow-up to 154: a plain Effect `Schema` has no `~standard` and no `parse` but is callable, so `runValidator` called it, the call threw for every value, and the route or server function answered 400 to all input with nothing saying why. Found with it: a run that answers 500 before any render, a throwing `authenticate` say, was answered bare and never projected, so the error was logged nowhere, in dev or production. Red against cc7755d0: tests/integration/builder.test `input plain effect` with the real effect 3.21.4 (a 400 and no log line).
157.2 [x] Fix: shared/validation/effect.ts `checkValidator` recognizes Effect's schema marker (`Symbol.for("effect/Schema")`) without `~standard` and throws `<owner>: a plain Effect Schema is not a validator; wrap it with Schema.standardSchemaV1(schema)`; `runValidator` calls it first on the server, with the route id or server function name as owner, and the input stage turns the throw into a 500 the answer projects. A bare 500 that carries a thrown error, this one or a throwing `authenticate`, is now projected in lifecycle/exchange.ts `bare`, on the document and data paths alike, so it is logged like any 500 (in dev with its message and stack; in production as its digest until plan 21 9.4). The check runs on the server only: a definition-time check in the shared route builder put 145 bytes on createPage's client bundle (tests/size, budget 1120), so it was dropped. dx/server-fn.md says so.
158 [x] In production a 500's error never reached the server log, only its digest
158.1 [x] Plan 21 9.4, found during BUGS 157: server/render/projector.ts logged `sink.log.error("render", digest)` and nothing else, against B76 as revised on 2026-10-13 (the server log contains the digest, the error's name, its message, and its stack). An operator holding a client's digest had no message or stack to read. dx/plugin.md still described the old rule, and projector.test `prod generic` asserted it. Red: tests/integration/builder.test `production error log` (a loader throwing a TypeError under DEV=false logged `[flare:render] <digest>` alone).
158.2 [x] Fix: the production branch logs the digest with the thrown stack, which opens with the name and message, or `name: message` for a thrown non-Error. The body is unchanged: the generic message and the digest. `prod generic` now asserts B76's log, and dx/plugin.md says it.
159 [x] A middleware path filter was bypassed by a locale prefix or the basePath
159.1 [x] Plan 21 12.1, from the third outside review: server/middleware/filters.ts matched a filter against the pathname as sent, so a middleware listed with `{ prefix: ["/admin"] }` that blocks the admin area did not run for `/hr/admin` under a `[[locale]]` root, which rendered with 200, and under `basePath: "/base"` never matched at all (`/base/admin`). Red: tests/integration/middleware.test `filter route path` through the real createServer(...).fetch: `/hr/admin` answered 200 past the block.
159.2 [x] Fix: the runner hands each filter the route path too, the pathname without the basePath and without a configured locale (any case) as its first segment, and a filter enters when either matches. `/admin`, `/hr/admin`, `/HR/admin/x`, and `/base/admin` are blocked; `/about` is not. dx/server.md says so.
160 [x] A custom head script could load from any URL through `extra.src`
160.1 [x] Plan 21 12.2, from the third outside review: shared/head/tags.ts `scriptTag` spread the allowed `extra` attributes onto the script, and `src` is an allowed name. Since BUGS 146 wrote `extra` on an inline script too, `{ children, extra: { src } }` became an external script carrying the request nonce, which any CSP that trusts the nonce runs; on an external script `extra.src` replaced its own `src`. Head data from a CMS or a loader could load any script. Red: tests/integration/head.test `custom extra url` through the real createServer(...).fetch: both scripts pointed at evil.example.
160.2 [x] Fix: a script's `extra` skips `nonce`, `src`, and `href`, any case. The inline script stays inline with its nonce, and the external one loads from its own `src`. dx/head.md says so.
161 [x] proxy() forwarded the client's Host, proxy credentials, and forwarding claims upstream
161.1 [x] Plan 21 12.9, from the third outside review: server/proxy/proxy.ts dropped only the hop-by-hop set, so the client's `Proxy-Authorization` and `Proxy-Authenticate`, its `Forwarded` and `X-Forwarded-*` claims, and its `X-Real-IP` reached the upstream, and on Bun its `Host` did too, picking the upstream's virtual host. v0's api-proxy dropped `host` and the proxy credentials; v1 lost them with no decision. Red: tests/integration/middleware.test `proxy client headers` against a real upstream: under Node, `proxy-authorization` arrived; under Bun, `Host: internal-admin.upstream.test` arrived.
161.2 [x] Fix, ported from v0: the hop-by-hop set gains `proxy-authenticate`, `proxy-authorization`, and `trailers`, and the request drops the client's `host`, `forwarded`, `x-forwarded-for`, `x-forwarded-host`, `x-forwarded-port`, `x-forwarded-proto`, and `x-real-ip`. The upstream sees its own host and none of those; other headers pass. Green under Node and Bun. dx/server.md says so.
162 [x] A private page could be edge-cached through surrogate and vendor CDN headers its route set
162.1 [x] Plan 21 12.10, from the third outside review: a rendered page dropped only `Cache-Control`, `CDN-Cache-Control`, and `Cache-Tag` from its route's `.headers()`, and `privately` deleted only `cdn-cache-control` and `cache-tag`. `Surrogate-Control`, `Surrogate-Key`, the Cloudflare, Vercel, and Netlify `CDN-Cache-Control` forms, Akamai's `Edge-Control`, and nginx's `X-Accel-Expires` passed through on a `private, no-store` answer, so a CDN that honors them stored one visitor's page. Red: tests/integration/cache-modes.test `private edge headers` through the real createServer(...).fetch: `surrogate-control: max-age=3600` arrived on the private document.
162.2 [x] Fix: server/lifecycle/policies.ts names every edge-cache header once; `privately` and the private branch of a mount or middleware exit drop them all, and `applyPolicy` drops them from a rendered answer whose decision is `no-store`. A public page keeps a route's surrogate key for its CDN's purge. dx/cache.md says so.
163 [x] A backslash path got a different answer on each runtime, so a path rule could be walked around
163.1 [x] Plan 21 12.8, from the third outside review: Node's `toRequest` answered 400 for any backslash, as B22 said, but Bun and Workers parse the request URL with WHATWG's rules before Flare runs and hand it over with no backslash left. Measured with raw request lines: Bun 1.4.3 gives `/\admin` as `//admin` (a 400 by B22's `//` rule) and `/a\b` as `/a/b` (served); workerd under Miniflare the same. So `/a\b` was a 400 on Node and `/a/b` on Bun and Workers, and a raw-path rule in front of Flare matched neither. Red: tests/integration/adapters.test `node backslash` (a 400 for `/a\b`); `bun backslash` and tests/workerd/server.test `worker backslash` passed as measured.
163.2 [x] Fix, as flare-3c ruled: Node reads a backslash in the path, before `?` or `#`, as `/`, then applies the other rules, so `/a\b` is `/a/b` and `/\a/b` is a 400 on all three runtimes. Decision edit: B22 now reads a backslash as `/` and cites the three measurements. dx/adapters.md says so, and dx/server.md's security section warns that a raw-path rule in a proxy or WAF in front of Flare is bypassable with a backslash: enforce path rules in Flare middleware.
164 [x] The dev image endpoint served a file outside the project through a symlink
164.1 [x] Plan 21 16.4, from the v0 comparison: features/image/vite/dev-endpoint.ts checked containment on the lexical path only (`resolve` and `relative`), so a symlink inside the project, to a file or a directory outside it, passed the check and `sharp` read the file it pointed to. v0 resolved the path with realpath first. Red: dev-endpoint.test `symlink escape`, the real endpoint behind the dev guard: `src=/src/link.png`, a symlink to a PNG outside the root, answered 200.
164.2 [x] Fix: after the lexical check, the file and the root are resolved with realpath and the containment check runs again on those; a symlink that leads out is 400, a missing file or a directory 404, and the real path is what `sharp` reads. A symlink that stays inside (`alias.png`) is still served. dx/features.md says so.
165 [x] A route folder named with a newline broke the generated route module
165.1 [x] Plan 21 16.6, from the v0 comparison: vite/codegen/escape.ts `escapeSource` escaped only the backslash and the double quote, and every generated string literal went through it or through a bare `JSON.stringify`. A folder name holding a newline ended the string mid-line, and U+2028 and U+2029, which `JSON.stringify` leaves raw, rode through unescaped; v0 wrote every literal through one JSON-based writer. Red: vite/codegen/routes.test `hostile names`: the routes module for folders named with a newline, U+2028, and U+2029 failed to parse with oxc.
165.2 [x] Fix: escape.ts `codeLiteral` is the one writer: `JSON.stringify` with U+2028, U+2029, and `</` escaped; `escapeSource` is its body without the quotes, so every template that writes its own quotes uses it, and the route record's `client` and `intercept` values and closures.ts' chunk URLs use `codeLiteral`. The module parses and each name reads back exactly (escape.test `hostile text`). A param name is already an identifier. dx/routes.md says so.
166 [x] A cached GET server function sent its errors and cookies as public
166.1 [x] Plan 21 2.5, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: server/server-fn/cache-control.ts wrote `public, max-age` on every answer of a GET server function with `cache.maxAge`, whatever its status or headers, so a CDN could keep a 400 for one visitor's bad input, a thrown `notFound()` 404 or an error 500, and a returned `Response` carrying one visitor's `Set-Cookie`, and serve them to everyone for the max-age. Red: tests/integration/server-fn.test `cache errors private` through the real createServer(...).fetch: the 400 came back `public, max-age=60`.
166.2 [x] Fix: `setCacheControl` takes the status, and only a 2xx answer with no `Set-Cookie` is public; every other answer of a cached function is `private, no-store`. The success case stays `public, max-age=60`. dx/server-fn.md says so.
167 [x] Text or a script holding `self.flare=` was taken for the state script
167.1 [x] Plan 21 2.6, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: server/cache/nonce.ts found the state payload with `(?<=>self\.flare=)` and swapped the nonce in the first match, and client/boot/hydrate.ts `readState` took the first script whose text held `self.flare=`. An app's inline head script starting `self.flare=`, which comes before the state, took the stored copy's nonce swap, so a hit replayed the state with the miss's nonce in it, and the boot would read the app's script as the state; a non-JSON one would have thrown during the store write. Red: tests/integration/cache-modes.test `state text` (the hit's state nonce was not the hit's) and client/boot/hydrate.test `state among lookalikes` (readState read the app's script).
167.2 [x] Fix: the state script is `<script id="flare-state">`; the stored copy's nonce rewrite and `readState` take the last script with that id, which follows the page's content, so a lookalike or even a forged id earlier in the page is left alone. 02-system says so.
168 [x] A CR or LF from the request made server.fetch reject instead of answering
168.1 [x] Plan 21 2.7, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: a `.headers()` value built from the request with a CR or LF made `Headers.append` throw while the answer was written, and a `redirect({ href })` to such a value did the same at the Location header, so `server.fetch` rejected and an adapter had nothing to send. Red: tests/integration/hostile.test `hostile values answer` through the real createServer(...).fetch: `/split?v=a%0D%0Aset-cookie:...` rejected with `TypeError: Headers.append`. The audit's third input, a malformed escape in the locale slot, already answers below 500 with and without the i18n middleware (B81 keeps a param encoded); the case keeps it as a guard.
168.2 [x] Fix: route-steps writes each match's `.headers()` values into a `Headers` inside the step that catches a `.headers()` throw, so a value it cannot hold is that match's bug (B82): its headers are omitted and the page answers 200, no header split. `redirect({ href })` refuses a control character, so the loader fails and the answer is the 500 page. dx/builder.md and dx/link.md say so.
169 [x] Server code reached the client graph through route shapes the split did not see, and through loader helpers
169.1 [x] Plan 21 2.4, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: vite/boundary/split.ts read a route chain only as `export const name = createPage(…)…` with the builder imported by name. A chain through the namespace import (`flare.createPage(…)`), an `export { route }` of a local chain, and a chain bound to a variable went to the client whole, loader and all, and the tripwire, which reads chunks with the same walk, missed them. A module-scope loader helper stayed after its `.loader(helper)` was blanked, so the server modules it imported stayed in the client graph and ran there on import. Red: boundary/split.test `route shapes` (the namespace chain was not split; the other shapes passed silently) and `dead helpers` (the helper and its server import stayed), and tripwire.test `loader helper` (a real Vite client build held the server module's code) and `route method` (the namespace chain's loader passed the tripwire).
169.2 [x] Fix: split.ts reads the builders through named and namespace imports, and every builder root call must be a direct export chain; any other call, a builder or namespace read as a value, and a local binding that shadows one are build errors naming the file, as createServerFn's are (B85). After the split, reads.ts `deadDeclarations` finds the module-scope declarations only removed code reached, through other declarations too, and pruneImports removes them before it prunes the imports; a declaration nothing read before the split stays, and so does an exported one. The same pruning covers a server function's handler helper and, in the server graph, a `.client()` body's helper (transform.test `ssr prunes client import`). dx/routes.md says so.
170 [x] A hung loader on a store miss blocked its page for every later visitor until restart
170.1 [x] Plan 21 7.5, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: server/cache/inflight.ts held a key's slot until the flight's render and store write settled, with no bound, so a loader or a `store.set` that never settled kept the slot forever, and every later miss of that page joined the hung flight and waited with it. v0 bounded its background render at 30 s. Red: inflight.test `deadline` (a joiner still waited past 30 s, and the next miss did not render) and cache-modes.test `hung miss` through the real createServer(...).fetch (a request that joined the hung miss timed out).
170.2 [x] Fix: a flight's slot has a 30 s deadline. Past it the slot frees, a joiner gets nothing to serve and renders live (`private, no-store`, as for any flight that could not serve), and the next miss starts a new render and store write. The request that started the render keeps waiting on its own. dx/cache.md says so.
171 [x] An input key named `constructor` broke the 400's fieldErrors
171.1 [x] Plan 21 8.10, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: shared/validation/issues.ts built `fieldErrors` on a plain object, so an issue whose first path key was `constructor` read the inherited `Object` function as its list and threw `list.push is not a function`. The throw surfaced as the 400's only issue, with that internal message and empty `fieldErrors`, and a `__proto__` key would have set the object's prototype. Red: server-fn.test `prototype keys` through the real createServer(...).fetch with a zod record (the 400 held `list.push is not a function` and no fields) and issues.test `prototype keys`.
171.2 [x] Fix: the lists grow in a Map and `Object.fromEntries` writes them, so every key, `constructor` and `__proto__` included, is an own field. zod skips a `__proto__` key itself. zod joins core's devDependencies for the case. dx/server-fn.md says so.
172 [x] A signed-in visitor's cached pages showed to the next visitor in the same tab
172.1 [x] Plan 21 2.8, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: the client match cache, the held prefetches, and the query client kept one visitor's data across a sign-out and sign-in in the same tab, and with the page fresh no request was sent, so user B saw user A's page; there was no client API to drop them. Red: tests/integration/cache-client.test `cookie forgets` (a fresh page stayed held after a server function wrote a cookie: the loader ran 0 times, not 1) and `cookie forgets prefetch` (the click took the prefetched landing), server-fn.test `cookie signal` (no signal, and a handler's forged `flare-cookies` passed through), and unit cases for the call, the match cache, the prefetcher, the query getter, and the root export.
172.2 [x] Fix, B133 (the reviewer's ruling): a server function whose handler returns a `Response` with `Set-Cookie` answers `flare-cookies: set`; a middleware's or authenticate's cookie never does, and the handler's own copy is dropped. The client call drops every match-cache entry, every held prefetch, and the query client's cache (`clear()`) before the answer is used. `invalidate()`, a new root export beside `navigate`, does the same and refetches the page on screen. A middleware-refreshed session cookie on data answers keeps the cache (`cookie forgets`). dx/navigate.md, dx/server-fn.md, dx/cache.md, dx/features.md, and 03-surface say so.
173 [x] A namespace-imported createIsomorphicFn kept its server body in the client graph
173.1 [x] Plan 21 13.15, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: vite/boundary/env.ts walked an isomorphic chain from its outermost call down through member calls, and `flare.createIsomorphicFn()` is a member call itself, so the walk passed the helper's own call and never matched the chain: the client graph kept `.server()`'s body and the server graph `.client()`'s, and the tripwire, which reads chunks with the same walk, let it through. The namespace read as a value, `const { createIsomorphicFn } = flare`, also escaped every check. Red: env.test `iso namespace` (the client graph held the server body) and tripwire.test `iso server body` (a real Vite client build held it).
173.2 [x] Fix: the walk stops at the helper's own call, named or namespaced. Reading the namespace other than as `ns.name`, outside a type position, is a build error naming the file in both graphs; the route split (BUGS.md 169) reports the same shape with the same message. dx/env.md says so.
174 [x] A render error after the shell was stored as a success, and never logged
174.1 [x] Plan 21 3.2, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: a `<Loading>` fragment that failed after the shell is contained by Solid itself, which streams a rejection for the client to render again and reports it only to the stream's `onError` hook, and server/render/document.ts ignored that hook. The shared render saw a complete body, so the page was stored and every visitor replayed the failed fragment for the whole ttl (24 h for `isr: true`), and production logged nothing. Red: server/cache/write.test `late render error not stored` through the real createServer(...).fetch (the store was written, and nothing was logged).
174.2 [x] Fix: the stream's `onError` passes a fragment that rejected after the shell, Solid's `client` handling, to the document's `onRenderError`; a failed render is the slot boundary's to report before the shell, so it is logged once (stream.test `dev before shell`, which the first version of this fix broke by logging it twice), and a value Solid could not serialize is no failed render. The hook lives in server/render/stream-options.ts (stream-options.test `render errors`). A shared render with such an error gives nothing to store, so the visitor gets the live render, and a live answer logs it through projectError (the digest, plus the stack in production, B158). dx/cache.md says so.
175 [x] A failing store answered 500 on every stored page, a hung one hung them, and a corrupt record never healed
175.1 [x] Plan 21 3.3, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: server/cache/epoch.ts `lookup` had no guard and no deadline, so a store whose `get` threw made `server.fetch` reject on every stored page, one whose `get` never settled held every such request open, and a record whose `tags` were no list threw the same way on every request until its ttl, while one whose `storedAt` was no number aged as NaN and was served as fresh forever. Red: tests/integration/cache-modes.test `store read fails` (the fetch rejected with the store's error) and `corrupt record heals` (`(entry.tags ?? []).map is not a function`).
175.2 [x] Fix: a stored page reads through epoch.ts `readStored`: a read that throws, or does not answer within 2 s, is logged at error level and the page renders live, `private, no-store`, with no shared render and no write. `lookup` checks the record's envelope, an object with a finite `storedAt` and string `tags` if any; a corrupt one is logged and read as a miss, so the miss writes a good record over it. dx/store.md says so.
176 [x] A server-only deploy kept the build id, so it served the previous deploy's stored pages
176.1 [x] Plan 21 3.1, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: vite/plugin/build-id.ts hashed only the client manifest's URLs (B84), so a deploy that changed only server code, a loader, a render, or a server function, kept its build id, and with it every store key: the new server served pages the old one had rendered, with the old data shape, until their ttl. Red: vite/plugin/build-id.test `server output` (two real builds that differ only in a page's render had one id).
176.2 [x] Fix, B84 revised (the reviewer's ruling of 2026-10-16): the id is sha256 over the client manifest's URLs plus each server JS chunk's file name and final code, never a sourcemap. Both server builds write the 12-character placeholder `__FLARE_ID__`; a post `generateBundle` in the ssr build hashes its final chunks and writes the id over the placeholder, and the prerender build after it writes the same id. The same fixture built in two directories has one id (`server output`); a built copy of the docs app holds no absolute path and no placeholder. B84, dx/plugin.md, and dx/store.md say so.
176.3 [x] The first version stamped every ssr bundle, so an ssr bundle with no build info module, made while the client manifest was never written, read the missing manifest and failed (vite/boundary/tripwire.test `clean`, vite/plugin/environments.test `urls agree` and `css server only`, found by the Mac gate run). The plugin now stamps only a bundle that holds the placeholder.
177 [x] Node answered 400 for a URL in the query string, which Bun served
177.1 [x] Plan 21 3.14, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: adapters/node/request.ts refused any target holding `://` anywhere, meant for the absolute form `http://evil/`, so `/login?next=https://example.com/x` was a 400 on Node and served on Bun and Workers. Red: tests/integration/adapters.test `node backslash` with that target (a 400 where Bun served it).
177.2 [x] Fix: a target must be in origin form, starting with `/` but not `//`; the absolute form is still a 400, and the query is plain text. `bun backslash` serves the same target under Bun. dx/adapters.md says so.
178 [x] Blocked sessionStorage stopped hydration
178.1 [x] Plan 21 3.6, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: client/boot/create-client.ts handed the scroll store `window.sessionStorage` at boot, and with cookies off or in a sandboxed iframe that read throws a SecurityError, so the boot threw and the page never hydrated. Red: tests/integration/client.test `storage blocked` in each engine, with the getter made to throw before the page loads (the page never hydrated).
178.2 [x] Fix: the scroll store takes the storage as `() => Storage`, read inside its existing guards each time it saves or restores, as the reload guard already did, so positions are not kept and the page hydrates and navigates. dx/navigate.md says so.
179 [x] Bun on macOS formats Arabic numbers with Arabic-Indic digits, and an e2e case asserted the digits
179.1 [x] Found by the Mac gate run of 2026-10-16 (a9ef10cf): tests/e2e/docs-features `format message` failed on bun-dev-chromium and bun-dev-firefox only. Measured with the exact calls the case makes: `new Intl.NumberFormat("ar").format(14)` is `١٤` (numbering system `arab`) under Bun 1.4.3 on macOS 14.6, which uses the system's ICU, and `14` (`latn`) under Node 24 on the same Mac and under Node 26 and Bun 1.4.3 on Linux; `new Intl.PluralRules("ar").select(14)` is `many` on all four, and `en` is `14` and `other` on all four. So the server render under Bun on macOS writes `١٤ releases (many)`. That is the runtime's Intl data, which a Flare app under Bun on macOS meets too; the case's own comment says the digits prove nothing (B29), yet it asserted `14`.
179.2 [x] Fix: the case asserts the plural category with any decimal digits (`\p{Nd}+ releases (many)`); the English line keeps `14`. dx/features.md says numbers follow the rendering runtime's Intl data, naming Bun on macOS.
180 [x] A HEAD request left its page's render running
180.1 [x] Plan 21 3.12, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: server/lifecycle/methods.ts answered a HEAD with the document's status and headers and an empty body, and dropped the document's body unread, so the render behind it was never cancelled: a page with a slow or unending `<Loading>` value kept its render, and its request's resources, alive with no reader. Red: tests/integration/hostile.test `head stops the render` through the real createServer(...).fetch (the page's cleanup never ran).
180.2 [x] Fix: the HEAD answer cancels the dropped body, which stops the render (renderStop) and runs its cleanups. dx/server.md says so.
181 [x] nonce() threw in a component that rendered after an async boundary
181.1 [x] Plan 21 3.13, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: shared/csp/nonce.ts read the render's event from a slot set only during the render's synchronous pass, so a component inside `<Loading>` whose value came after the shell ran with the slot empty and `nonce()` threw `nonce`: the fragment failed and the client rendered it again. `placeStylesheet` and `devStyles` read the same slot. Red: tests/integration/nonce.test `after loading` through the real createServer(...).fetch (the fragment rejected with `Error: nonce`, which BUGS.md 174's logging now reports).
181.2 [x] Fix: `withRequest` also keeps the render's event on Solid's request event, which a late fragment still renders under, and the readers take the synchronous slot first, then that. dx/server.md says so.
182 [x] A redirect during a client navigation overwrote the page it left, and a redirect loop never stopped
182.1 [x] Plan 21 3.10, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: client/navigation/navigate.ts followed an `x` frame with the frame's `replace`, which `redirect()` defaults to `true`, and Flare writes history only when a navigation commits, so a push to `/a` that redirected to `/b` replaced the page the user came from: Back skipped it. A Back step that redirected to the page on screen counted as a same-URL revalidation and wrote nothing, leaving the stepped-to URL in the bar. Nothing counted the follows, so two loaders redirecting to each other looped forever. Red: client/navigation/navigate-redirect.test `redirect keeps the push` (no entry was added), `redirect keeps the replace` (the bar kept `/a`), and `redirect loop` (51 requests, stopped only by the test's guard).
182.2 [x] Fix: the followed navigation takes the entry the requested one would have taken, a push for a push and a replace for a replace or a pop, and a followed redirect always writes its entry. The frame's `replace` still applies to a server function's redirect. A navigation follows at most 10 redirects, then loads the 11th as a document. dx/builder.md says so.
183 [x] useBlocker's unload prompt was never attached, so leaving the document never asked
183.1 [x] Plan 21 3.5, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: client/navigation/blocker.ts had `beforeUnload`, which B84 specifies, but nothing added it to the window, so a reload, a tab close, and a link that loads a document left without asking even while a blocker refused. Red: tests/integration/client.test `blocker unload` in each engine (a `beforeunload` on a page whose blocker returns `false` was not cancelled).
183.2 [x] Fix: the client's listeners add a `beforeunload` listener that asks the last blocker with `next` equal to `current`, and the client's dispose removes it (client/boot/leave.test `blocker unload`). The window's scroll store moved from create-client.ts to client/navigation/scroll.ts `browserScroll`, keeping create-client.ts under its line limit. dx/navigate.md says so.
184 [x] `[[locale]]` swallowed ordinary words: /pricing/x read the locale `pricing`, 302ed, and overwrote the locale cookie
184.1 [x] Plan 21 3.8, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: the router's locale param accepted any segment `Intl.Locale` accepts (B73's locale-like rule), and `pricing` is a well-formed language subtag, so under `[[locale]]/[topic]` the path /pricing/x matched with locale `pricing`, and the slot's "optional strip" redirected it to /x with a `flare.locale=en` cookie. Red: tests/integration/i18n.test `locales` through the real createServer(...).fetch (`/pricing/x` answered 302 to `/x` with the cookie).
184.2 [x] Fix, B73 revised (the reviewer's ruling of 2026-10-16): the locale param matches only a configured tag, in any case, so the wrong-case 301 stays; any other segment is an ordinary path segment, and the strip redirect is gone, as Next.js treats locale prefixes. A tag the app does not list is a 404 unless a route matches it. Since the router now does the filtering, `createServer({ locale })` throws unless the router's own `locale` has the same tags, default, and param name (my call while the reviewer was busy; locale-slot.test `router locale`; BUGS.md 185 makes the router the one source). B73, the bot-detection row, dx/features.md, and dx/router.md say so.
184.3 [x] Follow-up found by the Mac e2e on 2a874cd1 (12 failed of 2127, every one this case): e2e docs-features `unknown locale` still expected `/fr` to 302 to `/`. Under the revised B73 `/fr` is an ordinary path no route matches, so the case now expects the app's 404 at `/fr` with no redirect, and bits/13's row says so. I had not run the e2e grid before reporting 184; the unit and integration gates cannot see it.
185 [x] The locale config was stated twice, on the router and on `i18n()`, which invites the two to disagree
185.1 [x] Follow-up to BUGS.md 184, the reviewer's ruling of 2026-10-17: 184 made `createServer` throw when the router's `locale` and `i18n()` disagreed, but an app still had to write the tags, the default, and the param name in both places. And 184 broke a server whose router had no `locale`: server/cache/prerender.test `bare path` built one (`createServer: locale needs the router's own locale`). Red: server/lifecycle/locale-slot.test `router locale` (a slot with no fields threw) and tests/integration/i18n.test `router config` (`i18n()` with no fields threw at the call).
185.2 [x] Fix: the router's `locale` is the one source. `i18n()` takes every field as optional; the slot builds its config per request from `router.locale` plus its own `botDetection`. `createServer` throws without a router `locale`, and throws naming the field when `i18n()` was given `locales`, `defaultLocale`, `paramName`, or `cookieName` that differ. Prerender's bare locale URLs read the router's config. B73, dx/features.md, and dx/router.md say so.
186 [x] An in-flight render outlived a purge, so a request after a mutation could be served the data from before it
186.1 [x] Plan 21 3.4, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: server/cache/inflight.ts kept a key's slot through a `revalidate()`, so a request that arrived after a mutation and its purge joined a shared render that had read the data before the mutation, and was answered with it; there was no read-your-writes. Red: tests/integration/cache-modes.test `purge drops the flight` through the real createServer(...).fetch (the request after the purge got `version 1`).
186.2 [x] Fix: a purge drops the slots it may affect, each purged key's, and every slot on a tag purge, since a render's tags are known only when it is done; the next request starts its own render. A dropped render still writes, and the purge's key and tag epochs make that write a miss. A slot's own cleanup deletes the slot only while it is still that flight's. The other half of 3.4, a store write that never settles, is BUGS.md 170's deadline. dx/cache.md says so.
187 [x] basePath was ignored by Link, navigate, and setLocale, so client navigations left the base and 404ed
187.1 [x] Plan 21 3.7, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: a `Link`'s `href` and `navigate()`'s target were `buildUrl` of the route id, the route's path without the router's `basePath`, and `setLocale` navigates through `navigate()`. Under `basePath: "/app"` the client asked for `/about?_flare=data` outside the base, the router matched nothing, and the navigation never landed. Red: tests/integration/basepath-client.test `base path navigation` in each engine (no navigation landed under `/app`).
187.2 [x] Fix: `resolveTarget` and the `Link`'s `href` put the route's path under the router's base, through shared/url/prefix.ts `underBase` (BUGS.md 188's `routeHref` now), which prerender's own copy now shares. The three navigations ask for `/app/about?_flare=data`, `/app/home?_flare=data`, and `/app/hr/home?_flare=data`. `buildUrl` stays the path without the base. dx/router.md and dx/link.md say so.
188 [x] `trailingSlash: "always"` broke prerender, and links, navigations, and the sitemap lacked the slash
188.1 [x] Plan 21 3.9, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: the router's `trailingSlash` applied only to the canonical redirect, while prerender asked for `/p/a`, so under `always` its request met the 301 and captured nothing, and a `Link`'s `href`, `navigate()`'s target, and the prerendered paths the sitemap lists all lacked the slash. Red: server/cache/prerender.test `render` (`{ status: 301, path: "/p/a" }`), client/navigation/target.test `trailing slash`, and shared/components/link.test `base path` (`/about?q=1`).
188.2 [x] Fix: shared/url/prefix.ts `routeHref` writes a route's URL as the router does, under its base and with its trailing-slash rule on the path only, and the `Link`, `resolveTarget`, and prerender, which feeds the sitemap, all use it; it replaces BUGS.md 187's `underBase`. dx/router.md says so.
189 [x] Dev-only client code shipped in production bundles
189.1 [x] Plan 21 3.17, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: client/boot/create-client.ts chose its dev paths at run time, `env.dev` and whether a hot channel exists, so the dev tab module (client/dev/tab.ts, its `flare:tab` announcement), the hot channel's `flare:error` sender, and the overlay's error payload were in every production client bundle. The size suite could not see it: Vite reads production from NODE_ENV, which vitest sets to `test`, so its "production" bundles were built with `import.meta.env.DEV` true. Red: tests/size/size.test `client without dev` (the minified createClient bundle held `flare:tab`).
189.2 [x] Fix: the dev tab, the hot channel's sender (`devSend`), and the overlay report sit behind `import.meta.env.DEV`, which a production bundle replaces with false, so the bundler drops them; client/boot/production.test covers the DEV-false paths. tests/size/breakdown.ts builds with NODE_ENV `production`, so the size suite measures what ships.
190 [x] A shallow or hash navigation made during an in-flight fill was reverted when the fill committed
190.1 [x] Plan 21 3.11, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: a fill revalidates the page's URL as it was when the fill began, and its commit wrote that URL's location and search into the store, so a shallow or hash move made while the fill was open lost its location and the page's search, while the address bar kept the move's URL. Red: client/navigation/navigate-fill.test `later move wins` (the store's location went back to `/about`).
190.2 [x] Fix: a fill's commit keeps the page as it is when the values land, on the same path: the store's location and each match's search stay the later move's, and the fill only settles the values (client/navigation/request.ts `onPage`). A data navigation still aborts the fill as before. dx/defer.md says so.
191 [x] An image's EXIF orientation was ignored, so portrait phone photos came out sideways
191.1 [x] Plan 21 3.16, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: features/image/vite/optimize.ts and the dev endpoint resized the stored pixels and dropped the metadata, so a photo stored landscape with EXIF orientation 6, as a phone writes a portrait shot, became a landscape webp, and its binding's width and height were the stored ones. Red: features/image/vite/optimize.test `exif orientation` (the intrinsic size was 1000x500 for a photo that shows 500x1000).
191.2 [x] Fix: sharp's `autoOrient()` runs before the resize in the plugin and the dev endpoint, and the intrinsic size is `metadata().autoOrient`. The variants and the binding are upright, and no orientation tag is left. dx/plugin.md says so.
192 [x] A loader's prefetched queries were dropped on a client navigation, so the page queried again from the browser
192.1 [x] Plan 21 3.15, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: only the document state carried `queries` (B83); a data or prefetch answer had no query frame, so a loader's `ctx.queryClient.prefetchQuery` ran on the server for nothing, and the page's `useSuspenseQuery` ran its query again in the browser. Red: tests/integration/query.test `data queries` (the data answer had no `q` frame) and tests/integration/query-client.test `click navigation` and `hover then click` in each engine (with the commit's hydrate taken out, the page never showed the server's result).
192.2 [x] Fix, B134 (the reviewer's ruling, with its three conditions): a data or prefetch answer writes the request's dehydrated query cache as one `q` frame before `r` (server/render/state.ts `answerQueries`, the stored page's NDJSON too); the client reads it into the landing and hydrates it through the registered query getter's new `hydrate` at the navigation's commit, in the view transition's update after the abort check and in an overlay's commit, so a superseded navigation and an untaken prefetch leave the cache alone (client/navigation/navigate-queries.test). TanStack's hydrate keeps a query whose client data is newer (getter.test `hydrate keeps newer`). 02-system's frame table, B134, and dx/features.md say so.
192.3 [x] Follow-up found by the Mac e2e on 9199b16a (7 failed of 2127, every production project): e2e docs-navigation `pending read` held the docs page's feedback read to exercise a commit with a read pending (B106), but a stored SSG page's data now carries the queries its render resolved, the tally among them (B134), so on a production build the read was never pending and the skeleton never showed. Bisected on the Mac: 133a4ccc passes, b4514510 fails. The test drops the data answer's `q` frame, so the read is pending again under its control; dev, whose data requests render nothing, was unaffected.
192.4 [x] Follow-up found by the Mac e2e on df01e87f (3 failed, node-dev-chromium, node-dev-firefox, bun-dev-chromium): the frame filter 192.3 added fetches each data and prefetch answer itself, and on dev a hover's prefetch of `/hr/docs/middleware` was still in it when `pending read` ended, so its `route.fetch` failed with `Test ended` and Playwright charged that to the next case in the worker, `back keeps scroll`. `pending read` now ends with `page.unrouteAll({ behavior: "ignoreErrors" })`, Playwright's advice for routes in flight.
193 [x] An emoji in a path param made buildUrl throw, so a Link crashed its render
193.1 [x] Plan 21 8.2, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: shared/url/href.ts `encodeParam` walked the param by UTF-16 code unit, so a character outside the BMP was split into its two surrogates, and `encodeURIComponent` threw `URIError: URI malformed` on each half: a `Link` to a user named with an emoji crashed the render. Red: shared/url/href.test `emoji param` (`URIError`).
193.2 [x] Fix: the param is walked by code point, so the pair is encoded whole (`%F0%9F%98%80`) and round-trips; a lone surrogate, which no URL can carry, throws `param` like any other bad param, never a URIError. `String.prototype.isWellFormed` is above the browser floor, so the check is `encodeURIComponent`'s own. dx/link.md says so.
194 [x] A rewrite applied to the server-function path, so a locale rewrite broke every server function
194.1 [x] Plan 21 8.4, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: server/lifecycle/canonical-redirect.ts exempted `<basePath>/_flare/fn/` from the trailing-slash and case rules but ran the app's `rewrite` on it, so a rewrite that prefixes paths with a locale answered every server-function POST with a 308 to `/en/_flare/fn/<id>`, which matches no endpoint. Red: tests/integration/server-fn.test `rewrite skips functions` (a 308).
194.2 [x] Fix: the rewrite step skips a server-function path, as the other canonical rules do. dx/server.md says so.
195 [x] An X-Forwarded-Proto list or uppercase value behind a proxy made every server-function POST a 403
195.1 [x] Plan 21 8.9, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: shared/url/forwarded.ts `forwardedHttps` compared the whole header with `https`, so behind a proxy chain that lists one scheme per hop (`https, http`), or one that writes `HTTPS`, the request read as http, its origin was `http://…`, and the server function's origin check refused every POST from the https page. Red: tests/integration/adapters.test `node trust proxy` (`https, http` read as `http:`).
195.2 [x] Fix: with a trusted hop, the first entry of the list, the client's scheme, decides, in any case, as Express reads `X-Forwarded-Proto`. Node and Bun share the function; the case runs under both. dx/adapters.md says so.
196 [x] Every .ts file was parsed as TSX, so a generic arrow in a .ts route stopped dev and failed the production bundle
196.1 [x] Plan 21 8.1, from the 2026-10-16 gap audit: the boundary transform (split.ts, transform.ts), the server-function extractor, and codegen's route scan and types writer all parsed with `lang: "tsx"`, and TSX reads `<T>(value: T) => value` as a tag, so a `.ts` route, server function, or app module with a generic arrow failed with `Unexpected token`. Red: boundary/transform.test, codegen/scan.test, route-meta/extract.test, and codegen/types-gen.test `ts generic` (each threw the parse error).
196.2 [x] Fix: vite/route-meta/lang.ts `langOf` picks the grammar by extension, the query left out: TypeScript for `.ts`, `.mts`, `.cts`; TSX for `.tsx`; JavaScript or JSX for theirs; TSX for anything else. Every parse site of an app module uses it; the tripwire keeps `js` for emitted chunks.
197 [x] A gzip Response from `fetch()`, returned as-is, reached the client with its upstream Content-Encoding over a body fetch had decoded
197.1 [x] Plan 21 8.8: a `.response()` page, a server function, a middleware, or a mount that returned `await fetch(upstream)` passed the upstream's `Content-Encoding: gzip` and compressed `Content-Length` on with the body the runtime's fetch had already decoded. Node wrote a length the body overran and the next read failed to parse; Bun sent plain bytes labeled gzip; workerd gzipped them again for a client that sent no Accept-Encoding. proxy.ts already dropped both. Red: tests/integration/fetched.test.ts `node fetched` (a parse error) and `bun fetched` (`gzip` on four answers), and tests/workerd/server.test.ts `worker fetched` (gzip bytes).
197.2 [x] Fix: lifecycle/policies.ts `decodedHeaders` drops `Content-Encoding` and `Content-Length` from a Response whose `url` is set, which only `fetch()` gives, when it has an encoding. `answered`, server-function `passThrough`, and `exitHeaders` (the middleware and mount exit) use it. A Response the app built keeps its encoding. Measured decode sets: Node every coding, Bun each single coding, workerd gzip and br; each asks only for those by default, so the rule holds unless the app forwards the client's Accept-Encoding (dx/server.md).
198 [x] Bun's 10 s idle timeout cut a stream that went quiet: a slow deferred value or a server function's `.stream()` ended in ECONNRESET
198.1 [x] Plan 21 8.3: Bun.serve closes a socket idle for `idleTimeout` (10 s by default), a response in flight included, so a streamed body with a gap longer than that was cut mid-body. Measured on Bun 1.4.3: a 12 s gap under the default is ECONNRESET, a 5 s gap under `idleTimeout: 1` too (Bun checks every few seconds); a handler slow before its first byte is not cut. `server.timeout(request, 0)` alone keeps the stream, but the idle keep-alive socket then never closes (still open at 25 s, against 12 s), so the timeout is set back when the body ends: then it closes 12 s after. Red: tests/integration/adapters.test.ts `bun idle stream` (the body stopped at `one`), and serve.test `idle timeout` (createBunFetch took only trustProxy).
198.2 [x] Fix: finalize records the answers whose body is a stream (`streamsBody`), and createBunFetch's `fetch(request, server)` lifts the request's timeout while such a body is sent and restores `idleTimeout` in the body's flush. A body that is not a stream keeps the timeout and Bun's fast path. createBunFetch takes `idleTimeout` (0 to 255, Bun's 10 by default) and returns it for Bun.serve, so the restored value is the server's. `rebodied` in finalize.ts builds the rewrapped answer, so `new Response` stays in finalize (funnel-only).
199 [x] A cached GET server function answer from the previous build forced a reload after every deploy, for the whole max-age
199.1 [x] Plan 21 8.5: a GET server function with `.cache({ maxAge })` answers `public, max-age=N`, and the URL held only `_i`, so the browser's cache or a CDN served the previous build's answer to the new client; its `flare-build` differed, so the call reloaded the page and rejected, and the reloaded page read the same cached answer until it expired. Red: client/server-fn/call.test `cached across a deploy` (a URL-keyed cache: "The app was updated. Reloading the page").
199.2 [x] Fix, decision B43 revised: the client sends its build id as `_b` beside `_i` (`getQuery` in shared/protocol/get-codec.ts, `fnRequest` takes `{ basePath, build }`), so each build has its own cache key; dev has no build and sends none. The server accepts `_b` once, in any order, and ignores its value. Chosen over a revalidating retry (`cache: "no-cache"`), which costs a second request and misses a CDN that ignores a request's Cache-Control. Tests: codec.test and get-codec.test `build param`, server-fn integration `wire limits`.
200 [x] A redirect a server function threw after a mutation showed its target's data from before the mutation
200.1 [x] Plan 21 8.6: the client followed a server function's same-origin redirect with `visit(url, replace)`, a plain navigation, so a target held inside `staleTime`, or a held prefetch of it, came back from the client cache and the loader never saw the write. Red: tests/integration/cache-client.test.ts `redirect refetches` on every engine (`/kept`, held for 10 minutes, ran 0 more times after the redirect, 1 expected), and navigate-cache.test `visit revalidates` (2 requests, 3 expected).
200.2 [x] Fix, decision B85 revised: `visit` takes `revalidate`, and the server-function transport's `go` passes it, so the target loads as `revalidate: true` loads: no fresh entry kept, no prefetch reused. A `.stream()` redirect takes the same `go`. Other visits are unchanged.
201 [x] A server function outside src/ was never registered, so every call to it was a silent 404
201.1 [x] Plan 21 8.7: codegen lists the server functions under src/ alone, while the boundary transform gives an id to any module under the root, so a `createServerFn` in `lib/fns.ts` got an id no server knew; one outside the root, a workspace package, got none. Either way every client call was a 404 with no hint. Red: plugin/flare.test.ts `server function home` (`lib/echo.ts` transformed without an error).
201.2 [x] Fix: vite/boundary/home.ts `assertRegistered` fails dev and the build for a module outside src/ that declares a server function, naming the file and the function. The plugin checks every script module it sees but dependencies, Flare's own source, and virtual ids; a module outside the root is checked and not rewritten. A test run (mode `test`) skips the check, since a test file may define a server function no client calls. Registering modules outside src/ was rejected: the set is unbounded (tests, scripts, workspace packages) and a test file's function would ship in the server.
202 [x] A callback failure past every stage rejected server.fetch: Node sent a bare unlogged 500, Workers showed error 1101
202.1 [x] Plan 21 9.1: measured through the real createServer on Bun 1.4.3, a server-function module that fails to import, a mount that answers `undefined`, and a BigInt in loader data on a navigation each rejected `server.fetch` with nothing logged, and a throwing `serverContext` answered 500 with nothing logged. A throwing `tags` function, a BigInt on a document, a throwing or `undefined` store read, a throwing store write, a throwing `head`, `rewrite`, or `authenticate` already answered a logged 500 or served live. Red: tests/integration/fetch-boundary.test.ts, one case each (three rejected, one logged nothing), and tests/workerd/server.test.ts `worker boundary` (workerd's own exception page, no security headers).
202.2 [x] Fix: lifecycle/exchange.ts `bounded` wraps each request at server.fetch: a rejection or a sync throw is projected once and answered with an empty 500 and the default security headers. `serverContext`'s throw goes through `failed`, which projects it. A mount's answer that is not a Response throws `mount <prefix> answered <type>, not a Response`, so the log names the bug.
202.3 [x] Plan 21 9.2, a throwing `authenticate` on a page as a silent 500, held since BUGS.md 157 (the bare 500 is projected): fetch-boundary.test `authenticate throw` now pins it, the dev overlay payload and the log line on a document and on a navigation. Plan 21 9.4 is BUGS.md 158.
203 [x] A throwing onReady or shouldRefetch left the page never hydrated, and a throwing onHydrated skipped the stored page's fill
203.1 [x] Plan 21 9.3: createReady ran the app's callbacks bare, and the match cache asked a page's `shouldRefetch` bare in the hydrate's first plan, so one throw ended the boot: no `data-flare-hydrated`, no later callback, no fill of a stored page's holes. Red: tests/integration/callbacks-client.test.ts `throwing callbacks` on every engine (the client did not hydrate), ready.test `throwing callback`, and match-cache.test `should refetch throws` (the throw escaped the plan).
203.2 [x] Fix: each createClient callback runs guarded in ready.ts, and a throwing `shouldRefetch` is caught in match-cache.ts's asker and counts as no answer. Both report to one reporter the boot names (create-client.ts `callbackReporter`): `console.error("[flare] an app callback threw", error)`, plus the dev overlay payload in dev; before the boot names it, each logs alone. The rest of the phase runs, so the page hydrates and fills.
204 [x] A line comment after the last property of a createServerFn config swallowed the injected id, so every call 404ed
204.1 [x] Plan 21 13.1: the transform wrote `__id` and `__name` at the end of the config's trimmed text, so in `createServerFn({ method: "get" // read\n})` they landed inside the comment and the function shipped with no id. Red: boundary/transform.test `config comment` (a line comment, a block comment after a trailing comma, an empty object holding only a comment).
204.2 [x] Fix: route-meta/extract.ts records `after`, the offset just past the config's last property (or inside `{` when it has none), from the AST, and the transform writes the fields there. A trailing comma or comment now stays after the fields.
205 [x] Any `.response()` call in a page file, a helper's included, made the page a `.response()` route that always answered 500
205.1 [x] Plan 21 13.2: codegen's scan read every method call in the file, so `const answer = (res) => res.response()` beside `export const route = createPage(…).render(…)` flagged the page `.response()`; the server then found no `response` on it and answered 500. `.authenticate()`, `.authorize()`, `.cache()`, `.input()`, and `.intercept()` were read the same way. Red: codegen/scan.test `route chain only` (a helper's `.response()` and another builder's `.authenticate().cache()` marked the page).
205.2 [x] Fix: scan.ts `routeCalls` walks the exported `route`'s chain, down each call's receiver, through module-level consts it starts from, and through parentheses, `as`, `satisfies`, and `!`; only those calls are read. The id check still reads every builder call. The scan tests that used a bare `page.authenticate()` now build the route's chain.
206 [x] Codegen kept one of two layouts with one id, and built a page whose own root had no root layout
206.1 [x] Plan 21 13.3: `assertRoot` passed when any root layout existed, so an `_admin_` page beside only `_root_/root.root-layout.tsx` built and rendered with no document, its chain holding no root layout; and two layout files with one id, two `*.layout.tsx` in a folder or one beside the root layout, both entered the generated `layouts` record, which kept the second. Red: codegen/routes.test `own root` (neither threw).
206.2 [x] Fix: codegen/layout-checks.ts. `assertRoot` checks each page against its own root, the id up to the first root folder, and names every page missing one by root; `assertOneLayout` fails on a second file for one layout id, naming both. The route tests that relied on `_root_` serving `_admin_` pages now give those pages their root.
207 [x] On Workers every 404 fell through to the assets binding, so an SPA binding turned a page's notFound(), a server function's 404, and a navigation's 404 into a 200 index.html
207.1 [x] Plan 21 13.4: `createWorkersHandler` sent any 404 the server answered to `env[assets]`, so with `not_found_handling: "single-page-application"`, which answers every path with its index, the app's own 404s reached the client as 200. Red: adapters/workers/handler.test `app 404 kept` (`/gone`'s notFound(), its data request, and a server function's notFound() all answered 200).
207.2 [x] Fix: finalize.ts marks the not-found document of a request no route matched (`markUnmatched`, set in create.ts `routed`), the middleware exit and the dev tags carry the mark onto their rewrap (`carryMark`), and the handler forwards only a marked answer to the binding. The handler tests' fake servers answer with that mark.
208 [x] A `.response()` page answered every method, so a PUT or a DELETE to a GET-only page got its full body
208.1 [x] Plan 21 13.5: B87 gave a `.response()` page no method list, so every method reached its function; a page that served a file or a JSON document answered PUT and DELETE with that body. Red: server/lifecycle/methods.test `response methods` (HEAD sent the body; POST, PUT, PATCH, and DELETE reached the function).
208.2 [x] Fix, decision B87 revised (my call while the reviewer was away): `.response(fn, { methods })` declares the methods, GET when none. HEAD runs the function as GET does and sends no body, OPTIONS is 204 with `Allow` unless declared, and any other method is 405 with `Allow` and never runs it. Codegen reads the literal list from the route's chain and writes it on the node, so a refused method imports nothing (create.test `method before load`); a hand-built router's static record carries it. shared/route-builder/methods.ts checks a list in codegen and at `createServer`; the shared builder only stores it. Two integration routes that took POST now declare it.
209 [x] TRACE on Node was a bare 500 with no headers and no log
209.1 [x] Plan 21 13.6: the Node listener built a fetch `Request` with the request's method, and undici refuses `TRACE` (one of fetch's forbidden methods), so the build threw and the listener's catch wrote a bare 500, logging nothing. Bun builds the request and the server answers 405. Red: tests/integration/adapters.test.ts `node trace` (`500 internal server error`).
209.2 [x] Fix: adapters/node/listener.ts answers CONNECT, TRACE, or TRACK itself with the 405 a page gives (`Allow: GET, HEAD, OPTIONS`, the default security headers, through finalize) and does not ask the server; the catch for a failure before the headers now logs it. A refused method is no server fault, so it logs nothing, as on Bun.
210 [x] A percent-encoded unreserved character in a path 404ed, and the sitemap listed a literal-bracket route by its pattern
210.1 [x] Plan 21 13.7: the canonical path kept escapes as sent, so `/docs/%72outing`, `%72` being `r`, matched no route and was a 404; and prerender described a static route by its node path, the pattern, so the docs app's `[_]flare]` folder reached the sitemap as `/[_]flare]`, a URL the app does not serve. Red: shared/url/canonical.test `escapes` and server/cache/prerender.test `concrete path`.
210.2 [x] Fix: shared/url/canonical.ts decodes an escape of an unreserved character and uppercases every other escape's hex (RFC 3986 6.2.2), so such a path is the canonical 301 to its plain form (tests/integration/hostile.test `unreserved escapes`); an escaped `.` or `..` segment is hostile. prerender.ts builds a static route's path with `buildUrl`, so the sitemap lists `/[flare]`, the URL the router serves (an escaped `%5Bflare%5D` is a 404 there, so the sitemap writes the brackets as served).
211 [x] A stored page opened with a query kept a link current that was not, and a deep link's hash read empty after hydrate
211.1 [x] Plan 21 13.8 and 13.9: hydrate adopts the server's DOM without applying the client's first values, and the client hydrated on the browser's URL while the server had rendered another, a stored render with no search and every render with no hash. So on `/a?utm=mail` from a stored `/a` the exact link to `/a` kept its server `aria-current`, a second current link beside the section link, and on `/a#top` a `useLocation().hash` binding kept the server's empty text. Red: tests/integration/active-client.test.ts `stored page with a query` and `deep link hash` on every engine.
211.2 [x] Fix: the state carries `search`, the query the render saw, none for a shared or stored render (server/render/state.ts, document.ts). The client hydrates on the browser's path with that search and no hash (`renderedLocation`, client/boot/hydrate.ts), so the DOM matches, and commits the browser's own URL once mounted, before the hydrated callbacks, so every binding that reads the search or the hash updates.
212 [x] Back onto an entry the server answered 404 kept the previous page on screen
212.1 [x] Plan 21 13.10: a URL no page matches resolved to a data navigation, and the server answers a data request for an unknown URL with a bare 404 (B73); the client painted that status at the root, which in an app whose root renders the document cannot be painted, so Back from `/a` onto `/missing` left page a on screen under the `/missing` URL. A page that threw notFound() painted correctly. Red: tests/integration/history-client.test.ts `unmatched entry` on every engine.
212.2 [x] Fix: client/navigation/target.ts resolves a URL no page matches to a document load, so the server's not-found document renders, the not-found page inside its root, as on a first visit. On a Back or Forward the URL is already the current one, so the load replaces the entry.
213 [x] A navigation that committed nothing left the current page's deferred values pending forever
213.1 [x] Plan 21 13.11: starting a navigation aborted the previous request, and after its commit that request is still the one streaming the page's deferred values, which stay pending once it aborts (frames.ts assumes a newer page owns the screen). When the new navigation commits nothing, an input failure (400) or a blocked one, the page stays on screen with values that never settle. Red: navigate-fill.test `uncommitted keeps stream` (still pending 200 ms after its frame was ready).
213.2 [x] Fix: client/navigation/requests.ts. Starting a navigation aborts only the newest request that has not committed; the one whose page is on screen streams on until another page commits, which aborts it.
214 [x] Closing an overlay after searching inside it took one Back per search
214.1 [x] Plan 21 13.12: an overlay entry was always a push, even with `replace`, so each search inside the open docs search overlay, which navigates with `replace: true`, pushed another overlay entry, and dismissing it (`history.back()`) stepped through every search before reaching the page beneath. Red: tests/integration/overlay-client.test.ts `replace inside an overlay` on every engine (one Back landed on `/photo?n=2`, overlay open). The language-switch half of the item is by design: switching locale moves the background page to another locale's URL, which the overlay does not intercept, so it closes; dx/navigate.md and B84 say so.
214.2 [x] Fix, decision B84 revised: opening an overlay still pushes its entry; a navigation inside the open overlay with `replace` replaces that entry and keeps it intercepted (client/history/history.ts `replace(url, usr, intercepted)`, client/navigation/overlay.ts `writeEntry`).
215 [x] A render that threw before the shell, with no boundary, answered a 0-byte 500
215.1 [x] Plan 21 13.13: B93 gave a throw before the shell an empty body in production, and B37 gave a root with no document an empty 500, so a page whose render threw outside any `errorRender`, or a root render that threw, reached the browser as a blank page with no word of what happened. A loader error already rendered through the boundaries. Red: tests/integration/fetch-boundary.test.ts `render before the shell` (empty body), and projector.test `bigint`, shape.test `prod document-less` asserted the empty body.
215.2 [x] Fix, decisions B93 and B37 revised: server/render/error-page.ts writes a minimal document, the status and `Something went wrong` (or a named status's message), with the digest a production bug carries and the message and stack only in dev; it loads nothing, so any security policy allows it. lifecycle/answers.ts answers it for a render that failed before the shell and for a root with no document.
216 [x] createServer ignored the observability options dx/observability.md documents, so onError never fired
216.1 [x] Plan 21 13.14: dx/observability.md showed `createServer({ logger?, onError?, requestLog?, tracer? })` as working options, while v1 has none of them (BUGS.md 113 moved them under Planned in dx/server.md only). `ServerOptions` has no such key, so TypeScript refuses one, but a JavaScript app, or one that casts, passed `onError` and heard nothing. Red: lifecycle/create-input.test `planned options` (no throw).
216.2 [x] Fix, the plan's interim ruling until plan 15 ships them: createServer throws for any of the four keys, naming it, and dx/observability.md opens by saying the page is planned and not in v1.
217 [x] In a production build a notFound() thrown in render answered 500, and a render bug's log line lost its message
217.1 [x] Found during 215: Solid's production server build sanitizes a render-phase throw, replacing anything not marked safe with a generic `Internal Server Error`, before the slot boundary and the projector see it. So a page whose render threw notFound() answered 500, not 404 (B93 says its own status), and every render bug logged `Error: Internal Server Error at ssrSanitizeError`, never the thrown message or stack (against B76 as revised by BUGS.md 158). Vitest loads Solid's development build, which does not sanitize, so only a built app shows it. Red: tests/integration/render-errors.test.ts `production render throws` on a vite production build (`/missing` answered 500).
217.2 [x] Fix: server/render/stream-options.ts answers Solid's `onError` for a render error with what Solid should use instead: a named status passes as itself, the app's answer; a safe error keeps Solid's handling; a bug becomes a fresh generic error, which the client sees as it would Solid's own, tied in a WeakMap to the thrown value. projector.ts `projectError` reads the thrown value back (`thrownBehind`), so the status and the log line are the throw's own.
218 [x] The sharp peer floor admitted versions with two high-severity advisories
218.1 [x] Plan 21 14.3: `peerDependencies.sharp` was `>=0.35.3`, so an app could resolve 0.35.3 or 0.35.4, which GitHub's advisory database lists under GHSA-wq5f-xc86-pv6w (librsvg, fixed in 0.35.5) and GHSA-rgj7-g3m4-5g8c (libheif, fixed in 0.35.4), both high. Red: tests/structure/exports.test.ts `advisory floors` (35003 under 35005).
218.2 [x] Fix: the catalog, the dev dependency, and the peer floor are 0.35.5, with bun.lock; README and 00-rules name it. Miniflare's own sharp 0.34.5 is its private test tooling, never resolved by an app through Flare.
219 [x] The release job, which holds publish credentials, ran `npm install -g npm@latest` and actions by moving tags
219.1 [x] Plan 21 14.4: release.yml's npmjs job, with `id-token: write`, installed whatever npm was latest before it published, and every workflow step used an action by its major tag (`actions/checkout@v4`), so a moved tag or a compromised npm release would run inside the publish. Red: tests/structure/workflows.test.ts `pinned actions` (every `uses:` in ci.yml and release.yml unpinned) and `nothing latest` (release.yml).
219.2 [x] Fix: every action in ci.yml and release.yml is pinned to the commit SHA its tag named on 2026-10-17, its version kept as a comment; the npm install is gone, since Node 24's own npm 11 publishes with `--provenance`.
220 [x] Dev dropped Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy, so an opener bug passed in dev and failed in production
220.1 [x] Plan 21 14.2: `buildCsp` deleted both HSTS and Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy in dev. HSTS has a reason, it would pin https for every local server; COOP had none, and a popup or OAuth flow that needs `window.opener` worked in dev and broke once deployed. Red: server/security/csp.test `dev extras` (no COOP in dev).
220.2 [x] Fix: dev drops HSTS alone. dx/server.md now lists every dev difference with its reason (HSTS dropped; `'unsafe-eval'`, the localhost `ws:` and `http:` sources, and `blob:` workers added for Vite). The tests that proved a production stub by COOP prove it by the CSP's missing `'unsafe-eval'`. features-client `theme script` re-applies the tab's emulated color scheme and reloads: under COOP, Firefox moves the first load into a new browsing context group, which Playwright's media emulation does not follow; a visitor's own color scheme is the system's and is unaffected.
221 [x] The default CSP left form-action and frame-ancestors open, a source could inject policy, and a route's CSP dropped the server's
221.1 [x] Plan 21 14.1: the default policy named no `form-action` and no `frame-ancestors`, so a form could post anywhere and only `X-Frame-Options` guarded framing; a source string holding `;` or a line break went into the header as written, ending its directive and adding the caller's own; and a route's `.security()` CSP object replaced the server's whole object, so a page that allowed one frame origin lost every server directive. Red: server/security/csp.test `form and frame defaults`, `injected source`, and apply.test `route csp merge`.
221.2 [x] Fix: the defaults gain `frame-ancestors 'self'` and `form-action 'self'`; csp.ts throws for a source holding `;`, `\r`, or `\n`, naming the directive; apply.ts merges a route's directives onto the server's, the sources of a directive both name kept once (v0's directive merge, the reviewer's ruling for phase 14), while a route string or `false` replaces the whole policy.
222 [x] The client read a data stream with no cap on its frame count or nesting
222.1 [x] Plan 21 16.5, a v0 parity gap: v0's NDJSON client stopped after 10,000 messages and scanned placeholders no deeper than 50 levels; v1 capped only a line at 1 MiB, so a hostile or broken stream of endless small frames, or one frame nested thousands deep for `revive` to recurse through, could exhaust the tab. Red: shared/protocol/frames.test `caps` (10,001 frames all decoded).
222.2 [x] Fix: shared/protocol/frames.ts `frameDecoder` gives one `e` frame and stops past 10,000 frames in a response or for a frame nested past 256 levels, checked without recursion; the client already handles that stop, a document load before `r` and rejected values after it.
223 [x] Every request to a listed page ran its `params()`, with the URL's params
223.1 [x] Plan 21 16.7, a v0 parity gap: v0 kept each `params()` result in a 500-entry cache outside dev; v1 called the page's `params()` on every request, so a list read from a database or a file cost a read per request, and an attacker's unique slugs a read each. The call also passed the URL's params, while the contract and the prerender call give `ctx.params` as `{}`. Red: server/cache/seed.test `params once` (100,001 calls for 100,000 unique slugs and one listed) and `params once served` (3 calls for 3 requests).
223.2 [x] Fix: server/cache/seed.ts reads a page's list once per server, keyed by its params function (one entry per page, so unique URLs hold no memory), with `ctx.params` as `{}`, and indexes it by each entry's names, so a request costs one set lookup per distinct name list. A failed read is not kept; dev reads on each request (`params dev and failure`). B86 and dx/cache.md record it, with `dynamicParams: false` now fixed per server as at prerender.
224 [x] No answer carried Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy unless the app set it
