224.1 [x] Plan 21 16.3, a v0 parity gap: v0 sent `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin` by default; v1 listed it as absent with no decision recorded, so another origin could pull a document, a data answer, or a server function's JSON in as an image or script and bring it into its own process for a Spectre-class read. Red: server/security/defaults.test `corp` and `nosniff`, apply.test `every key`, and integration ingress.test (the Node document and its 404 carry it).
224.2 [x] Fix: server/security/defaults.ts adds `same-origin`, so the security set gives it to every answer Flare sends; a string or `false` from the server or a route replaces it. Decision B135 records why it reaches `.response()` pages and mounts too, past the ruling's documents and data, and that platform-served static files never carry it.
225 [x] No documented safe pattern for a webhook that purges
225.1 [x] Plan 21 16.2, a v0 parity gap: v0's built-in revalidate endpoint checked `revalidateSecret`; v1 dropped it (dx/store.md) and said only that an app writes its own route, so each app had to get the method, the secret's storage, and its comparison right alone, and a plain `===` leaks the secret's prefix by timing.
225.2 [x] Fix: dx/cache.md Purge from a webhook, a POST-only `.response()` route that reads the secret from `env`, compares SHA-256 digests in full, rejects an unset secret, validates the tags, and answers only after `revalidate` resolves. tests/integration/cache.test `webhook purge` runs that route on Node and Bun: four wrong headers, an empty one, and an unset secret are 401 and the page stays a hit; the right one is 204 and the next visit is a miss.
226 [x] SIGTERM killed in-flight requests, store writes, and purges on Node and Bun
226.1 [x] Plan 21 16.11: neither adapter could wait for its work before the process exited. Node retained `waitUntil` promises but exposed no way to await them; Bun handed no `waitUntil` at all, so its background work ran untracked. A deploy's SIGTERM cut a streaming page, dropped the store write a miss had started, and lost a purge a CMS had already been told succeeded. Red: tests/integration/shutdown.test `node sigterm` (`listener.drain is not a function`) and `bun sigterm` (`ctx.waitUntil` undefined, `app.drain is not a function`).
226.2 [x] Fix: adapters/drain.ts tracks answers in flight and `waitUntil` work; `createNodeListener(...).drain({ timeout })` and `createBunFetch(...).drain({ timeout })` resolve once all of it settles, or `false` at the timeout. Node answers sent while draining carry `Connection: close`. Bun now passes a `waitUntil` context, tracks each streamed body until it ends, fails, or is cancelled, and turns a synchronous throw from the server into a rejection. Decision B136 records why the app, not Flare, wires the signal; dx/adapters.md Shutdown and the README's Node server show it, and exports.test `readme node serve` runs that server from the packed package and SIGTERMs it to a 0 exit.
227 [x] A v0 app's service worker kept serving the old build after the move to v1
227.1 [x] Plan 21 16.1, a v0 parity gap: v0 registered the app's worker at `/service-worker.js`, scope `/`, and left a cleanup worker at `/sw.js`; v1 registers none and answers both with a 404, which fails the browser's update check and leaves the old worker in place, so a returning visitor keeps getting the old build from its caches. Red: server/lifecycle/retire-worker.test `retired path` and `checked` against the previous create.ts (a 500 document and no startup check), and e2e minimal.test `retired service worker`.
227.2 [x] Fix: `createServer({ retireServiceWorker })` answers each listed path on GET and HEAD with a worker that activates at once, deletes the origin's caches, unregisters, and reloads the pages it controlled, after the app's middleware and outside the canonical rules, since a worker's update fetch fails on a redirect. The plan named a Vite plugin option; it is a server option, so dev and every runtime answer the path the same and no static file can be cached by a CDN or skipped by a static server (dx/server.md Migrating from v0).
228 [x] After a deploy a CDN kept the previous build's pages with no way to purge them as a set
228.1 [x] Plan 21 16.10, a v0 parity gap: v0's data and asset URLs were keyed by build; v1 dropped that, sent no build tag, and documented no deploy order, so after a deploy an edge could serve the old HTML, which names the old build's hashed chunks, for its whole `maxAge` plus `swr`, and an app that deleted the old assets served pages whose scripts 404. Red: server/lifecycle/policies.test `cache tag` (`a, b` where `a, b, flare-build-dev` was expected, and no tag at all for a public page that names none).
228.2 [x] Fix: policies.ts ends every public page's `Cache-Tag` with `flare-build-<id>`, the id `flare-build` carries, so a deploy can purge the previous build's pages at the edge in one call. dx/server.md Deploying gives the order: read the live id, ship beside the old assets, purge the old tag, then delete the old assets. The client's build-mismatch and missing-chunk reloads stay the backstop, as docs-resilience `build mismatch reload` and `missing chunk reload` show; docs-routing `segment cdn` reads the tag from a real build.
229 [x] Back and Forward waited a round trip and every loader even for a page the client cache held
229.1 [x] Plan 21 16.9, a v0 parity gap: v0 painted a held match at once on Back (0 requests); v1 treated a popstate step like any navigation, so at the default `staleTime` of 0 every step waited on the server with the old page on screen, and no decision recorded the drop. Red: tests/integration/history-client.test `back paints held` in chromium, firefox, and webkit (200 ms after Back the slot still read `page a` while the data answer was held), and client/navigation/navigate-cache.test `back paints held` (the request went out with `root[home:H2]` on screen).
229.2 [x] Fix: the match cache's plan takes `pop` and reports `held` when it holds every match of the entry at any age; request.ts commits such a step from the cache with no request, marked `stale` when any match is past its `staleTime`, and the navigator then revalidates the page once in place. Decision B137 records the ruling and revises B30; dx/cache.md and dx/navigate.md say so.
229.3 [x] Follow-up found by the Mac e2e on 34ac14cc (6 failed, every production project): docs-navigation `view transition document` counted 5 transitions for 4 navigations, because the revalidation after a held Back step committed under the router's default transition, so the page animated twice. The revalidation now runs with `viewTransition: false`. Red: client/navigation/navigate-transition.test `held back once` (4 transitions where 3 were expected, against the previous navigate.ts).
230 [x] dx/link.md told apps to read a boolean back from the URL with `z.coerce.boolean()`, which reads "false" as true
230.1 [x] Plan 21 4.7: a boolean goes into the URL as `String(value)`, and the table's advice for reading it back, `z.coerce.boolean()`, is `Boolean(input)`, true for any non-empty string, so `?open=false` validated as `true` and a toggle written to the URL could never turn off. Red: tests/integration/link.test `boolean values`, a route validating with the documented `z.coerce.boolean()` through the real server, rendered `open:true` for `?open=false`.
230.2 [x] Fix: the row names `z.stringbool()`, which reads `"true"` and `"false"`, says why `z.coerce.boolean()` is wrong, and gives the valibot form; the case's route uses `z.stringbool()` and both values round-trip.
231 [x] A key purge outside a request could not name a production page's key
231.1 [x] Plan 21 4.5, the reviewer's ruling: a store key is `static:<build>:<routeId>:<rawParams>` (B32), but the server exposed neither its build id nor the key builder, so a queue consumer or script calling `createRevalidateFn(store)({ keys })` had to rebuild the key by hand, guessing the id `flare()` hashes and the raw-param encoding, and a wrong guess purged nothing, silently. Red: tests/integration/cache.test `key purge` (`server.buildId` undefined).
231.2 [x] Fix: `createServer` returns `buildId` and `cache.key(target)`, which takes `buildUrl`'s input, matches the URL it writes as a request would (base path, trailing slash), and keys that match with server/cache/key.ts `storeKey`, so the key is the one that page's requests store under; an id the router has no page for throws. key.test `route key` covers the base path, an absent optional, and the raw params' percent-encoding. dx/server.md, dx/store.md, and the README say so.
232 [x] A mount answered ahead of every middleware, so a prefix guard never covered a sub-app
232.1 [x] Plan 21 4.2, the reviewer's ruling: a mount was dispatched at 02-system step 3 and middleware ran only at step 6, so an auth middleware listed for `/api` guarded the app's pages under `/api` and let every request to a mount at `/api` through, unauthenticated. Red: tests/integration/middleware.test `mount middleware` (a refused request got the mount's 200).
232.2 [x] Fix: server/mount/mount.ts `mountFor` finds the mount without calling it, and create.ts runs that call as the innermost handler of the middleware list, with `ctx.kind` `"mount"`; a refused request never reaches the mount's fetch. The mount keeps its place before the server-function path and outside the locale slot. Decision B138, 02-system steps 3 and 6, and dx/server.md's middleware tables say so.
233 [x] A `.response()` route took a cross-site POST, so a page elsewhere could act as a signed-in visitor
233.1 [x] Plan 21 4.3, the reviewer's ruling: server functions check Origin, Sec-Fetch-Site, and Referer (B44), but a `.response()` route that declared POST, PUT, PATCH, or DELETE ran for any sender, so a form on another site could post to it with the visitor's cookies and the route's `authenticate` would accept them. Red: tests/integration/hostile.test `response csrf` (a POST with `Origin: https://evil.example` ran the handler and answered 200).
233.2 [x] Fix: server-fn/csrf.ts `routeCsrf` gives such a request the server-function check, as a POST, and create.ts runs it before the route's loaders, with the chain's effective auth turning off the fail-open, so a webhook with none of the three headers still reaches a route that reads no identity. auth.test `response passes` now sends its own origin. B44 and dx/server-fn.md say so.
234 [x] A public page with no store rendered on the visitor's request under public CDN headers
234.1 [x] Plan 21 4.4, the reviewer's ruling: with a store, a public page's body came from the anonymous shared render; without one, the same page rendered live on the visitor's own request, so a loader or `serverContext` that read the cookie or the search put that visitor's data into an answer marked `public, s-maxage`, which the edge then served to everyone. Red: tests/integration/cacheability.test `public render anonymous` (the bare server's body held `alice` from the visitor's cookie).
234.2 [x] Fix: server/cache/shared-page.ts `sharedPage` runs the shared render for a public decision no store answers and returns it in the stored-page form, and serve.ts serves it as a miss would, with no `flare-cache` and no write; scrub.ts renders on the request's origin when no `site` is set. The case covers `isr`, `ssr`, and `ssg` with and without a store; serve.test `no store` now expects the shared request. Decision B139 and dx/cache.md say so.
235 [x] A server-function stream cut short read as a finished one
235.1 [x] Plan 21 4.6, a v0 parity gap: v0 ended a stream with `{ d: true }`; v1's NDJSON had no end line, so a connection a proxy, a timeout, or a crash closed early ended the client's iterator as cleanly as a generator that finished, and the app showed a partial list as the whole one. Red: server/server-fn/stream.test `two yields` (no end line), client/server-fn/stream.test `cut stream` (the iterator resolved done for a body cut after one line, mid-line, and empty).
235.2 [x] Fix: server/server-fn/stream.ts writes `{ name, status: 200, end: true }` when the generator finishes; client/server-fn/stream.ts returns only at that line, and a body that stops before it, or ends in a part of a line, rejects with `ServerFnError` status 0. Decision B140, 02-system, and dx/server-fn.md say so.
236 [x] A client navigation left focus on the link it followed and announced nothing
236.1 [x] Plan 21 4.1, the reviewer's ruling: a document load puts focus at the top of the new page and a screen reader reads its title, but a Flare client navigation swapped the page under the old focus, so a keyboard user's next Tab continued from the link in the old page's position and a screen reader user heard nothing at all. Red: client/navigation/arrival.test (no module), and tests/integration/history-client.test `arrival focus` against the previous create-client.ts in chromium, firefox, and webkit (focus on `body`, no live region).
236.2 [x] Fix: client/navigation/arrival.ts `createArrival` moves focus to the hash target, else the main heading, else `main`, with `preventScroll`, and sets the polite live region `#flare-announcer` to the title; the navigator calls it from `land` when a navigation commits another pathname, Back and Forward included, and never for a search-only change, a hash move, a revalidation, or an overlay. Decision B141 and dx/navigate.md Focus and announcement say so; the overlay's trap and the axe scan are the next phases.
237 [x] An open overlay left keyboard focus on the page under it, and Escape did nothing
237.1 [x] Plan 21 4.1, the reviewer's ruling: an intercepted route's overlay painted over the page while focus stayed on the link that opened it, so Tab walked the hidden page behind the dialog, Escape did not close it, and closing it lost the user's place. Red: shared/components/intercept-outlet.test `overlay focus` (focus still on the opener), and tests/integration/overlay-client.test `overlay keyboard` against the previous intercept-outlet.tsx in chromium, firefox, and webkit (focus never reached the overlay).
237.2 [x] Fix: shared/components/overlay-focus.ts `trapFocus` moves focus in (`autofocus`, else the first focusable, else the first element), wraps Tab, dismisses on Escape, and gives focus back to the opener on close; `InterceptOutlet` raises it in an effect once its child is on screen and reads the child's elements lazily through its accessors, so it never calls the child twice (`overlay focus kept`). Decision B142 and dx/navigate.md say so.
238 [x] On Bun a text Response from a middleware or a `.response()` page was sent as application/octet-stream
238.1 [x] Plan 21 12.7: Bun types a string body `text/plain` only when it sends that Response with its body unread, and never puts the type in `headers`; Flare rebuilt every middleware, mount, and `.response()` answer from `response.body` to add the security set, so the type was gone and Bun sent `application/octet-stream`, which under `nosniff` made the browser download a maintenance page instead of showing it. Red: tests/integration/adapters.test `text body type` under Bun (`undefined` for both the middleware's 503 and the page's text, where Node gives `text/plain;charset=utf-8`).
238.2 [x] Fix: exchange.ts `answerFrom` keeps a Response that names no type as itself, setting the final headers on it with finalize.ts `keptAs`, so its body is never read, and builds anew one that names a type or whose headers cannot change; adapters/bun/serve.ts skips the idle lift for a Blob body, which Bun's inspector tells from a stream without reading it, so a real stream is still lifted (`bun idle stream`). dx/adapters.md says so, and that Bun's `node:http` cannot see the type either.
239 [x] A stored page stayed free of injected script by one line no test guarded
239.1 [x] Plan 21 7.3, from the reviewer's mutation run: the hit's state script is written back with `escapeInlineScript` in server/cache/nonce.ts `withStateNonce`, and a mutant that wrote plain `JSON.stringify` there survived every test, though it would turn loader data holding `</script><script>` into a live script on every cache hit. The code was right; nothing failed without it. Red: server/cache/nonce.test `escaped state` and tests/integration/cache.test `hostile state hit` both fail against that mutant (the replayed page holds `<script>alert`).
239.2 [x] Fix: the two cases, a unit round trip through the write and a hit, and a real stored page served as a miss and then a hit, each asserting the hostile text stays escaped (`\u003c/script>`) and opens no script; they pass on the code as it is.
240 [x] Two URLs of one route could share a store key or a match id
240.1 [x] Plan 21 7.8: the store key joined param values with `/`, the same character a catch-all joins its segments with, so on `/[...a]/s/[b]/t/[...c]` the URLs `/a/s/b/t/a/b` and `/a/b/s/a/t/b` both keyed `a/b/a/b` and one page's stored copy answered the other; a match id wrote `key=value&...` with the values as they were, so a value holding `&` or `=` could read as another param, and the client cache and `_flare_stale` mixed two pages. Red: tests/structure/keys.test `no shared key`, every shape of up to three plain, optional, catch-all, and optional catch-all params with hostile values through the real router (the first collision it named was that store key).
240.2 [x] Fix: server/cache/key.ts joins param values with `&` and escapes `%`, `/`, and `&` inside a value; shared/route-tree/match-id.ts escapes `%`, `&`, and `=` inside a value. Single-param and single catch-all keys keep their form; key.test and match-id.test now name the `&` form. B86 and dx/cache.md say so.
241 [x] Decided: a CDN-cached public page shares its nonce with every visitor of that copy
241.1 [x] Plan 21 7.9: a public page's CSP header and HTML are cached together, so one nonce serves every visitor for the copy's `s-maxage`. The plan asked for a hash-based policy or a nonce-free shell. Weighed against the client: on a client navigation Flare inserts nonced elements, the stylesheet loader's script (`shared/components/stylesheet.tsx`), the view-transition style (`view-transition-css.tsx`), and a route's inline head scripts and styles (`client/head/apply.ts`), and a hash-only policy from a public first page blocks every one of them, which would leave the next page unstyled.
241.2 [x] Decision B144: the nonce stays. It grants nothing to an attacker, since the cached copy's content was fixed at the render that chose it, every inline value is escaped (BUGS.md 239 guards it), and the render is anonymous (B139, BUGS.md 234), so no visitor's data reaches it once the nonce is known. dx/server.md One nonce says so, and that a page needing a per-visitor nonce goes without `cdn.maxAge`. No code changed.
242 [x] Back with a failed data request changed the URL and left the old page on screen

246 [x] In Chromium a long docs session keeps every page it left that holds a flare `<Image>`: about one image and 46 elements a navigation
246.1 [x] Plan 21 12.6. Red: e2e `docs-session.test.ts` `long session` on node-prod chromium, sixty navigations between home (a fill, priority, blur `<Image>`) and /docs/routing (a fixed, lazy one): after a forced collection the live images went 11 at step 10 to 61 at step 60, elements 588 to 2893. Between /changelog and /playground, which hold no image, elements stay flat (152, 153, 153), so the pages are freed unless they hold an `<Image>`. The harness reproduces nothing: `memory-client.test.ts`, forty steps between two pages of fixed srcset `<Image>`s, keeps elements flat and images at 4, and stays as a guard.
246.2 [x] What it is not: each `<Image>`'s owner is disposed (61 made, 60 cleaned up), view transitions are not it (the count is the same with `startViewTransition` removed), and a heap snapshot (CDP, 20 steps) shows no JS path to a left page's `<article>`: its only holder is Chromium's own, the document's `blink::MediaQueryMatcher` keeping each image's `HTMLImageElement::ViewportChangeListener`. Removing `srcset` and `sizes` in the component's cleanup, or from every image while still connected before each navigation, changes nothing; removing each image from the DOM before leaving cuts elements to 16 a step but the images stay. Plain-DOM copies of the same `<img>`, made with createElement, innerHTML, a template clone, importNode, attributes before or after insertion, eager or lazy, removed loaded or mid-load, are all collected.
246.3 [x] The cause, a Chromium bug Solid's templates trigger. Blink's `HTMLImageElement::SelectSourceURL` registers an image whose candidate came from a `srcset` with its document's `MediaQueryMatcher` viewport listeners, and `RemovedFrom` drops it only from the document the image is in when it leaves. Solid's `template()` clones from the `<template>`'s content (`cloneNode`; `importNode` only with flag 1), so a page's elements belong to the template document until they are inserted. `Dynamic` makes the `<img>` in the main document with its `srcset`; inserted under a cloned parent it is adopted into the template document (`DidMoveToNewDocument` selects again, registering it there), and adopted back on insertion into the page. Leaving the page removes it from the main document's listeners alone, so the template document, which lives as long as the page, holds every image and the page around it. The heap path reads Window, HTMLDocument, HTMLDocument (the template document), MediaQueryMatcher, ViewportChangeListener, img. Plain DOM reproduces it: twenty `<img srcset>` under a template-cloned parent, inserted and removed, leave 20 images; under a created parent, none.
246.4 [x] Fix: `image.tsx` gives a client-rendered image its `src`, `srcset`, and `sizes` only once it is on the page, at `onSettled`, so Blink never selects a source while the image belongs to the template document; the server's and a hydrated image's sources are written as rendered, and only a hydrated image checks `complete` for B20, since an image with no source reports complete. `long session` passes with `test.fail` removed (twice), and the docs grid passes on node prod Chromium and Firefox and node dev Chromium (556). Red first: `image.test.tsx` `sources once placed` (each source written while detached). The Image budget is 18669, 49 bytes more (bits/01).

Both stay as guards; `hydrate.api.ts` gains the image module the memory guard renders.

264 [x] No table held every kind of value through every place the wire carries one
264.1 [x] Plan 21 6.2 (serialization): `tests/structure/wire.test.ts` runs 21 values, 0, -0, NaN, the infinities, BigInts, hostile and unusual strings (`</script>`, U+2028, NUL, `<!--`), undefined keys, valid and invalid Dates, a RegExp, a Map with object keys, a Set of objects, a sparse array, nested mixes, prototype-named keys, a null-prototype object, URL and URLSearchParams, typed arrays, a cycle, and a shared reference, through the codec, an `l` frame, the document's state script, and a server-function envelope: each arrives equal on Node and Bun, the state script keeps one closing tag whatever the strings hold, a shared reference stays one object, a cycle points at itself, and -0 stays -0. It passes with no change to flare.
265 [x] No test sent hostile raw requests to every runtime, and the e2e static layer crashed the Node server on a malformed escape
265.1 [x] Plan 21 17.1 (the hostile-request corpus): e2e `docs-hostile.test.ts` `hostile corpus` writes 22 raw HTTP/1.1 requests, which no client normalizes, to the node, bun, and workers production servers: dot segments plain and encoded, encoded slashes in either case, a malformed and a NUL escape, `//` and `\` starts, a Host that holds a path and a foreign one, every method on a page, a doubled and an unknown `_flare` mode, an unknown server function, an empty body, the wrong method, an 8,000-character path, and a CR/LF in the query. Each runtime gives each request one expected answer. The first run found the e2e boot's own static layer (`boot.ts` STATIC) decoding the path outside a try: `GET /docs/%zz` killed the Node production server and answered 500 on Bun, and a Host Bun cannot read left `request.url` relative, which its `new URL` threw on. The README's `serve.js` already guards its decode, so an app that follows it was never exposed. Fix: the static layer skips a path it cannot decode or parse, as the README's does, and the app answers it: 404 for the escape on all three, 400 for the Host on Node and Bun. workerd itself answers 500 to a Host that holds a path, before the worker runs (`invalid hostname when stringifying URL`); Cloudflare's edge validates Host first, so the corpus names that one answer per runtime.
266 [x] A public page whose anonymous render was not a clean 200 answered a visitor's own render with the public policy
266.1 [x] Review of BUGS 234. A public page renders as the anonymous request, so the CDN copy never carries a visitor (plan 21 4.4). When that render gives nothing to serve, such as a loader that throws `notFound()` without a cookie, the visitor's own render answers, and it carried the shared decision: a visitor with the cookie got their page as `public, max-age=0, s-maxage=60`, which an edge keeps and serves to everyone. Both paths did it: the no-store path from BUGS 234 (fixed in that commit) and the stored miss (`serve.ts` `missed`), which is older. A second cause hid under both: `answers.ts` `awaited` rebuilt `rejected` from the deferred values alone, so `uncached()` never reached a live render, and an incomplete shared render (B15) went out public too. Fix: a render with nothing to serve answers with the visitor's own render, `private, no-store`, on both paths, and `awaited` keeps a policy already rejected. Red first: integration `cacheability.test.ts` `public render refused`, with a store and without, document and data.
267 [x] A thrown redirect({ to }) and the i18n locale redirect skipped the trailing-slash rule
267.1 [x] Review of BUGS 188, which made a `Link`, `navigate()`, and a prerendered path follow the rule. Two writers still built the URL by hand: `redirect/builder.ts` `redirectLocation`, which a loader's, a middleware's, and a server function's `redirect({ to })` use, and `i18n/server/match.ts`'s locale-slot redirect. Under `trailingSlash: "always"`, `throw redirect({ to: "_root_/about" })` sent Location `/about` and then a 301 to `/about/`, and a negotiated `hr` visitor got a 302 to `/hr/pricing` and then a 301. Both also wrote `/app/?q=1` for the index under a base, where the router writes `/app?q=1`. Fix: both write through `routeHref` with the router's base and rule, and a server function's dispatch carries the rule. Red first: `builder.test.ts` `redirect location`, `match.test.ts` `route href`; `envelope.test.ts` `redirect` holds a server function's under `always`.
268 [x] The default frame-ancestors 'self' was looser than the X-Frame-Options DENY it overrides
268.1 [x] Review of BUGS 221. A browser that reads both `X-Frame-Options` and a CSP `frame-ancestors` obeys the CSP and ignores the header. The default `frame-ancestors 'self'` therefore let a same-origin page frame every Flare page, where the default `X-Frame-Options: DENY` let none, and an app that set `X-Frame-Options: false` to allow a partner's embed was blocked cross-origin by a directive it never wrote. Fix: the default `frame-ancestors` restates the `X-Frame-Options` the answer sends, the server's or a route's: `'none'` for `DENY`, `'self'` for `SAMEORIGIN`, and no directive when the header is removed; a named `frame-ancestors` still wins. `apply.test.ts` `server security` now asserts a value only the callback sets, since the default's text no longer proves the callback ran. Red first: `csp.test.ts` `form and frame defaults`, `apply.test.ts` `frame follows x-frame-options`.
269 [x] In `vite dev` an `<Errored>` fallback showed "Internal Server Error" instead of the thrown message
269.1 [x] Review of BUGS 217. `stream-options.ts` `onError` handed Solid a generic error in place of a bug in every build, to undo the production server build's sanitizing. Solid's dev build sanitizes nothing and gives the boundary, and its serialized copy, what `onError` returns, so in `vite dev` an app's `fallback={(error) => error().message}` showed "Internal Server Error". Fix: `inPlace` maps only under Solid's production build (`@solidjs/web` `isDev` false); in dev it returns nothing and Solid keeps the thrown value. Red first: `stream-options.test.ts` `dev keeps the error` rendered "shown: Internal Server Error"; `tests/integration/render-errors.test.ts` still holds the production build's 404 and generic message.
270 [x] A retired worker path went through the locale slot, so a negotiated visitor's worker update met a redirect
270.1 [x] Review of BUGS 227. A retired worker path is exempt from the trailing-slash, rewrite, and case rules, since a worker's update fetch fails on a redirect, but not from the locale slot. With `i18n()` and a `[[locale]]/[...slug]` catch-all, a visitor whose cookie or Accept-Language named `de` got `/sw.js` as a 302 to `/de/sw.js`, so the v0 worker never retired. Fix: `create.ts` `route` runs the locale slot for a page request only when its path is no retired worker's. Red first: integration `i18n.test.ts` `retired worker`, for the cookie and Accept-Language alike; a page beside it still redirects.
271 [x] A middleware that read a mount request's body made the mount answer 500
271.1 [x] Review of BUGS 232, which runs middleware for mounts. `mount.ts` `mountFor` built the mount's stripped `new Request(url, request)` when it matched the prefix, before middleware ran, and a new Request takes over the body stream: a middleware that read `ctx.request.clone().text()` on a POST to a mount then threw "Body is unusable", a 500. Fix: the stripped request is built when the mount runs, after middleware. Red first: integration `middleware.test.ts` `mount body after middleware`.
272 [x] A middleware's rewrap of the not-found answer lost the no-route mark, so on Workers it never reached the assets binding
272.1 [x] Review of BUGS 207. The router marks the not-found document of a GET or HEAD no route matched, and only that answer falls through to Workers' assets binding (plan 21 13.4). The mark was the Response object itself, so a middleware that rewrapped the answer, `new Response(response.body, response)` as bits/08 and bits/09 show, returned an unmarked one: with assets, `/app.js` was the app's 404 and never the file, while dx/adapters.md said the mark survives a rewrap. Marking the body stream instead would carry the mark into a server function's rebuilt answer too, which must stay the app's 404 (`handler.test.ts` `app 404 kept`). Fix: `finalize.ts` `rewrapWatch` keeps the router's answer as the middleware chain gets it, and the chain's own answer takes the mark when it rewraps that one over the same body stream; a body is read only on a marked answer, so Bun's untyped Blob bodies stay unread (BUGS.md 238). Red first: `workers/handler.test.ts` `rewrapped unmatched`; `tests/workerd/handler.test.ts` `rewrapped unmatched` shows workerd keeps the stream through the rewrap.
273 [x] A non-GET `.response()` route could not accept a legitimate cross-site POST
273.1 [x] Review of BUGS 233, which gave a non-GET `.response()` route the server functions' Origin check. That made a foreign Origin 403 whatever the route did, with no way out: an auth-free OIDC form_post callback (Apple posts with `Origin: https://appleid.apple.com`), a SAML assertion consumer, or a public CORS POST were refused, and B44 covered only webhooks that send none of these headers. Decision (B44 revised, reviewer-approved): `.response(fn, { methods, crossOrigin: true })` drops the check for that one route, whose handler proves the request (a signature, OIDC `state` and `nonce`, a signed assertion); it adds no CORS header. A route that answers only GET or OPTIONS has no check to drop, and one whose chain, layouts included, has `.authenticate()`, `.authenticateOptional()`, or `.authorize()` would let a forged cross-site post act as the visitor, so both are refused: by the codegen scan (a non-literal `crossOrigin`, or GET only) and codegen's chain check, by `createServer` for a hand-built router, and when the route answers for a lazy module neither saw. A per-route flag keeps every other route closed, where a global allow-list would not. Red first: integration `hostile.test.ts` `cross origin` (403 without the flag, 200 with it and no `access-control-allow-origin`, each refusal), `scan.test.ts` `response cross origin`, `routes.test.ts` `cross origin chain`, `methods.test.ts` `cross origin rule`, `csrf.test.ts` `route cross origin`.
274 [x] The critical-CSS cache never hit under the default CSP, since its key held each request's nonce
274.1 [x] Review of BUGS 258. `critical.ts` keys its 64 kept matches on the shell's structure, which wrote every attribute's value, the `nonce="…"` of Solid's hydration script, the preload links, and the head tags included. The default CSP makes a fresh nonce per document, so the key never repeated: every request paid the full match and churned the map, and `kept by structure` passed only because its shells had no nonce. Fix: a `nonce` attribute counts by its presence, never its value, which no selector reads. Red first: `critical.test.ts` `kept across nonces`.
275 [x] A preloader that returned a union lost the members' own keys, and an optional key turned required
275.1 [x] Review of BUGS 248. `route-types.ts` `Merged<A, B>` mapped over `keyof A | keyof B`, and `keyof` of a union is only its shared keys, so a preloader returning `{ signedIn: true; user: string } | { signedIn: false }` made `ctx.preloaderContext.user` TS2339 even after narrowing on `signedIn`. A mapped type over that key union also dropped every `?`, so `{ note?: string }` became a required `note: string | undefined`. Fix: `Merged` distributes over each side's union members and merges each pair: the keys only one sets keep their modifiers (`Omit`), and a key both set is `A[K] & B[K]`, optional when both make it optional, so an incompatible pair is still that key's `never` alone. Red first: types app `checks-chain.ts` `preloader union narrows` (TS2339 on `user`) and `preloader optional stays optional` (TS2741 on `note`).
276 [x] A `.response()` options object the scan could not read passed the build silently
276.1 [x] Review of BUGS 208. The codegen scan read `methods` from a literal options object, but `.response(fn, { ...options })`, a computed `["methods"]` key, or another key beside it passed with no methods on the route node, so the route answered GET only, though `scan.test.ts` said any other shape fails. Fix: the options are an object literal holding only plain `methods` and `crossOrigin` keys, or the scan fails naming the file. Red first: `scan.test.ts` `response methods` with a spread, a computed key, and an unknown key.
277 [x] The wire corpus compared with toEqual, so its undefined-key and null-prototype rows could not fail
277.1 [x] Review of BUGS 264. `tests/structure/wire.test.ts` checked each value's trip with `toEqual`, which ignores undefined keys, array holes, and prototypes, the very things those rows exist for. A codec that went through JSON (dropping undefined keys and the null prototype) passed `undefined key` and `null prototype`; only `sparse array` failed, on its holes turning `null`. Fix: every comparison is `toStrictEqual`; under it the same lossy codec fails all three rows, and the real codec passes all 22.
278 [x] dev-hmr.test filtered the very warning that marks BUGS 254.4, on every load
278.1 [x] Review of BUGS 259. `dev-hmr.test.ts` drops Firefox's "Loading failed for the module with source" from its problems, since an edit's full reload cuts module loads short by design, but it dropped it on the first load too, where it is the 254.4 signal (a dependency Vite found only at runtime). Fix: the first load's problems must be empty but for Vite's own lines; only the edits' reloads may cut loads short. The 254.4 guard is `flare.test.ts` `client entry define`, which fails with 254.4 reverted (`CLIENT_LIBRARIES` emptied); the first-load check was never seen red, since this host's e2e copy, its dependency cache warm, does not reproduce the runtime discovery, and it stays to catch any other first-load fault. The case passes on node and bun dev, Chromium and Firefox, twice each.
279 [x] The router's locale cookieName was never checked, so a name holding `;` wrote a broken Set-Cookie
279.1 [x] Review of BUGS 185, which made the router's `locale` the one source of the locale config. The slot then took `cookieName` from `router.locale`, whose `localeConfig` (shared/router/features.ts) never checked it, while `i18n({ cookieName })` checked its own against the RFC 6265 token rule. `createRouter({ locale: { …, cookieName: "a; Domain=x" } })` therefore wrote that text into Set-Cookie. Fix: `localeConfig` throws `locale.cookieName` for a name that is no token, and the one `COOKIE_TOKEN` rule serves both. Red first: `features.test.ts` `locale cookie name`.
280 [x] Node's 405 for TRACE named the page methods on every path, where Bun's named the route's own
280.1 [x] Review of BUGS 209. Node's `Request` refuses CONNECT, TRACE, and TRACK, so the listener answers them with a 405 before the server runs, and it always sent `Allow: GET, HEAD, OPTIONS`. Bun builds the request and its router answers: a POST-only `.response()` route said `Allow: POST, OPTIONS`. RFC 9110 has a 405's Allow list the target's methods, so Bun's is the right one. Fix: the server exposes an internal channel (`methods.ts` `ALLOWED`, beside the prerender one) that gives a path's Allow from the match alone, with no route code loaded, and the Node listener uses it for a refused method; a server without the channel keeps the page set. Red first: integration `adapters.test.ts` `node trace` on `/echo`; `methods.test.ts` `allow channel`.
281 [x] A global middleware that threw `redirect()` answered 500
281.1 [x] Review of BUGS 267, whose row said a middleware's redirect follows the trailing-slash rule; that held only for route-chain middleware, which runs inside the loader run. A global `createMiddleware` throw reached the pipeline's `failed()`, which projected every throw as a 500. Users write `throw redirect(...)` in an auth guard, and returning `Response.redirect()` instead would hand a client navigation's data fetch a 3xx it follows to HTML. Decision: a thrown redirect anywhere outside a route answers as a loader's does, by the request's kind, through the one `exchange.ts` `redirectAnswer` the locale slot already used: a document gets the 3xx, a data request the one `x` frame, a prefetch a bare 404, a server function the envelope redirect its caller follows, all `private, no-store`, the Location written by `routeHref`. Any other throw stays the projected 500. Red first: integration `middleware.test.ts` `middleware throws redirect` (500 on all three).
282 [x] A crossOrigin route's handler could still act as the visitor through a server function
282.1 [x] Review of BUGS 273. A `crossOrigin` route skips the Origin check, and its chain may not authenticate, but a server function its handler calls ran in the request's scope: `callInRequest` has no CSRF check, and its `.authenticate()` resolved the scope's session, which ran `createServer({ authenticate })` on the cross-site request's cookies. A handler that called `deleteAccount()` (with `.authenticate()`) let an attacker's auto-submitted form delete the account whenever the cookie rode along (SameSite=None, or none in Firefox); before 273 that post was a 403. Decision (reviewer's ruling, B44 revised): a crossOrigin request's loaders and handler run in a scope with no identity (`ctx.ts` `withoutIdentity`): `authenticate` is never asked, a server function that needs a session throws a bug naming the route, a logged 500 rather than a 401 an attacker could probe, and `serverContext` runs on the request without its Cookie and Authorization, so `serverCtx()` holds no visitor. Red first: integration `hostile.test.ts` `cross origin identity`, with a session cookie (was 200 and the account deleted).
283 [x] The scan read the first of two equal `.response()` option keys, where JavaScript keeps the last
283.1 [x] Review of BUGS 276. `scan.ts` `valueOf` returns the first property with a key, but an object literal keeps the last, so in a `.jsx` route `{ methods: ["GET"], methods: ["POST"] }` put GET on the route node while the page answered POST, and `crossOrigin: false, crossOrigin: true` passed codegen's chain check as false while the route ran with true. Fix: a repeated key in the options fails the scan, naming the file. Red first: `scan.test.ts` `response methods` and `response cross origin` with a repeated key.
284 [x] Node's answer to TRACE skipped the canonical step and mounts that Bun's pipeline runs
284.1 [x] Review of BUGS 280. The Allow channel matched the raw path against the router alone. On Bun a TRACE runs the whole pipeline: a path the app's rewrite replaces is its 308, and a mounted prefix such as `/api` goes to the mount. On Node `/old` and `/api/x` were both a 405 naming the page set. Fix: the channel (now `refusedChannel`, `REFUSED`) runs the pipeline's own canonical step (`canonical-redirect.ts` `canonicalStep`, which `serveOne` now uses too) and answers its redirect; a mounted path, which a method Node cannot make a request of can never reach, is 501 Not Implemented, the status for a method the server cannot carry out; any other path keeps 280's 405 with its own Allow. Red first: integration `adapters.test.ts` `node trace` (405 on all three); `methods.test.ts` `refused channel`.
285 [x] An Image under a boundary that resolved after the first settle still took its sources off the page
285.1 [x] Review of BUGS 246. The fix placed a client-rendered image's sources at `onSettled`, taking that moment as the image being on the page. An image under a `<Loading>` whose data arrives later settles while the boundary still holds its content off the page, so it selected a source there; a dropped render's image, never inserted, then stays registered for good, as one in Solid's template document did. Fix: at settle the image takes its sources only when it is connected and in the page's own document; otherwise it is looked at again every 16 ms until it is, and never once its owner is disposed. Red first: `image.test.tsx` `placed after a boundary` (each source written while detached). The Image budget is 18722, 53 bytes more. `long session` and the docs grid on node prod Chromium and Firefox pass (384).
286 [x] A crossOrigin route's server functions saw a GET with no abort signal
286.1 [x] Review of BUGS 282. `ctx.ts` `withoutIdentity` built the identity-less request as `new Request(url, { headers })`, dropping the method and the abort signal: a server function called in a crossOrigin handler read `request.method` as GET, and a stream it served waits on `scope.request.signal`, so it never stopped when the client left. Fix: the request keeps the method and the client's signal and drops only Cookie and Authorization, with no body, which the handler's own request holds. Red first: integration `hostile.test.ts` `cross origin request`.
287 [x] A serverContext that threw on a crossOrigin route skipped the pipeline's failure answer
287.1 [x] Review of BUGS 282. The identity-less scope rethrew a serverContext throw synchronously, past the route's handling, so the 500 left without the app's `security` set (the default `X-Frame-Options: DENY` where the app set SAMEORIGIN), and a `redirect()` thrown there was a 500 too. Fix: `withoutIdentity` rejects, and `create.ts` answers that through `failed(exchange)` like any route's failure: the 500 with the app's set, a redirect its 3xx (BUGS.md 281). Red first: integration `hostile.test.ts` `cross origin context throws`.
288 [x] A global middleware's `redirect({ replace: false })` replaced the history entry on a client navigation
288.1 [x] Review of BUGS 281. `exchange.ts` `redirectAnswer` wrote `r: true` in a data request's `x` frame for every redirect, where a loader's writes `r: thrown.replace` (answers.ts), so a global middleware's `redirect({ replace: false })` replaced the entry instead of pushing one. Fix: the frame carries the thrown redirect's `replace`; the locale slot's own redirects still replace. Red first: integration `middleware.test.ts` `middleware throws redirect` with `replace: false`.
289 [x] Node's TRACE still differed from Bun on internal params, server-function paths, and a throwing rewrite
289.1 [x] Review of BUGS 284. The refused channel skipped the pipeline's first step and its server-function branch: `TRACE /page?_flare=bogus` was 405 on Node and 400 on Bun, and `TRACE /_flare/fn/<id>` was Node's page 405 where Bun's dispatch gives 404 for an unknown id and 405 `Allow: POST, OPTIONS` for a POST function. A rewrite that threw, the rewrite loop included, rejected the listener's pump, a bare 500 with no security headers. Fix: the channel answers in the pipeline's order: classify's status, then the canonical step (a throw is projected and answered with its status, through the finalize every answer takes), then a mount's 501, then a server function's lookup, then the route; it is async, since a server function loads lazily. Middleware still does not run, since it needs the request Node cannot make, and dx/adapters.md says so. Red first: `methods.test.ts` `refused channel`.
290 [x] An Image disposed before it settled polled for the page forever, and one in a frame never placed
290.1 [x] Review of BUGS 285. Solid runs a `ref` with no owner, so the `onSettled` the Image's ref called was the unowned one: it ignores disposal and boundaries and runs once the queue drains. An image disposed while its render still waited on data had already cleared its retry timer, then the late callback found it off the page and started a 16 ms loop that never ended, on an element nothing would show. And `ownerDocument === document` never placed an image in an iframe's or a popup's document. Fix: the settle callback is the component's own, on the element the ref captured: an owned one waits for its boundary to reveal and is dropped on disposal, so the loop and its timer go; the image takes its sources when it is connected in a document that has a window, which a frame's has and Solid's template document does not. Red first: `image.test.tsx` `disposed before settle` (a timer left running), and `document without a window`; `placed after a boundary` and `long session` still pass, and the docs grid on three projects (558).
291 [x] On Node a rewrite that threw on TRACE answered its own status, and a server function that failed to load escaped
291.1 [x] Review of BUGS 289. The refused channel answered a throwing step with the projected error's status, so a rewrite that threw `redirect()` was its 3xx with no Location and one that threw `notFound()` a 404 where the pipeline's `failed` answers 500; and `await serverFns[id]?.()` was unguarded, so a module that failed to load rejected the listener's pump, a bare 500. Fix: the steps after classify answer a throw as `failed` does, a redirect its Location and anything else the projected 500, the server function's load included. By the reviewer's ruling the locale slot stays out of the channel (TRACE is never a navigation) and dx/adapters.md says so; a finalized answer with the security set is the bar, not Bun's bytes. Red first: `methods.test.ts` `refused channel` with a step that redirects, one that throws `notFound()`, and a module that fails to load.
292 [x] A Link with a custom `isActive` did nothing when clicked on another page its predicate marks active
292.1 [x] Found by the seeded chaos run (plan 21 17.1), seed 1234567: on `/docs/styling` a click on the docs header's Docs link, `to` `/docs/routing` with `isActive={(location) => location.pathname.includes("/docs/")}`, left the URL where it was, in Chromium and Firefox alike. BUGS 148's rule, an active link stays put unless its hash moves the page, read `active()`, which a custom `isActive` decides; that predicate styles a section link, so on every other page of the section the link was dead. Fix: the stay-put rule compares the link's own URL with the location (the same pathname and search), whatever `isActive` says; `isActive` still sets the class and `aria-current`. Red first: `link.test.tsx` `section link`. The Link budget is 20823, 3 bytes more.
293 [x] A client Image that was not on the page when it settled never took its sources
293.1 [x] Review of BUGS 290. The owned settle callback runs once, so an image made before it was placed, `const img = <Image/>` held for a `<Show>` that opens later, a tab's resolved children inserted later, or a render into a container appended later, never got its sources: blank, never firing `onLoad`, stuck on the blur. Fix (reviewer's ruling, no per-image polling): such an image waits in one module-level set, and a single `MutationObserver` on the document, made when the first image waits, places every waiting image that is now on the page at each batch of mutations; the observer disconnects when the last one is placed or disposed, so nothing observes while nothing waits, and disposal still cancels. Red first: `image.test.tsx` `placed later` (a `<Show>` that opens later, and two containers appended one after the other), with `disposed while waiting`. The Image budget is 18850, 128 bytes more.
294 [x] On Node a TRACE whose rewrite threw a redirect it could not build escaped as a bare 500
294.1 [x] Review of BUGS 291. The refused channel built a thrown redirect's Location inside its catch, and `redirectLocation` itself throws for a route id whose params are missing, so that throw reached the listener's bare 500. Fix: building the Location is part of the failure answer, and its own throw is the projected 500 too. Red first: `methods.test.ts` `refused channel` with a redirect to a route whose param is missing.
295 [x] docs-perf split the first load's JavaScript from the navigation's before the idle prefetch landed
295.1 [x] Review of plan 21 15.6's commit. The docs app prefetches every route module once idle (`prefetch: { modules: "all" }`), and the test read the first load's scripts as soon as the page hydrated: on a slower runner the prefetched modules counted as navigation JavaScript, about 500 bytes inside that budget's room, or nowhere at all, hiding growth. Fix: the JavaScript is read once the app is idle and every `rel=prefetch` module is in the resource timings, as three numbers: the first load's (what it fetched but the prefetch), the prefetch's, and a navigation's, which with every module prefetched is 0. The longest-task and ratio bounds stay as set; their first CI runs are watched, and a flake widens them rather than retries.
296 [x] A click right after Back or Forward on a link to the page being left did nothing, or left the bar and the page apart
296.1 [x] Found by the seeded chaos run (plan 21 17.1), seed 7, and reproduced about one run in three: Forward from `/docs/routing` to the API page, then at once a click on the sidebar's `/docs/routing`: the URL stayed on the API page for good. During a Back or Forward the browser is already on its URL while the router's location still names the page left, until the step commits, and two places took that stale location for the current one: `link.tsx`'s stay-put rule (BUGS.md 148, 292) dropped the click as one on the current page, and `navigate.ts` `planOf` planned a navigation to that page as a same-URL revalidation, with no history entry, so the page could show `/a` while the bar showed `/`. Fix: the Link rule compares with the browser's own URL, and the navigator's "here" is the URL in the bar, `FlareHistory` `here()`: adopting, pushing, replacing, and every Back or Forward record it, the URL the browser shows for the entry it lands on, so an entry another document wrote behind a reload, and one a fragment link wrote, which is not Flare's, have it too; `adopt` and `pop` take that URL, and nothing falls back to the page on screen. The navigator fixture adopts its first entry with its URL, as the client boot does. Red first: `link.test.tsx` `click during a step`, `navigate-redirect.test.ts` `push during a step` and `push during a step after a reload`, history.test `here`; the e2e probe passed 4 of 4 after, 1 of 3 before. The Link budget is 20828 and createClient 56515.
297 [x] A link from a docs page to the admin root raised an uncaught error in Chromium
297.1 [x] Found by the seeded chaos run (plan 21 17.1), seed 42, once its final check stopped dropping every problem with "404" in it: a click on the auth page's `/admin` demo link, a full document load into the `_admin_` root, raised `Transition was aborted because of invalid state. ViewTransition opt-in disabled` in the new document. The main root renders `ViewTransitionCSS`, whose `@view-transition{navigation:auto}` opts the old document into a cross-document transition, and the admin root did not opt in; Chromium rejects the transition in the new document and reports it uncaught, and no page code can catch it: `pagereveal` carries no transition there, and handling the old document's `pageswap` transition does not stop it (a two-page probe with neither root Flare's raised it too). Fix: the admin root renders `ViewTransitionCSS` as the main root does, and dx/navigate.md says every root an app links between renders it. Red first: `docs-server.test.ts` `admin from the docs` on node-prod-chromium; green on Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit, dev and production.
298 [x] The README's install line and peer table left out two peers every app needs
298.1 [x] Found planning plan 21 5.5: `seroval` and `seroval-plugins` became required peers with the wire codec (B145), and the README's `bun add` line and peer table still named only the peers before them. npm 7+ and Bun install a missing peer on their own, so no install failed, but an app that pins its dependencies, or a reader of the table, got the wrong set. Fix: the line and the table name both (`>=1.6.7`, the wire codec), and the README states the runtime and TypeScript floors (Node 22.12 or later, B3; TypeScript 5.9 or later, tested on 5.9.3 and 7.0.2). Red first: readme.test `install names every peer`, which reads the line and the table against `packages/core/package.json`'s peers and ranges.
299 [x] The README's install line gave an app Solid 1.9 beside Solid 2's web package, so its vite config did not load
299.1 [x] Found by the consumer cell `readme install` (plan 21 5.5): the line named each peer bare, so a package manager installed its `latest` tag, and `solid-js@latest` is 1.9.17 while Flare needs `>=2.0.0-rc.15`. Bun put solid-js 1.9.17 beside @solidjs/web 2.0.0-rc.15, and loading the app's vite config failed with `The requested module 'solid-js' does not provide an export named 'creationStamp'`; npm resolved Solid 2 from the peers but warned `ERESOLVE overriding peer dependency` seven times, the root's `*` admitting no pre-release. Fix: the line gives each peer its declared range (`"solid-js@>=2.0.0-rc.15"`), and the README says why. Red first: readme.test `install names every peer` now reads each spec as `name@<declared range>`; then `readme install, bun` and `readme install, npm` install, build, and serve with no ERESOLVE.
300 [x] A feature used without its optional peer failed with a stub's missing export, or not at all
300.1 [x] Found by the consumer cells (plan 21 5.5), the minimal app installed without the five optional peers. i18n: `vite build` failed with `[MISSING_EXPORT] "match" is not exported by "__vite-optional-peer-dep:@formatjs/intl-localematcher:@lovrozagar/flare"`, Vite's stub for a missing optional peer, naming neither the other two peers nor a fix; a server built with the peers whose deploy dropped them died at start with Node's `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. image: `flare({ image })` built without sharp, which only an image import would load later. query: the build failed with six unresolved-import errors. No dx file documented any of it.
300.2 [x] Fix: `vite/plugin/peers.ts`'s `flare:peers` plugin runs first and, for the package's own import of an optional peer, resolves every peer of that feature; one missing, or answered with Vite's stub, fails the build and the dev server with one line per missing peer, `flare: <feature> needs <peer>. Install it: npm install <peer>@"<range>"`, the range the package declares. `flare({ image })` checks sharp when the config resolves. The probe: Rolldown calls the guard's `resolveId` for the i18n peers, which the Node server build externalized; a runtime guard in the matcher would have needed top-level await, since the locale slot is synchronous, so a build instead bundles the three i18n peers into the server (dev leaves them to Node, negotiator being CommonJS), and a built server no longer depends on them. dx/features.md Optional peers, dx/plugin.md, and the README say so. Red first: `peers.test.ts`, flare.test `client entry define` and `peer guard first`, and the consumer cells `i18n without peers`, `i18n peers removed after build`, `image without sharp`, and `query without solid-query`.
301 [x] In `vite dev`, a server entry that fails to load showed the error page but logged nothing to the terminal
301.1 [x] Found by the consumer cell `i18n without peers, dev` (plan 21 5.5): the i18n app without its peers answered `/` with a 500 whose error page carried the missing-peer message, and the dev server's terminal showed only Vite's startup lines. dx/plugin.md Development errors says the server logs the message and the stack. `vite/dev/handler.ts` `devFetch` reported the failure to the overlay and the page but never logged it, and the app's own logger does not exist when its entry did not load.
301.2 [x] Fix: `devFetch` logs a load failure as `[flare:load] <message>` and the stack through its `log`, which the installed handler points at Vite's logger (the vite/ side may not import server/'s logger.ts, and Vite's logger owns the dev terminal and honors `logLevel`). A route module that fails to load as a page renders was already logged, `[flare:render]` through the app's logger; its page is BUGS.md 302. Red first: handler.test `load error logged` and `load error installed`; the consumer cell `i18n without peers, dev` finds every peer on the page and in the terminal.
302 [x] A document whose route module failed to load answered an empty 500, in dev without its message
302.1 [x] Found checking BUGS 301's class (flare-e4's ask): the query app without `@tanstack/solid-query` under `vite dev` loads its entry, and its home page's module fails to load as `/` renders. The app's logger logged it (`[flare:render]`, message and stack), and the overlay payload went out, but the document was a 500 with an empty body, where dx/plugin.md says the body carries the message and stack before the shell. `server/lifecycle/create.ts` `loaded` answered any chain import failure with `answer(exchange, 500)`, the bare 500 every exit shares.
302.2 [x] Fix: a document whose chain did not load answers `renderFailed`, the built-in error page a render that failed before the shell answers: the message and stack in dev, the digest in production. A data request keeps the bare 500. Red first: create.test `lazy modules` (the broken document is `text/html` with `<pre>chunk missing`, its data request still a bare 500); the consumer cell `query without solid-query, dev` finds the peer on the page and in the terminal.
303 [x] A `vite build` run where NODE_ENV was `test` failed in prerender, and its server did not load
303.1 [x] Found by the consumer harness (plan 21 5.5): vitest's NODE_ENV=test leaked into a staged app's `vite build`, which failed as prerender imported the built server: `TypeError: Cannot set properties of undefined (setting 'server')`. A test runner's global setup or a CI step often exports NODE_ENV=test. Vite resolves its `development|production` placeholder by NODE_ENV, so the build resolved the bundled solid-js and @solidjs/web with `development`; their dev builds import `OBSERVE` from @solidjs/signals, which the build leaves external, and Node resolves an external package without `development`, to signals' prod build, which has no `OBSERVE`. The bundle paired dev builds with an external prod build.
303.2 [x] Fix: `vite/plugin/conditions.ts`'s `flare:conditions` sets each environment's conditions for a build with the placeholder as `production`, keeping the order and any condition the environment already has (the Cloudflare plugin's `workerd`); `vite dev` keeps Vite's. Forcing `production` over honoring NODE_ENV: a build's externals always resolve at run time with the runtime's default conditions, so only `production` keeps a bundle consistent with every external it reaches; honoring NODE_ENV would mean bundling every package with a dev/prod split, and the next one would break again. `import.meta.env.DEV` stays Vite's. Red first: the consumer cell `build under NODE_ENV=test`; conditions.test `build resolves production`, `keeps given conditions`, `dev untouched`. The repo's own builds, which vitest runs under NODE_ENV=test, now resolve `production` too: the full unit gate, src/vite and tests/integration under Bun, and 355 prod e2e cases across node, bun, and workers pass.
304 [x] A client that stopped a stream mid-pull logged a production render error with a digest
304.1 [x] Found in Mac run 15's server log (node-prod-chromium): `[flare:render] <digest> TypeError [ERR_INVALID_STATE]: Invalid state: Controller is already closed` from a stream's `pull`. A server function's `.stream()` body (`server/server-fn/stream.ts`) awaited the generator in `pull`; a client that stopped reading, as the docs playground's "stop the stream halfway" does, cancelled the body during that await, and the pull then enqueued into the cancelled stream. The throw landed in the pull's own catch, which projected it as the function's error, logged it with a digest, and enqueued again. `server/render/document.ts`'s body, `abort.ts`'s race, and the Bun adapter's `ended` had the same await-then-write shape: their writes after a cancel threw too, unseen, and Bun's ran `done` twice.
304.2 [x] Fix: each of the four streams sets a `cancelled` flag in its own `cancel` and returns before any write once it is set; the server function's catch does too, so a generator that throws after the cancel is never written or logged (Bun's catch needs none: after a cancel its read resolves done, never rejects). No write is wrapped in a try/catch, so a real double close still throws. The document body moves to `server/render/body-stream.ts`. The navigation data stream and the Node request body already guarded their writes. Red first, each a cancel that lands while a pull waits: stream.test (and `throw after cancel`, which a mutant without the catch's flag fails), body-stream.test (the case first ran in document.test), abort.test, and Bun serve.test `cancel during a pull`, the last four through `writes.fixture.ts`, which records every controller write that throws; a mutant without the body's flag fails.
305 [x] A router that turned prefetch off still warmed every route's code after load
305.1 [x] Found by plan 21 6.1's link.md review: `createRouter({ prefetch: false })`, `"intent"`, or `{ modules: "intent" }` still appended a `<link rel=prefetch>` for every unloaded route chunk once the page loaded and the browser idled (Chromium), or imported each route module (WebKit). `create-client.ts` always schedules `prefetcher.all`, and `prefetch.ts` `all()` read only the network, never the router's `modules`. Fix: `all()` runs only when the router's `modules` is `"all"`. Red first: prefetch.test `all only when the router says all`, five settings, with and without rel=prefetch support.
306 [x] `activeProps` and `inactiveProps` lost `class`, `tabindex`, and the other attributes Link writes itself
306.1 [x] Found by plan 21 6.1's link.md review: Link merged the state props before its own getters for `class`, `rel`, `aria-current`, `aria-disabled`, and `tabindex`, which won even when they returned `undefined`, so `activeProps: { class: "bold", tabindex: 0 }` rendered neither. Fix: the state's `class` joins `class` and `activeClass`/`inactiveClass`, and each attribute Link writes itself falls back to the state's own when Link leaves it unset; `disabled`'s `tabindex: -1` and an active link's `aria-current="page"` still win. The getters move to `ownAttributes`. Red first: link.test.tsx `state props attributes`.
307 [x] An empty string path param built another route's URL instead of throwing
307.1 [x] Found by plan 21 6.1's link.md review: `buildUrl({ to: "_root_/users/[id]", params: { id: "" } })` gave `/users/`, which served the users list, and `/users/[id]/edit` gave `/users//edit`, which 301s to `/users/edit` and rendered `[id]` with `id = "edit"`; an empty `locale` threw the bare `href`. Fix: an empty string path param, or an empty catch-all item, is missing, as `null` is: a required one throws naming the param and an optional one drops. Red first: href.test `empty param`.
308 [x] A bad path param value threw the bare word `param`, naming neither the key nor anything else
308.1 [x] Found by plan 21 6.1's link.md review: a dot segment, a lone surrogate, a raw slash, or a bad percent escape threw `Error("param")` (BUGS.md 156 chose the literal; link.md said "naming the param"), and the tests pinned `/^param$/`. Fix (flare-e4's ruling): it throws `param <key>`, `param id` say, naming the key and never the value, which can be request data a 500 logs; the missing-param throw (`Error(<key>)`) is unchanged. A follow-up to BUGS.md 156.2, whose message now names the key. Red first: href.test (raw slash, bad percent, emoji param, catch-all) and link.test `dot segment params` pin `param <key>`.
309 [x] A Link watched while the page showed its own URL with a hash never prefetched, even after the page changed
309.1 [x] Found by plan 21 6.1's link.md review: a persistent nav Link to `/about` mounted while the page is `/about#part` resolves as a hash move, and `prefetch.ts` `watch` bound no trigger for it; Link watches again only when its href changes, so after a navigation to `/` hovering it fetched nothing. Fix: `watch` binds a hash move's triggers by the route's match, and each trigger resolves its target when it fires, prefetching only a data target. Red first: prefetch.test `hash on screen, then another page` (no fetch on the hash page, one `/about?_flare=prefetch` after it).
310 [x] `<Link href>` turned `mailto:`, `tel:`, a `#fragment`, and a relative path into a dead `href="#"`, silently
310.1 [x] Found by plan 21 6.1's link.md review: Link classified its href with `href()`, which accepts only a path and an http(s) URL, so `mailto:a@b.example`, `tel:+1`, `#top`, `?q=1`, `about`, and `""` all rendered `href="#"` and a click did nothing; link.md named only the dangerous schemes. Fix (flare-e4's ruling): Link classifies its own href. `mailto:`, `tel:`, and `sms:`, which the browser hands to another app and which carry no script, and a `#fragment`, an in-page anchor, render as written and their click is the browser's. A relative path, a bare `?query`, or `""`, which cannot resolve alike on the server and the client, render `#` and throw in dev pointing to `to` (builder.md's dev-throw precedent). `href()` and `redirect({ href })` are unchanged. Red first: link.test.tsx `handed-off and fragment hrefs`; `dangerous` still renders `#` with no throw.
311 [x] A hash or shallow move left a pending data navigation running, and it later committed over the move
311.1 [x] Found by plan 25's navigate.md review (D1): `navigate.ts` sent a hash or shallow target to `inPlace` and returned before `requests.start()`, so nothing aborted the request in flight. From `/`, a held `navigate({ to: "_root_/about" })`, then `navigate({ to: "_root_/", hash: "x" })` (or a shallow search), showed `/#x`, and when the fetch landed the URL and screen became `/about`. navigate.md says only the latest may commit. Fix: an in-place move that goes ahead, past its blocker, calls `requests.cancel()`, which aborts the newest pending request, never the page's own (its deferred values stream on) and never a fill, which `onPage` lands onto the page as the move left it (plan 21 3.11). Red first: navigate-shallow.test `in-place move supersedes a pending one` (the signal was not aborted); requests.test `cancel`.
312 [x] A hash-only navigation moved the URL without asking the blocker
312.1 [x] Found by plan 25's navigate.md review (D2): `steps.ts` `inPlace` asked the blocker only for a shallow target, so with `useBlocker(() => false)` registered, `navigate({ to: "_root_/", hash: "x" })` pushed `/#x` and called the blocker 0 times; navigate.md lists only `"initial"`, popstate, and a same-URL revalidation as skipping it. Fix: every in-place move asks the blocker before the history write, except a Back or Forward step, which the browser already took. Red first: navigate-shallow.test `hash blocked` (0 calls, `#x` pushed); its restore half pins that a pop onto a hash entry still runs no blocker.
313 [x] The blocker's `current` and `next` always carried `params: {}`
313.1 [x] Found by plan 25's navigate.md review (D3): `blocker.ts` built each side as `{ ...location, params: {} }`, and `create-client.ts` passed `params: {}` to `beforeUnload`, so a navigation to `/posts/8` from `/posts/7` gave the blocker `{}` on both sides, where navigate.md documents the page's params. Fix: `placeIn(router, location)` reads each side's raw params from the router's match of its pathname, an absent optional one left out; navigate, an in-place move, and `beforeunload` all use it. Red first: leave.test `blocker params` (beforeunload, a data navigation, and a hash move through the real booted client).
314 [x] A `state` that cannot be cloned threw only after the commit, leaving the screen on the new page and the URL on the old
314.1 [x] Found by plan 25's navigate.md review (D4): the browser clones `state` inside `history.pushState`, which runs in `land` after the store commit, so `navigate({ to: "_root_/about", state: () => 1 })` in Chromium showed `/about` while `location.pathname` stayed `/` and the call rejected with `DataCloneError`; a hash move had the same order. Fix: `navigate` structured-clones `state` once on entry, so one that cannot be cloned throws before the request, the commit, and the history write. navigate.md's `state` row cited history.test `state`, which does not exist; it now cites history.test `adopt`, which stores it, and this case. Red first: navigate-failure.test `state not cloneable` (a data navigation and a hash move each reject with no fetch, the page and URL unchanged).
315 [x] Refreshing an open overlay rebuilt its child and page: focus jumped to its first control and an input lost its caret
315.1 [x] Found by plan 25's navigate.md review (D5): `showOverlay` commits a new `Overlay` object on every landing, and `InterceptOutlet`'s memo read `overlay()` and called the child again under a new owner each time; the old trap's cleanup returned focus to the opener and the new trap moved it to the first focusable. So `revalidate()` or a `replace` navigation inside the overlay, with focus on its second control, moved focus to the first and replaced the input. navigate.md says the overlay is refreshed in place. Fix: the outlet builds the child once per overlay route and label, and every value it hands the child and the page (`render`, `params`, `search`, `backgroundLocation`, `match.loaderData`, the page's props and its hooks' match) reads the overlay on screen through an accessor, as a page on screen reads its data. Red first: intercept-outlet.test `overlay refresh keeps child` (built twice, focus moved).
316 [x] Tab left an open overlay when its last focusable was a disabled Link or a hidden element
316.1 [x] Found by plan 25's navigate.md review (D6): the trap's `FOCUSABLE` list matched `a[href]` and `button:not([disabled])` even with `tabindex="-1"` or `hidden`, and `wrap` takes over only at the list's ends, so from the second-to-last entry the browser's own Tab skipped the unreachable last one and left the overlay; a disabled `Link` renders `<a href tabindex="-1" aria-disabled="true">`. Seen in Chromium 156 and Firefox. Fix: the trap counts only what Tab reaches: `tabIndex` at least 0, nothing under `[hidden]` or `[inert]`, nothing `checkVisibility()` reports hidden where the browser has it, and `:disabled` for controls, which covers a disabled fieldset. Red first: overlay-focus.test `unreachable skipped`; `edges` now closes its last trap, whose listener had prevented every later Tab.
317 [x] Inside an overlay, `useMatch` with the page's own id returned the background page's match id
317.1 [x] Found by plan 25's navigate.md review (D7): `useMatch` built `id` from `LocationContext`, the store's location, which an open overlay keeps on the background, so inside `state.match.render` of `/photos/1` opened over `/`, `useMatch({ from: "_root_/photos/[id]" }).id` was `&`, the background's; navigate.md says the page's own hooks read the overlay. Fix: the overlay's page match carries the overlay's location (`OutletMatch.location`), and `useMatch` reads the URL of the match its `from` names: the overlay's for the page, the store's for a background route. The navigation fixture now provides the router and the store's location, as the client's app tree does. Red first: intercept-outlet.test `use match in overlay` (`card:&` where `id=1&x=2` was due).
318 [x] Back from an entry the browser wrote, a hash typed in the bar, restored no scroll
318.1 [x] Found by plan 25's navigate.md review (D8): `history.pop` returned on a `null` state before it moved `current`, so the next Back landed on the index `current` still held, with no step, and `restore` never ran: neither the saved point nor the top applied. Fix: a popstate with no state is the entry the browser just wrote on top of the one on screen; it is stamped `{ index: current + 1 }` with no step to replay, since the browser went to the fragment itself, so a Back off it is a step that restores. Found while proving it in the browsers: Firefox and WebKit scroll to the typed fragment before the popstate runs (Chromium after), so the popstate's save kept the fragment's position for the entry left; the client now keeps the position the last frame showed (`scroll.ts` `lastFrame`, the scroll event waits for the next frame) and saves that for a stateless popstate. State another script wrote is still not Flare's. Red first: history.test `browser entry` (null returned) and history-client harness `back from a typed hash`, red in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit against the old code; typed-hash.test pins the saved position.
319 [x] With no page title, the arrival announcement read the first `h1` while focus went to `main h1`
319.1 [x] Found by plan 25's navigate.md review (D9): `arrival.ts` focused `main h1`, else the first `h1`, but announced `doc.querySelector("h1")`, so a page with a site name's `<header><h1>` and no title focused the post's heading and read out "Site name". Fix: one `headingOf` serves both, so the heading read is the one that took focus. Red first: arrival.test `announced heading is the focused one`.
320 [x] A 204 or 201 data answer painted "Request failed"
320.1 [x] Found by plan 25's navigate.md review (D10): `request.ts` sent every status other than 200 to `failedAt`, so a 204 with no body, or a 201, from a proxy or middleware committed `root[Something went wrong]` with `{ status: 500, message: "Request failed" }` and pushed the URL; navigate.md paints `Request failed` only at or above 400. Fix: a 2xx that is not a 200 data body loads the URL as a document, under the reload guard, and commits nothing; a 200 with no body does too. A superseded one loads nothing. The status triage moves to `answered`. Red first: navigate-failure.test `other 2xx loads document`.
321 [x] `revalidate: true` with a move of the hash alone sent no request
321.1 [x] Found by plan 25's navigate.md review (D11): `fromLocation` returned a hash target whenever only the hash differed, and `hashAgain` read `revalidate` only for the hash already current, so on `/`, `navigate({ to: "_root_/", hash: "x", revalidate: true })` moved to `/#x` with 0 fetches, and from `/#x` a revalidating navigation to `/` did too; navigate.md's `revalidate` row promises a refetch. Fix: the target context carries the call's `revalidate`, and a revalidating move is a data target, never a hash move; the page lands and then goes to the hash. A revalidation that moves the hash writes its entry (`Plan.write`), where a same-URL one writes none. A server function's same-origin redirect, which revalidates, now refetches before it goes to its hash. `callTarget` and `urlTarget` in target.ts hold the call-to-target steps, keeping navigate.ts under its caps; create-client.test's server function cases move to server-fn-boot.test.ts at its 500-line limit. Red first: navigate-shallow.test `revalidate with a hash move` (0 fetches).
322 [x] A shallow call kept the reserved `_flare` keys in the page match's search
322.1 [x] Found by plan 25's navigate.md review (D12): `resolveTarget` built the URL from `withoutInternalKeys(input.search)` but gave the shallow target the raw `input.search`, which `inPlace` writes into the page match, so `navigate({ to: "_root_/", shallow: true, search: { q: "1", _flare: "data" } })` wrote `/?q=1` and a page search of `{ q: "1", _flare: "data" }`; navigate.md strips reserved params. Fix: the stripped search serves both. Red first: navigate-shallow.test `shallow strips reserved keys`.
323 [x] `useNavigationProgress` took a `trickle` or `minDisplay` above 2^31 - 1 ms, and its timers fired at once
323.1 [x] Found by plan 25's navigate.md review (D14): the options were checked only as integers above a floor and went straight to `setInterval` and `setTimeout`, which fire at once past 2^31 - 1 ms (Chromium fired `setTimeout(fn, 2 ** 31)` after 0 ms and Firefox after 1 ms; `setInterval` ticked 56 and 53 times in 200 ms), so `{ trickle: 2 ** 31 }` crept to `0.994` within 100 ms and `{ minDisplay: 2 ** 31 }` held for no time. Fix: each option throws above 2^31 - 1, as below its floor. Red first: progress.test `timer ceiling`.
324 [x] `ViewTransitionCSS` wrote invalid CSS for a negative or non-finite `duration`, so the rule set no duration
324.1 [x] Found by plan 25's navigate.md review (D15): `props.duration ?? 150` was interpolated unchecked, so `duration={-1}`, `{NaN}`, and `{Infinity}` wrote `-1ms`, `NaNms`, and `Infinityms`, which `CSS.supports("animation-duration", …)` rejects in Chromium 156 and Firefox; the browser drops the declaration. Fix: a duration that is not a finite number at least 0 throws, naming the prop, as `useNavigationProgress` rejects its own bad numbers; `1.5` and `0` stay valid. Red first: view-transition-css.test `duration checked`.
325 [x] BUGS 318's stamp read every stateless popstate as a fragment entry ahead of the one left, and its comment said another script's state is not Flare's
325.1 [x] Found by flare-e4's review of BUGS 318: a Back onto an entry another script pushed with `history.pushState(null, "", url)` also arrives with no state, and it is behind the entry left, yet it is stamped `current + 1`, so the next step's replay count starts from a wrong index; `pop`'s comment still said state another script wrote is not Flare's. The two cases look the same in a popstate.
