Ruling (option a): Flare owns its document's history, and an entry pushed elsewhere with no state reads as a fragment entry; navigate.md says so, `pop`'s comment is corrected, and history.test `script entry` pins what a Back onto one does (stamped as the next index, then a Back replays from it). No behavior changes; the Navigation API's entry index (option b) was not taken, since Firefox and Safari lack a stable one and it would track a second index on every step.
326 [x] A mount received control characters and an escaped backslash after the strip, 200 where server.md says 400
326.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S1): `mount.ts` checked only `rest.startsWith("//")`, whose comment said a control character takes that form, but a parsed URL percent-encodes it, so `GET /api/%00`, `/api/%01x` (and the raw `\u0001`), `/api/%0A`, `/api/%7Fx`, and `/api/%5C` reached the mount with 200; the cited `strip` case asserted the opposite of the doc. Fix: after the strip, an escape of a control character (`%00` to `%1F`, `%7F`) or a backslash (`%5C`), in either case, is 400 like `//`; any other escape reaches the mount. Red first: mount.test `strip`, rewritten to the doc.
327 [x] A mount prefix with a dot segment, a space, or a character outside ASCII passed the startup check and never matched
327.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S2): `assertPrefix` compared the prefix with `canonicalPath`'s output, which keeps dot segments, spaces, and non-ASCII characters, so `/a/./b`, `/a b`, and `/ä` were accepted while a request path, parsed and normalized, is `/a/b`, `/a%20b`, or `/%C3%A4`: every such mount gave the router's 404. Fix: a prefix must also equal its own parsed pathname, so these throw at startup. A non-ASCII prefix cannot be spelled under case-insensitive routing at all (its escapes' hex would have to be both upper and lower case); that rule is left as it stands. Red first: mount.test `root prefix`.
328 [x] `retireServiceWorker` accepted a dot-segment path that no request can match
328.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S3): `checkRetired` accepted `/./sw.js` and `/../sw.js`, while `isRetired` compares with the parsed request pathname, which never holds a dot segment, so a request for `/./sw.js` arrived as `/sw.js` and the retiring script was never served; the setting was accepted and silently had no effect. Fix: a path must also equal its own parsed pathname, so a dot segment, raw or escaped (`%2e`), throws at startup. Red first: retire-worker.test `checked`.
329 [x] `authenticate` returning `undefined` type-checked with no registered `auth`, and at runtime was an unlogged 500
329.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S4): `options.ts` typed the session as `unknown` when `FlareRegister` declares no `auth`, so `() => undefined` satisfied `unknown | null`, and `gate.ts` mapped `undefined` to a 500 without an error, so nothing was logged (0 `console.error` calls in dev and production, an empty body for the document and the data request). server.md says returning `undefined` is a type error. Fix: with no auth registered the session is `{}` (any value but `undefined` or `null`), so `() => undefined` is a type error; at runtime the 500 carries an Error, `authenticate returned undefined: return the session, or null for no session`, which the server projects and logs. Test helpers that typed `authenticate` as returning `unknown` now return `{} | null`. Red first: gate.test `authenticate undefined` (no error, and an unused `@ts-expect-error`); integration auth.test `authenticate undefined logged`, red against the old gate.
330 [x] A route's CSP directives replaced the server's default directives instead of adding to them, so `'self'` was lost
330.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S6): `apply.ts` `mergedCsp` merged a route's directives only into the ones a server object names, and with no server CSP it returned the route object alone; `csp.ts` `applyUser` then replaced each named default outright. So with no server `security`, a page's `script-src: ["https://cdn.example"]` sent `script-src https://cdn.example 'nonce-…'` with `'self'` gone, and the same over a server that names only `img-src`; the cited `route csp merge` passed only because its server listed `'self'`. server.md says route directives add to the server's. Fix: `buildCsp` takes the route's directives apart (`add`): the server's apply over the defaults first, then each route source joins its list once; a list that is only `'none'` takes the route's sources instead, `style-src` without `style-src-elem` adds to the element list, and a flag takes the route's value. Red first: apply.test `route csp adds to defaults`.
331 [x] A route's CSP directives over a server string CSP threw the server's policy away
331.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S7): with a server string such as `default-src 'none'`, a route's `{ "img-src": ["https://i"] }` was merged over Flare's defaults instead, sending `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'nonce-…'; … img-src https://i`, looser than the server's policy; server.md says route directives add to the server's and only a route string or `false` replaces it. Fix: `addToPolicy` adds the route's directives into the server's string: a directive it names takes each source once (a list that is only `'none'` takes the route's instead), one it does not name is appended, and a flag follows the route; the string carries no nonce, as before. Red first: apply.test `route csp over server string`.
332 [x] The stored-body nonce rewrite edited page text that held ` nonce="<request nonce>"`
332.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S8): `cache/nonce.ts` matched `\snonce="…"` anywhere in the body, and Solid writes a `"` in a text node unescaped, so an `isr` page rendering `<pre> nonce="<nonce>"</pre>` stored the entry's sentinel in that text, a hit wrote its own nonce there, and a hit under a string CSP deleted the text; server.md says other text is left as it is. Fix: the rewrite walks each start tag's attributes in order and changes only a tag's own quoted `nonce` attribute, so text and another attribute's value that spell one stay as rendered; a hit's plain nonce still goes over the sentinel in one pass, since the sentinel stands only where the write put it. Red first: nonce.test `text`.
333 [x] `X-Powered-By` reached the answer from `.headers()`, a `.response()` page, a mount, and a middleware
333.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S9): the request's security set is laid over an answer's headers, and with no `X-Powered-By` configured it held none, so a production server with no `security` answered `x-powered-by: Express` from a page's `.headers()`, `PHP/8` from a `.response()` page, and the mount's or middleware's own value; server.md says a configured string is the only way the header appears. Fix: where the set is laid over an answer (`exchange.ts` `typed` and `secure`), an `X-Powered-By` the set does not hold is deleted. Red first: integration ingress.test `powered by only from security` (all five sources, unset and configured).
334 [x] The default `frame-ancestors` did not restate a padded `SAMEORIGIN` or an `ALLOW-FROM` the answer sends
334.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S10): `csp.ts` `framedBy` compared the configured value untrimmed, so `"X-Frame-Options": " SAMEORIGIN"`, which Headers sends trimmed, gave `frame-ancestors 'none'`, and `ALLOW-FROM https://p.example`, sent as given, did too, where server.md says the CSP restates the answer's X-Frame-Options. Fix: the value is read as the header sends it, trimmed and in any case; `ALLOW-FROM <url>` is restated as that URL's origin, and one that does not parse stays `'none'`. Red first: apply.test `frame follows x-frame-options`.
335 [x] A Permissions-Policy origin with a backslash or a character outside ASCII made a malformed header instead of throwing
335.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S11): `csp.ts` `allow` rejected only a quote, whitespace, and `;`, so `camera: ["https://a\\b"]` sent `camera=("https://a\b")` and `camera: ["https://ä.example"]` sent `camera=("https://ä.example")`, both with 200; an RFC 8941 string holds printable ASCII and uses `\` only to escape, and a browser drops a field that fails to parse, which lifts every default-`false` feature. Fix: an origin holds only printable ASCII other than a quote, a space, `;`, and a backslash; anything else throws, naming the feature. Red first: csp.test `origin quote`.
336 [x] An `exact` middleware filter was bypassed by the trailing-slash form of the same page
336.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S12): `filters.ts` compared `exact` with the raw pathname and the route path, neither with its trailing slash trimmed, so with `exact: ["/admin"]` blocking a page, `GET /admin/` served it with 200 under the default `"preserve"`, and under `trailingSlash: "always"` `/admin` redirected to the open `/admin/`, so the block never fired; `exact: ["/admin/"]` left `/admin` open. server.md's SecurityConfig section tells apps to put access rules in middleware. Fix: `exact` compares with one trailing slash trimmed from both sides, the root aside. Red first: filters.test `exact`; integration middleware.test `filter exact trailing slash` (every mode, both spellings), red against the old filter.
337 [x] An app's answer replaced Flare's `flare-build`, `flare-render`, and `flare-cache`
337.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S13): `finalize.ts` set `flare-build` and `flare-render` first and then copied the stage's headers, the app's Response's among them, over them, so a `.response()` page returning `flare-build: evil`, `flare-render: isr`, and `flare-cache: hit` was answered with exactly those, a mount over an upstream Flare app kept the upstream's build id, and a middleware's answer did the same; server.md says finalize sets these. Fix: finalize sets the three after copying the answer's headers, and drops one it does not set; a stored page's `flare-cache` verdict reaches finalize as a stage field (`cache`), as `render` does, instead of a header. Red first: integration ingress.test `flare headers are flare's`.
337.2 [x] Found by the e2e run after 13514503: finalize runs again on an answer Flare built when it passes out through the middleware chain (`passed`, `answerFrom`), and that pass has no `render` or `cache` field, so `own()` deleted the `flare-render` and `flare-cache` Flare had set: docs-cache `ssg seed` and `isr stored`, navigation `stored hit hydrates`, and static `ssg served` failed on node-prod Chromium and Firefox. Fix: finalize remembers the render mode and cache verdict of each answer it builds (a WeakMap, so no `flare-` header is read back), `carryMark` carries them to a dev rewrap, and `answerFrom` hands them back to the next finalize; an answer the app built still drops its own. Red first: integration ingress.test `flare headers pass a middleware` (null and null against 13514503); the four e2e specs pass again, 88 of 88.
337.3 [x] Found by the full unit gate on T1 of plan 25's types.md review: two more paths make a new answer from one Flare built, a HEAD (`methods.ts` `applyMethod`) and the locale slot's private rewrite of a negotiated document or a cookie (`locale-slot.ts` `withLocale`), and neither handed on the remembered render mode and cache verdict, so both left with no `flare-render` or `flare-cache`. Fix: each passes `ownStage` of the answer it rewrites to finalize. Red first: i18n middleware.test `negotiated private` (null and null against 6b657afd), and methods.test `HEAD keeps flare headers`.
338 [x] `server.fetch` rejected, with nothing logged, when a thrown value's conversion to text threw
338.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S16): `bounded` and the stage-level `failed` call `projectError`, whose `describe` calls `String(thrown)` (and in dev `devErrorPayload` does too); a value such as `{ [Symbol.toPrimitive]() { throw v } }` thrown from a loader, a mount's fetch, a middleware, or `serverContext` made that throw inside the boundary, so `server.fetch` rejected with it and nothing was logged, and on Node the client got a bare 500 with no `flare-build` and no security headers. server.md says `server.fetch` never rejects. Fix: `projectError` first replaces a non-Error whose conversion throws with `Error("a thrown value that cannot be printed")`, so the boundary logs it once and answers its 500, or a data request its error frame. Red first: integration fetch-boundary.test `unprintable throw` (all five sources); projector.test `unprintable thrown` (dev and production).
339 [x] The error boundary's 500 lacked `Content-Security-Policy` and `Permissions-Policy`
339.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S17): `bounded` answers `finalize({ status: 500, body: "", url })`, whose `copySecurity` read `defaults.ts`, a set without CSP and Permissions-Policy, so a boundary 500 (a server-function module that fails to import, a navigation's unserializable data) carried neither, where a throwing middleware's stage 500 carried both; server.md says the boundary's 500 has the default security headers. Fix: finalize's `url` path writes `buildCsp`'s whole default set, CSP and Permissions-Policy included, for the boundary's 500 and the Node listener's own answers. Red first: integration fetch-boundary.test's cases now read both headers (red on `server function module` and `data serialization`).
340 [x] A proxied upload with `Expect: 100-continue` was a 500 on Node
340.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S19): `proxy.ts` forwarded `expect`, and Node's fetch (undici) rejects any request that carries it (`NotSupportedError: expect header not supported`), so `POST /api/up` with `expect: 100-continue`, which curl sends for a large upload by default, through a proxy mount behind a real Node listener answered 500, logged `fetch failed`; Bun accepts it. server.md says the body is streamed to the upstream. Fix: `expect` joins the hop-by-hop set, since it asks this hop to confirm the upload. Red first: proxy.test `hop by hop dropped`; integration middleware.test `proxy` through the real Node fetch (500 before), green under Node and Bun.
341 [x] The adapters' own 400s and the Node listener's 500 carried no `flare-build` and no security headers
341.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S20): the Node listener answered a target or Host it cannot route with `writeHead(400); end()` and a failure before the headers with a bare 500, and Bun answered an invalid Host with `new Response(null, { status: 400 })`, so a raw `GET //evil`, `Host: a?b`, or Bun's `host: "a b"` got only `Date`, `Connection`, and `Transfer-Encoding`, where server.md says every answer names its build. Fix: each is a `finalize` answer with no body, its URL read as plain http since the request's may be unknown, so it carries `flare-build` and the default security set, CSP and Permissions-Policy included. The listener's own-answer cases move to listener-answers.test.ts, since listener.test.ts reached its 500-line limit. Red first: listener-answers.test `fetch throws`, `bad target`; bun/serve.test `hostile host`.
342 [x] The Node listener never handed on the socket address, so `clientIp` was `""` where server.md says the socket address is used
342.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S21): `listener.ts` called `toRequest({ url, headers, trustProxy })` with no `socketAddress`, and its request type had no socket, so `toRequest` returned `clientIp: ""` for `trustProxy` of 0 and for fewer forwarded entries than it trusts. Nothing in v1 reads `clientIp` yet (its context field is in the Planned table), so no answer showed it. Fix: the listener builds its adapted request through `adaptedOf`, which hands on `req.socket.remoteAddress`. Red first: listener-answers.test `socket address`.
343 [x] A HEAD on an unmatched path returned the whole not-found document from `server.fetch`
343.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S22): `create.ts` sent an unmatched GET or HEAD to the not-found render and returned it without `applyMethod`, which holds the HEAD body cancel, so `server.fetch(new Request("/nope", { method: "HEAD" }))` answered 404 with the full 1057-byte render and ran it to completion; Node dropped the body on the wire and Bun sent its content length with no body. A matched page's HEAD, and `/nope?_flare=data`, already answered with no body. Fix: the unmatched answer goes through `applyMethod` too, keeping its unmatched mark. Red first: integration hostile.test `head on an unmatched path`.
344 [x] Three adapter lines called `console.error` directly, against the rule that only the logger calls `console` on the server
344.1 [x] Found by plan 25's server.md review (S23): `bun/serve.ts` (`waitUntil rejected`) and `node/listener.ts` (`the request failed`, `waitUntil rejected`) wrote with `console.error`, where server.md and 00-rules say only `server/lifecycle/logger.ts` may call `console` and every other module logs through it. Fix: each logs through `createLogger({ dev: false }).error`, which writes an error in dev and production alike, under the same `[flare:node]` and `[flare:bun]` tags; a structure test fails on any other `console.` in src/server and src/adapters. Red first: structure logger.test `console only in the logger` (the three lines).
345 [x] The types app fixture never checked its generated register, so a broken import there turned a route into `any` while the compile passed
345.1 [x] Found by plan 25's types.md review (T13): `tests/types/app.test.ts` writes the generated register as `_gen/types.gen.d.ts`, and the fixture's tsconfig inherits `skipLibCheck: true` from tsconfig.base.json, so tsc never checked that file: an import in it that does not resolve made its route `any`, and `app compiles` still saw only its deliberate `checks-unused` error. Every other type case leans on this compile, so it is fixed first. Fix: the check's tsconfig sets `skipLibCheck: false`; the fixture compiles with no new error (0.9 s more). Red first: app.test `register checked` (a register with a broken import reported nothing).
346 [x] A `.input()` given a const object typed neither side in the register, so `Link` and `useSearch` took the raw record while the page's render had the validated type
346.1 [x] Found by plan 25's types.md review (T1): codegen's `scan.ts` `inputSides` read only an object literal written inside `.input()`, so `const validators = { searchParams }` then `.input(validators)` left `routeSearchParams` without the route: a `Link` to it needed no `search`, and `useSearch` returned `Record<string, string | string[]>`. Fix: an Identifier argument resolves through the module's top-level consts (through `as`, `satisfies`, `!`, and parentheses), and a spread of such a const adds its keys; an import or a call result still adds nothing, which types.md now says. Red first: scan.test `input sides` (a const, a spread const), and the types app's `_root_/vsearch` page with checks.ts `search validated through a const` (TS2322 and TS4111, and an unused `@ts-expect-error`).
346.2 [x] Replaced by BUGS 349 (flare-e4's review): the scan's reading of the `.input()` argument, a literal, a const, a spread of one, still missed an imported object and a factory's result, the common forms. 349 drops it: every route whose chain calls `.input()` gets its register entries from its own type, whatever the argument. A probe settled the gate: reading every route's type looped through `moved`'s redirect with params (TS7022), and reading only the routes that call `.input()` did not.
347 [x] An app's generated register was a `.d.ts`, which the README's `skipLibCheck: true` never checks, so a broken import in it turned a route into `any` with no error
347.1 [x] Found by flare-e4's review of BUGS 345: 345 made the types fixture check its register with `skipLibCheck: false`, but codegen wrote every app's register as `src/_gen/types.gen.d.ts`, and README.md's tsconfig, like tsconfig.base.json, sets `skipLibCheck: true`, so in any app that follows the README tsc skipped the file: an import there that did not resolve made that route `any` while the compile passed, T13's hole in every app. Fix: codegen writes the register as the module `src/_gen/types.gen.ts` (its `export {}` keeps it an augmentation), which skipLibCheck does not skip; the three committed e2e app registers, the tsconfig exclude, codegen.test and write.test, files.tsv, README.md, decision B74, and the docs that name the file follow. Red first: app.test `register checked` with `skipLibCheck: true` appended a broken import and saw no TS2307 against the `.d.ts`.
348 [x] A validator whose output is a union with no key in common forbade `params` or `search` on a `Link` instead of requiring it
348.1 [x] Found by plan 25's types.md review (T2): `register.ts` read a target's params and search keys with `keyof` of the output, which for a union keeps only the keys every member shares, so `{ n: number } | { s: string }` and `{ a: string } | { b: string }` read as no keys: the target took `params?: never` and `search?: never`, and every `Link` to the page failed to compile. Fix: the keys are read member by member (`Keys<T>`, distributive), and `search` is optional when `{}` satisfies it (an all-optional object, a union with an all-optional member, the raw record), required otherwise; `Empty` and `RequiredKeys` go. types.md gains the `union output` row. Red first: the types app's `_root_/either/[id]` page and checks-union.ts (four TS2322, each `not assignable to type 'never'`, against e66d8822's register.ts).
349 [x] An `.input()` given an imported object or a factory's result left the route's targets raw: `Link`, `navigate`, `buildUrl`, `useParams`, and `useSearch` took the bracket string and the raw record while the page's own callbacks had the validated types
349.1 [x] Found by flare-e4's review of BUGS 346: codegen decided which sides a route validates from `.input()`'s argument, which it can read only as text, so `import { input } from "./validators"`, a namespace member, a `paths` alias, and `paged(...)` added no register entry. Probe (.tmp/grunt/probe346/modes.ts), types app plus loop pages, strict, skipLibCheck off: the AST register 816,259 instantiations; reading every route's type 833,233 and `moved`, whose loader redirects with params, loops (TS7022, TS7024, TS2502); reading only routes whose chain calls `.input()` 820,502 and nothing loops outside BUGS 350's class. Fix: the scan records only that the chain calls `.input()`, as v0's generator did; types-gen gives each such route a `routeSearchParams` and a `routeParams` entry, `SearchOutput` and `ParamsOutput` of its own type; `InferParams` keeps the brackets when the route's `.input()` has no params side, read from the route's type-only field, so a `never` output stays `never` and BUGS 348's union bug is not hidden. The literal and const reading of 346 goes; the docs app's register gains three entries. Red first: the types app's `imported/[id]`, `spaced/[id]` (a namespace member), `aliased/[id]` (an `@/` alias, with the fixture's `paths`), `sorted/[id]` (a default import, search only), and `made/[id]` (a factory) with checks-input.ts read raw against 3061aebf (TS2322, TS4111, unused expect-errors); `void/[id]` pins that a `never` output is not the brackets; types-gen.test `input call` (no ParamsOutput entry for a search-only literal against 3061aebf).
350 [x] A loader that redirects with params to a route that validates them, its own route included, failed to compile with no hint at the cause
350.1 [x] Found by the BUGS 349 probe (.tmp/grunt/probe346/modes.ts): `canon/[id]`, which validates `params: { id: number }` and whose loader redirects to itself with params, and `vping/[id]` and `vpong/[id]`, two validating pages whose loaders redirect to each other, fail with TS7022 ('route' implicitly has type 'any'), TS7024, and TS2502 in the register, under every register tried: the AST register of 346, every route's type, and 349's routes that call `.input()`. Checking the redirect's params reads the target route's type, which holds the loader whose return is being inferred. An annotated loader return breaks the cycle: 0 errors. Requiring an exported validator was ruled out (flare-e4): a route-local const cannot be named from the register unless exported, and an inline literal never can. Fix: a documented rule, dx/types.md: a loader that redirects with params or search to a route that validates them annotates its return type, naming the TS7022, TS7024, and TS2502 symptom; the types app pins canon and the vping/vpong pair, annotated, with 0 errors. Open path, after the RC: a redirect typing that breaks the cycle at `redirect`'s params slot.
351 [x] On the Mac, every hydrate-harness WebKit case timed out after 60 s, 79 a run, so "expected WebKit failures" counted a harness that never ran WebKit
351.1 [x] Found by flare-e4's review of the Mac gate at 2cf8a1bf: 83 harness failures, all "Test timed out in 60000ms", 79 under `webkit` and 4 under `webkit visitor`, against a baseline of 69 names that only tracked them. Cause: the Mac is macOS 14.6, and playwright-core 1.64.1's browsers.json pins WebKit 2370 with `revisionOverrides: { "mac14-arm64": "2251" }`, so Playwright launches the frozen `webkit_mac14_arm64_special-2251`, whose protocol it no longer speaks. A probe of the harness's own `webkit.launch()` launched it in 191 ms, then `newContext()` never resolved, over SSH and as a gui-domain LaunchAgent alike; `DEBUG=pw:protocol` shows `Unknown setting: PushAPIEnabled` and `'Playwright.closePage' was not found`. Fix: scripts/browser-server.ts `frozenWebkit` reads the WebKit executable Playwright resolves and calls a `webkit…_special-N` build frozen; with the installed engines and that build, the integration project's setup file, tests/integration/frozen-webkit.setup.ts, skips each case under a `webkit` suite with the reason "BUGS 351: Playwright's frozen WebKit for macOS 14 cannot speak its protocol", so a Mac report counts them skipped, about 80 minutes a run no longer spent. The OS alone never decides, so Linux WebKit, the pinned container's and the local launcher's, still runs. The Mac WebKit baseline file is retired: a Mac report states the skip count, and any other WebKit failure is a real failure. WebKit coverage is Linux WebKit (CI and the local launcher) plus real Safari through `bun run verify:safari`. The route back to Mac harness WebKit is the user's: macOS 15 or later, where Playwright runs its current WebKit. Red first: the Mac run's 79 timeouts; browser-server.test `frozen webkit` (a `_special-2251` path, POSIX and Windows, is frozen; Linux's webkit-2370, the local launcher's run.sh, and a path that merely holds `_special-` are not).
352 [x] A client boot whose module import failed was an unhandled rejection: the page stayed unhydrated and silent, and dev-hmr failed in Firefox on 7 of 12 local runs and 4 to 5 of 24 on the Mac
352.1 [x] Found by the BUGS 349 Mac gate: dev-hmr.test `route, layout, and new server function` failed on bun-dev-firefox with `pageerror: error loading dynamically imported module`, and failed as often at 3061aebf, before 349. A probe (.tmp/grunt/hmrprobe) logging Vite's socket frames, documents, and page errors found two unhandled imports. Boot: `createClient` ran `void bootClient(...)`, so a route module import that rejected in `loadChain` was unhandled, stack `at bootClient (create-client.ts:49) | at async*createClient/< (create-client.ts:32) | at (src/client.tsx:3)` for `root.root-layout.tsx?t=…` and `fns/echo.ts`. Entry: the document's client script was `<script type="module">import("<entry>")</script>` with no handler; with the script rewritten to log first, 16 of 16 page errors in 16 runs came from it (stacks at the inline script, `echo:1`), framework modules of the entry's static graph. Every one fell after the page's own `beforeunload` and before `pagehide`: a reload cut the imports short. In dev an edit can send two reloads (a root layout's `js-update`, then its `full-reload` 18 ms later; and @tailwindcss/vite's bare `full-reload` when codegen rewrites src/_gen/server-fns.gen.ts), and Firefox rejects the imports and fetches the first reload cut short. In production, a cached or back-forward document whose entry or route chunk a deploy removed stayed unhydrated with no recovery, where a navigation's chunk failure loads the document (bits/06 `chunk import`). Fix: one policy for both, from shared/protocol/chunk-reload.ts (the key, window, and five messages client/navigation/reload.ts now reads too). `bootSafely` wraps the boot, and the entry script's inline handler, built from those constants in shared/protocol/entry-import.ts, wraps the entry import; the text lives in shared, not server/render, since it is browser code (tests/structure/logger.test reads `console.` under src/server), and outside the protocol barrel, so the client bundle never carries the inline escape. A failure after the page's own `beforeunload`, whose listener lives only while the import is pending, is skipped: nothing is loaded, logged, or stored. A chunk message is one document load of `location.href` under the `flare_chunk_reload` guard, with the same `{ at, href }` record. Any other failure, or a load the guard holds back or storage refuses, logs `[flare] the client failed to boot`; a boot failure also goes to the dev overlay in dev, and the app's callbacks never hear the client is ready. A non-chunk throw later in the boot, such as a mount that throws while hydrating, was an unhandled rejection too and now takes the same log and overlay. Accepted edge: a `beforeunload` with no navigation after it, a cancelled prompt or a download, inside the pending import, together with a real chunk failure in that same window, skips that one boot; the next boot recovers by the normal path. installCall moved to client/boot/install-call.ts (create-client.ts at its 300-line limit), and server-fn-boot.test.ts became its sibling install-call.test.ts. The minimal app's echo page handles its call's rejection, since Firefox rejects a fetch a reload cuts short (one probe run in 16). dev-hmr waits out the reload a new server function causes: the page's next `load` is armed before the write and awaited after Tailwind's `full-reload` frame, which a probe timed at codegen's rewrite (+57 to 92 ms after the write, 24 of 24 runs), not the new file's own update. createClient's budget is 57026 (+131: `bootSafely` 499 raw, `BOOT_FAILED` 54 raw). Red first: boot-failure.test (no `bootSafely`, and an unhandled rejection from createClient against e66d8822); client-script.test against HEAD's bare `import()`, 6 of 7 red with unhandled rejections; dev-hmr at the rates above. Green: dev-hmr node-dev-firefox and bun-dev-firefox at --repeat-each 24, 48 of 48 twice.
353 [x] A bare `createPathSegment(id)` had no `_type`, in its type or at runtime, while every other builder's export names its kind
353.1 [x] Found by plan 25's types.md review (T3): `segment.ts` set `_type: "segment"` only on the record `.cache()` returns, so a segment exported bare, one with no `cdn` default, was typed without it in the generated register (`typeof import(...).route`) and reached the router only through `load-chain.ts`'s fallback to the entry kind. types.md promises the export's `_type` is `"segment"` with no qualifier, and `layout.ts`, `page.ts`, and `root-layout.ts` set it on the builder. Fix: `SegmentBuilder` has `readonly _type: "segment"` and `createPathSegment` returns it. Red first: segment.test `bare kind` (`undefined` against `"segment"`, and TS2339 on the type).
360 [x] Two navigate harness cases waited a fixed time for what they assert, so a slow runner failed them
360.1 [x] Found by CI 39085432960 on e66d8822, e2e-mac 1/3 (both cases from 606fb385, their first CI run): progress-client `progress creep and hold` held the data request a fixed 2 s, and chromium recorded 58 of the 62 creep values and firefox 26, since the runner's timers fired late; transition-client `view transitions` slept a fixed 400 ms per step for a 50 ms transition to be recorded on `finished`, and chromium's second step recorded no start. A local CPU throttle (CDP, 25x) did not reproduce either: the runner's late timers are the cause, and the CI log is the red (.tmp/grunt/ci-39085432960/failed.log). Fix: the data request answers once the creep has reached its 0.994 cap, so every step is seen however late the timers fire; each transition step waits until every start it made has finished and been recorded (`__flareOpen`), not a fixed time. Local: 6 of 6 under Node and Bun.
361 [x] The docs app's lists over loaded data rebuilt every row on a refetch, and Solid's dev warning failed docs-smoke
361.1 [x] Found by CI 39085432960 on e66d8822, e2e-linux 2/3: docs-smoke `click through` on bun-dev-webkit saw Solid's `[UNSTABLE_LIST_IDENTITY] list "<For>" recreated 2 of 2 rows` warning from `<Toc>`: a refetched doc's sections are fresh objects equal field for field, so an identity-keyed `<For>` threw away each row's DOM. Fix: the docs lists over loader and query data key by a stable field (`keyed`): doc-parts.tsx's sections by `id` (the table of contents and the sections), changelog releases by `version`, search hits by `slug`. Local: docs-smoke, docs-navigation, docs-search, and docs-transition on bun-dev-webkit, node-dev-chromium, node-prod-firefox, node-prod-webkit, workers-prod-chromium, and node-prod-floor, 361 passed (one run also lost `back keeps scroll` on bun-dev-webkit to a Vite page reload from another session's edit of route-builder/chain.ts in the shared tree; 3 of 3 alone).
362 [x] A page a client navigation mounted ran its effects before history moved, so an effect reading the URL saw the page it left
362.1 [x] Found by the dashboard app (plan 26, B146): its sign-in redirect read `window.location` in an effect of the 401 render, and the navigation log showed `framenavigated /app/settings/billing` 3 ms before the redirect, which carried the old URL. B106 has history move with the DOM commit, but `navigate.ts` committed and flushed inside the update, so the new page's effects ran, and pushed only after `await shown`, a microtask or more later; an overlay (`overlay.ts` `showOverlay`) flushed before `writeEntry` in the same way. An analytics page view in an effect counted the previous page. Fix: `store.commit(next, shown)` calls `shown` from the store's own effect as that snapshot goes on screen, which runs before the effects of the components the commit mounts; the navigator writes history there (`steps.ts` `moveHistory`, `land` skips a written step), and the overlay writes its entry there. A commit Solid holds still writes nothing until it shows, and a superseded one never writes (B106). Red first: navigate-slots.test `effects see the url` read `["/"]` and reads `["/watch"]`; navigate-transition.test `view transition` now pins the push inside the update (`pushedInUpdate`), and overlay.test `overlay entry` drives the write through the store's `shown`. Node 417 client unit cases, Bun 226 navigation and router cases, integration 577, and docs-navigation, docs-resilience, docs-hash, docs-transition, navigation, and dashboard-auth on six projects, 330 passed.
